### §7.3 — GL-substrate stream (later V1.x; 3 items)

*Later-V1.x GL-substrate work decomposed out of the V1.0 §2.4 manual-entry track per [ADR-033](DECISIONS.md#adr-033) — GL-engine edits (net-new `fn_gl_entries` contra branches) that are a distinct work-class from the manual-entry INSERT-path slices. Milestone target (M4-GL-adjacent later V1.x) confirmed by F/CTO 2026-07-26; the exact product-V1.x label is pending roadmap sequencing with Architect. Promotes to Linear at milestone-rotation when the GL-substrate milestone becomes current + next. **The manual-sale writer** — G3's named blocker, and the capability §2.5.1's capital-gains columns and wash-sale treatment wait on (V1.4 sitting R1 / R2) — **has no assigned milestone**; the booking lives at §7.32 item 1 and this stream's scoping owes it a home.*

**G1. Manual in-kind transfer entry (`group_type='transfer_in_kind'` + net-new in-kind clearing contra).** [V1-SHIP-BLOCK]
- **Source.** [PRD §2.4.3.b](docs/PRD/index.html#story-2-4-3) (AcctSetup non-cash event entry — transfer-in-kind subtype) + [ADR-033 Decision 4](DECISIONS.md#adr-033) (transfer-in-kind deferral to its own SELF issue).
- **AC.** In-kind transfer entry modeled as journal `group_type='transfer_in_kind'` (`033`) over two `standard` security legs (out-leg + in-leg): (1) capture source account + destination account + position + cost-basis carry-over preserving original basis; (2) two `standard` legs — P1 cash branch skips (no cash), P2 emits a `trade_position` leg on each side; (3) **author the net-new in-kind clearing contra** in `fn_gl_entries` so the two legs offset (in-kind legs currently have no clearing contra → they park in Suspense, unlike cash transfers which get P3's Journal-Clearing contra via the `Transfer + journal_id` branch); (4) cost-basis carry-over + multi-lot reconcile; (5) per-security `Σ(quantity)=0` conservation enforced at close by `fn_journal_close_balance` (`037`); (6) **Sec-joint-review-mandatory** (GL/money-flow edit — a `fn_gl_entries` contra-branch change); (7) QA two-tenant pgTAP battery on the new contra branch + the conservation law.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: [ADR-033](DECISIONS.md#adr-033) Decision 4 + GL machinery (`033` `journal.group_type` + `035`/`037` `fn_gl_entries` + `037` `fn_journal_close_balance` + `019` holdings). **Blocked-by:** the net-new in-kind clearing contra must be authored (net-new `fn_gl_entries` work; not present in the current engine). Evaluate for a Decision-3 fence at its migration (per ADR-033 Consequences). Sec joint-review gate. Downstream: completes the §2.4.3.b transfer-in-kind capability deferred from SELF-203.

**G2. Per-lot stock-split precision (lot-linkage substrate + per-lot `fn_create_stock_split`).** [refinement]
- **Source.** [ADR-033 Decision 2](DECISIONS.md#adr-033) + SELF-203 OWD-1. F/CTO chose per-lot (2026-07-26); the Architect feasibility pass established per-lot as a **substrate project, not a `039` line item**, so SELF-203 shipped POSITION-LEVEL (v1.114) and per-lot is deferred here.
- **AC.** Per-lot stock-split recording — one book-neutral `corp_action` row per open buy lot (vs SELF-203's position-level single row): (1) a lot-linkage self-FK on `account_trans` (e.g. `lot_parent_trans_id`) — a **NEW Decision-3 cross-tenant FK-bypass instance** (matched-tenant + matched-security fence, Sec-joint-review-mandatory) on the immutable ledger; (2) an authoritative DB open-lot-as-of read (`fn_open_lots_as_of`) — **depends on `lot_match` (036) matching inference being complete/authoritative** (currently Backend/worker-inferred, non-authoritative at write time — a DB open-lot read miscomputes until matching has run); (3) per-lot `fn_create_stock_split` computing per-lot deltas; (4) reverse-and-replace the SELF-203 position-level rows to per-lot (bounded — splits are rare). Sec-joint-review-mandatory.
- **Dependencies.** **Blocked-by:** authoritative lot-matching substrate (`lot_match` completeness — not built) + the lot-linkage FK. Architect feasibility memo: `temp/039-stock-split-design.md`. Note: per-lot tax-character precision is itself a deferred tax-layer refinement (`037` Option A), so the book GL loses nothing under position-level today. Downstream: upgrades SELF-203 position-level splits to per-lot precision.

**G3. Securities-edit surface (`fn_reverse_and_replace_trans` RPC + field-widened edit schema).** [V1-SHIP-BLOCK]
- **Source.** F/CTO ruling 2026-08-26 (securities-edit sitting; the A+C-deferred package — refuse security-row edits now at the write path, stage the real surface here) + the joint options brief's Option C mechanism sketch at [`docs/records/self340-securities-edit-options.md`](docs/records/self340-securities-edit-options.md) + [ADR-064](DECISIONS.md#adr-064) (securities-row edits refused at the write path; edit surface staged, not built). The interim refusal ships with the Edit-flow fix PR; this entry is the staged half whose existence makes the refusal window **bounded** rather than permanent — the ratified acceptance was *"uncorrectable for the window, correctable when this lands,"* which holds only while this entry stays on the V1 roadmap. *(PM label note: `[V1-SHIP-BLOCK]` is PM-assigned on that window-bounded reasoning — if this slips past V1.final, the ratified wording reverts to "permanent." F/CTO strikes to `[refinement]` only if the permanent reading is acceptable.)*
- **AC.** (1) Edit schema widened with `quantity` + `cost_basis`; **`security_id` stays non-editable** (changing the instrument is a different trade, not an edit). (2) The whole reverse-and-replace moves into a DB function `pfin.fn_reverse_and_replace_trans(...)`, `SECURITY INVOKER`, `set search_path = ''`, so the `{reversal, corrected}` pair **and** the annotation carry commit as one transaction (closes the swallowed-annotation two-statement defect the sitting surfaced). (3) **Reversal carries `cost_basis` negated** — else the P2/P8 asymmetry silently books the position's book value to Suspense (the sitting's cost_basis-blind-reversal finding, promoted to an AC, not lore). (4) **Matched-sell refusal**: a row with `lot_match` children refuses edit (orphaning hazard, same class as the split-parent guard) — this entry narrows the interim refusal; it does not retire it. (5) **Reversal date semantics decided by F/CTO ratify at spec time** — back-date (the window's NAV/allocation/tax history is restated as though the error never happened) vs correction-date (the window's history keeps the wrong figures; only forward reads are repaired). Ratify-level, not implementation; the current code back-dates silently and that choice must not be inherited. (6) **Sec-joint-review-mandatory** — money-flow RPC + a Lock 14 mass-assignment surface widening (`.strict()` + the numeric battery both extend). (7) QA battery legs per fact-kind (`standard`+security / `acct_setup`+security / `corp_action`) + a no-`suspense`-row leg on an edited trade. (8) On landing, the interim write-path refusal lifts for the covered classes and the Edit affordance re-renders on security rows.
- **Dependencies.** **Blocked-by:** M4-GL `lot_match` activation + the manual-sale writer (both named at the sitting; a sell path must exist before matched-sell edits are reachable). Upstream: the interim refusal guard (ships with the Edit-flow fix PR). Downstream: closes the V1 no-correction window ratified at the sitting; the PRD §2.4.3 boundary fence lifts with it.

### §7.4 — V1.6 reconciliation / statement tie-out stream (1 item)

*Read-only reconciliation work re-homed from the retired V1.0 §2.4.3 per-transaction reconcile mechanism per [ADR-035](DECISIONS.md#adr-035). Distinct work-class from §7.3 GL-engine edits: this is a read-only DETECTOR over existing SECURITY INVOKER helpers + service_role-populated checkpoints — it authors no `fn_gl_entries` contra branch and writes nothing to the immutable ledger. Homed in the dedicated **V1.6 — Reconciliation / statement tie-out** milestone (F/CTO-approved 2026-07-27; Platform/Cross-cutting project). The comparand substrate it reads is already shipped (see R1 Dependencies), so this is a standalone read-only feature over landed objects — not a pending substrate wave. **SELF-205 retains its ID, re-scoped to this entry.***

**R1. Period statement-vs-GL reconciliation control tie-out (read-only detector).**
- **Source.** [ADR-035](DECISIONS.md#adr-035) (supersedes SELF-205's per-transaction `reconciled_flag` / B1 / B2 mechanism; successor = statement control tie-out) + [PRD §2.4.3](docs/PRD/index.html#story-2-4-3) verbatim reconciliation prose.
- **AC.** Read-only DETECTOR, no auto-action: per reporting-period × account, compare the **GL-derived** as-of balance (cash, via `pfin.fn_compute_nav` cash-leg) and holdings (via `pfin.fn_holdings_as_of`) against the **statement-snapshot** comparands (`pfin.account_balance_checkpoint` cash snapshot + `pfin.holdings_checkpoint` per-asset snapshot + `pfin.reconciliation_event.statement_balance` / `.statement_quantity`); surface each mismatch (balance and per-position quantity) for the user to investigate. **No `reconciled_flag` / `reconciled_at` column, no B1 per-transaction trigger, no B2 bulk "reconcile mode"** — the reconciled verdict is an append-only `reconciliation_event` / `reconciliation_event_trans` row (`005`), not mutable row-state on the immutable `account_trans` ledger. Precise terminology (binding per ADR-035): this is a **statement-vs-GL account reconciliation control tie-out at period boundaries** — NOT a bare internal "trial balance check" (near-vacuous, since the double-entry GL already balances by construction at close via `fn_journal_close_balance` `037`). **Auto-reconcile on balance-match (B3) stays V2+** (per §5 auto-reconcile deferral) — the detector flags; it never auto-clears. Detection-only, consistent with the SELF-204 sync-dedup gate ([ADR-034](DECISIONS.md#adr-034)); SELF-204 DETECTS / SELF-205 INTERPRETS boundary preserved.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: the comparand substrate — **already shipped** under Platform/Cross-cutting: `pfin.fn_compute_nav` / `pfin.fn_holdings_as_of` (`019` / SELF-277, SECURITY INVOKER, `set search_path=''`, Lock 11 read-composition), `pfin.account_balance_checkpoint` (`018` / SELF-276), `pfin.holdings_checkpoint` + `pfin.reconciliation_event.statement_balance` / `.statement_quantity` (`005` / SELF-188). The tie-out is therefore a standalone read-only feature over landed objects, not a pending substrate wave. **Build-time caveat:** verify whether the `005`-deferred `holdings_checkpoint` fan-out INSERT writer (the per-asset checkpoint populator) is fully covered by `018` or still outstanding — the holdings side of the tie-out needs populated checkpoints to compare against; if the writer is a gap, it carries its own migration + QA battery + Sec joint-review. **Sec joint-review + QA two-tenant pgTAP battery travel with this build** (read over the reconciliation substrate; not waived per ADR-035 Consequences). Downstream: closes the §2.4.3 external-agreement reconciliation commitment. **Re-scopes SELF-205** (retains ID; re-milestoned **V1.6 — Reconciliation / statement tie-out**; AC rewritten to this read-only-detector shape per ADR-035).

### §7.5 — Onboarding / provisioning (V1 cross-cutting; 1 item)

*Promoted into V1 per F/CTO ruling 2026-07-28 (**V1 signup is OPEN**, not gated to the F/CTO). Default taxonomy provisioning (**both `asset` + `cashflow` domains**) is a V1 deliverable so any open-signup user reaches a populated classify picker (and populated §2.4.3 manual-entry Sub-Cat dropdowns); without it the SELF-200 held-symbol classifier dead-ends for every user but the F/CTO (whose taxonomy is bootstrap-seeded). This **resolves the latent multi-user taxonomy gap in V1** — it is no longer a V2 prerequisite (see §5.7 note). Provisioning **mechanism** is Architect-owned, next cycle.*

**O1. Default taxonomy provisioning on signup (asset + cash-flow domains).** [V1-SHIP-BLOCK]
- **Source.** F/CTO ruling 2026-07-28 (open V1 signup + promote default-asset-taxonomy seeding into V1); surfaced by the SELF-200 UX-pass empty-picker gap; AC anchor = [PRD §2.2.1](docs/PRD/index.html#story-2-2-1) / [ADR-004](DECISIONS.md#adr-004) Decision C canonical asset taxonomy.
- **AC.** Every user is provisioned the canonical default `user_taxonomy` for **both domains — `asset` (§2.2.1) and `cashflow` (§2.3.1)** — on signup / first access in a **single provisioning pass** (don't provision twice). Asset domain = the 6-Cat × ~35-Sub-Cat grammar per PRD §2.2.1 / ADR-004 Decision C (Cash / Bonds / Equity / Alternatives / Liabilities / Real Estate top-level Cats + their ~35 Sub-Cats), seeding `tax_relevant=false / tax_character=NULL` on the asset side (matches the existing seed's V1-dormant asset tax-character decision); cash-flow domain = the §2.3.1 Income / Expenses / OtherCF / AcctSetup grammar per ADR-004 Decision C. **Demonstrable AC anchor stays the asset domain** the SELF-200 classify picker needs (the surface under test); the cash-flow domain is provisioned in the same pass so the §2.4.3 manual-entry Sub-Cat dropdowns are populated too. Both domains' canonical content currently lives **only** in the gitignored dev-only `seed.sql` (asset ← `pfin.asset_cat`; cash-flow ← `pfin.trans_cat`); it needs a **canonical, non-gitignored home** to provision from. **Open design point — Architect-owned, next cycle (NOT decided here):** the provisioning mechanism must respect the ratified *"no on-signup DEFINER trigger"* posture (024 `user_settings` D3 precedent). Candidates for the Architect to weigh: (a) **lazy app-upsert** on first taxonomy access (parallels `user_settings.mfa_policy` provisioning), or (b) a **migration-seeded canonical default-taxonomy template** + a controlled per-user provision step — either mechanism provisions **both domains in one pass.**
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: a canonical (non-gitignored) source for the default asset **+ cash-flow** taxonomy (both domains; migration-embedded template or equivalent — the content exists in `seed.sql` today but not in a provisionable home); `009_user_taxonomy` (target table — its V1-WRITE-DORMANT `authenticated` SELECT-only grant + the "no on-signup DEFINER trigger" 024 posture constrain the mechanism). **Architect design pass (mechanism selection) is the immediate next step** (post-SELF-200 merge, per team-lead scheduling). Sec joint-review if the chosen mechanism touches a privileged provisioning path (any DEFINER or service_role seed step). Downstream: unblocks open-signup users at the SELF-200 classify surface; resolves the §5.7 latent multi-user taxonomy gap.

### §7.6 — Sec joint-review follow-ups (SELF-214 onward)

*⚠ **This subsection is a STREAM, not a closed filing** — read the title that way, and append accordingly. It **began** as the follow-up filing from the SELF-214 (`nav_daily`) Sec joint-review, 2026-08-02, staged per [ADR-017](DECISIONS.md#adr-017) Decision 2 (§7 is the repo-versioned home for V1 work not currently in Linear). **S1–S10 are that original filing.** **S11 onward arrive from later Sec joint-reviews** — principally the ADR-048 / ADR-049 CPI-U stream — and are **not** SELF-214 follow-ups. ⚠ **The SELF-214 framing below governs S1–S10 only and must not be read across the whole section**; that scoping is the point of the title and the reason a contents-descriptive rename was rejected. The working specs live in `temp/` (gitignored — which is why these entries exist).*

*Accreting section — carries no item count by design (in an accreting set a header count is an uncheckable claim; the closed/accreting split per section is in the §7 index table above). Do not add one.*

*✅ **The title question this note opened is CLOSED. F/CTO ruled Option C on 2026-08-11**: retitle to name the section as a stream — `Sec joint-review follow-ups (SELF-214 onward)` — rather than rename it to describe its contents (Option A) or split it into closed and accreting halves (Option B). **Why C:** it encodes the thing that actually broke — a closed-set header on an accreting set — at the lowest cost, it **preserves the SELF-214 provenance** that S1–S10's framing notes depend on, and it is **reversible**: if §7.6 later needs the Option-B split, C has not made that harder. **A was rejected because a contents-descriptive title carries the same decay mode just removed, one level up** — contents drift, and the header would go stale again. **B was judged more correct in the abstract but not worth its cost**: the live cross-subsection references (S24↔S6, S21↔S20, S16↔S6) would then span subsections, a real price paid to fix a labelling problem. **Recorded rather than deleted** so a reader sees the question was answered, not abandoned — the note named a destination for the answer, and this is the delivery.*

*Framing notes — ⚠ **scoped deliberately, since the section outgrew SELF-214: (1) is section-wide; (2), (3) and (4) govern the SELF-214 filing (S1–S10) ONLY.** (The count on this list was dropped for the same reason as the header's: it read "Two" against three notes, and now four.) **(1) The Linear free-issue limit is a BOOKING constraint, not a CAPTURE constraint.** `save_issue` currently fails workspace-wide ("You've exceeded the free issue limit"), and resolving it is an unresolved F/CTO cost decision — but §7 is the ratified home for exactly this condition and needs no Linear seat, so **nothing below is blocked on that decision.** Promote at the first milestone-rotation after the limit lifts. **No live Linear IDs are cited** — a merged PR referencing one auto-closes it. **(2) S7 was in the original four follow-ups and is absent from Sec's ranked six; its omission is an artifact of ranking scope, NOT a de-prioritization** — [ADR-040](DECISIONS.md#adr-040) canonically records it as "EXPLICITLY DEFERRED, not satisfied, not waived" and states a tracked follow-up is required. It is retained below on that basis. **(3) Cross-reference into §7.7:** the close-gate joint review (§7.7, 2026-08-03) filed **F2**, a live `nav_daily` tenant-enumeration defect that **feeds S7's AC** — read the two together; S7's forensic-gap argument narrows once F2 lands. **(4) Sec's priority ranking covers S1–S6** — it was taken at the SELF-214 review and ranks that filing only. ⚠ **It says nothing about S11 onward, which were not yet written**; absence from it is not a de-prioritization of anything outside S1–S10 (the same reasoning note (2) applies to S7 within the filing).*

**S1. pgTAP assertion that `authenticated` holds NO table-level SELECT on `pfin.plaid_items` / `pfin.linked_source` (converts ADR-023 C3 into an enforced invariant).** [highest value per Sec]
- **Source.** SELF-214 Sec joint-review C3 ruling. Correcting the false REVOKE prose (S2/S3) **withdraws** a false promise but does **not** discharge [ADR-023](DECISIONS.md#adr-023) C3 going forward — it would leave the actual control as "review discipline," the procedural-over-structural trade Sec ruled against earlier in this same review. Sec: *"This is the item worth booking, more than the prose fix."*
- **AC.** A pgTAP assertion in the two-tenant battery that the `authenticated` grant on `pfin.plaid_items` and `pfin.linked_source` **remains column-scoped**, and that the credential-handle columns (`access_token_secret_id`, `credential_secret_id`) stay **absent from the column list**. Fails in CI the instant anyone adds a broad table-level grant — precisely what the comment falsely promised the `REVOKE` would do. Cheap, non-vacuous, and it makes the widening structurally detectable instead of review-dependent. Pairs with the ADR-023 C6 standing condition (every `pfin` relation is internet-facing once granted; the §4.5 two-tenant battery is exposure-gating).
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: the existing two-tenant pgTAP battery; `007` / `015` grants as the asserted baseline (verified present + column-scoped, 2026-08-02). Owner: **QA**. Sec advisory on assertion wording. Downstream: converts C3 from an assertion into an enforced invariant; **is the structural half of S2/S3, which are only the prose half.**

**S2. Fold the ADR-023 C3 prose correction into [ADR-023](DECISIONS.md#adr-023) (disposition (c)).** [refinement]
- **Source.** SELF-214 Sec joint-review; `temp/followup-column-revoke-false-rationale.md`. `008:104–105` states *"Belt-and-suspenders re-assertion so no later broad grant can silently re-expose the handle"* — written as the discharge of Sec condition **C3**, so a Sec-load-bearing condition is documented as held by a mechanism that does not hold it. Postgres has no negative grants: a column `REVOKE` neither blocks a later table-level `GRANT` nor claws one back (both directions measured 2026-08-02 in a rolled-back transaction). **Ownership verified, not assumed:** `008`'s block is headed *"SEC CONDITIONS (Option A ratify, SELF-196)"* and cites *"Sec C6 (ADR-023)"*, so **ADR-023 owns C1–C6 including C3.** **"C3" is overloaded in this codebase** — [ADR-029](DECISIONS.md#adr-029) has a C3 (the aal2 backstop obligation, which `054` cites) and there is an admission-worker C3 — so this entry names **ADR-023 C3** unambiguously; a correction filed against the wrong C3 would be its own drift.
- **AC.** Record the correction in **ADR-023**, where a future reader of C3 actually looks. **Disposition (c); (b) is dead on structural grounds, not merely "confusing"** — the false claims live in `--` line comments inside `.sql` files, which are **not database objects**, so no follow-up migration can alter them, and a `comment on column` would add catalog text the engineer about to add a grant never reads. **(b) cannot reach the defect at all.** Note in the correction that C3's own wording already names `007`'s column-scoped SELECT as the mechanism with `008` merely *"re-asserting"* — so C3's substance was never the REVOKE.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: none (`008` is merged; out of scope for the SELF-214 PR). Owner: **Architect** (`DECISIONS.md`). Sec ruled; F/CTO ratifies. Not a one-way door (prose). Downstream: **S1 is what actually discharges C3 forward** — this entry only withdraws the false promise.

**S3. INVERT the `007` / `015` column-`REVOKE` comments from reassurance to warning.** [refinement]
- **Source.** SELF-214 Sec joint-review, Sec's direct verification of both files. **There is NO live exposure — the entry says so plainly so nobody triages this as an incident.** `007` grants `select (item_id, users_id, plaid_item_id, …) on pfin.plaid_items to authenticated` with `access_token_secret_id` **not in the list**; `015` has the same shape on `linked_source` with `credential_secret_id` **not in the list**; and Sec checked the baseline rather than assuming — **no table-level grant to `authenticated` exists on either table** (independently re-verified 2026-08-02). The handles are genuinely unreachable today. **The real protection is the omission from the column list on the lines immediately above — not the REVOKE.** **Severity, per Sec's ruling:** in `054` this was a *new* no-op line with a false justification — a false fence standing alone. In `007`/`015`/`008` it is a **redundant line with a misleading comment, adjacent to a fence that actually works**: worse context (Vault credential handles), smaller defect. **Sec ruled it a NOTE** — follow-up, not urgent, correctly out of scope for this PR.
- **AC.** **Invert the comment; do not merely correct it.** The hazard Sec identified is not that the prose is inaccurate — it is that *"ensure the secret handle is not reachable via a broad grant"* invites a future engineer to conclude **it is therefore safe to add a broad grant**, because the REVOKE will catch it. It will not. **The comment could license the exact action it appears to defend against**, on a Vault-credential surface. Sec's suggested replacement, which is the AC: `-- The handle's protection is its OMISSION from the column list above. A table-level GRANT on this table WOULD expose it — column REVOKE cannot subtract from a table-level grant (Postgres has no negative grants). Any table-level grant here is Sec joint-review-mandatory.` Same treatment as `nav_value` in `054`: drop the executable no-op, keep the intent as a **warning rather than a reassurance**. **An entry that only said "correct the comment" would lose the point.** **SCOPING GUARD — this constrains the deliverable; carry it into any wider sweep this seeds, or the sweep will do damage.** Correct **forward-looking claims** (assertions about the future, e.g. *"no later broad grant can silently re-expose the handle"*): these were **never true** and decay into active hazards. **Leave point-in-time statements alone** (a ledger count at authorship, e.g. `008:50`'s *"ledger stays at 2 (RT-22 + RT-26)"* — **stale but NOT wrong**, accurate when `008` landed, before the 2→3 flip at SELF-212): these are the historical record of a merged migration and are **not defects**. Without this distinction a well-meaning sweep starts rewriting merged migrations' history and we lose the ability to read what was known when — a real risk given how many stale-but-correct counts exist across `001`–`053`.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: none (both merged). Owner: **Architect** (`supabase/migrations/**`). Routing: Sec (both surfaces are Vault-credential handling). Downstream: the AC's scoping guard governs any wider comment sweep this seeds.

**S4. Pre-push hook fencing untracked / stale files under `supabase/` and `workers/`.** [refinement]
- **Source.** SELF-214 Sec joint-review; `temp/self214-devops-followup-prepush-untracked-fence.md`. **CI structurally cannot catch this class:** an untracked file is not in the pushed tree, so a green check certifies the tree CI was given, not the tree that was intended. No amount of added CI closes it — the fence must be local, because its input set is the working tree *including what git has not been told about*. **Three instances in one review**, which makes it a class, not a coincidence: `055_pfin_etl_role.sql` fully untracked (`??`) while CI ran fully green — a merge would have shipped a deployment runbook linking to a migration that did not exist, and an ETL with no database login identity; the paired QA RLS battery staged-then-modified (`AM`) — a commit would have shipped an older battery than QA verified; the deployment runbook `MM`. Related standing memory: `feedback_verify_paired_artifacts_before_push`.
- **AC.** A pre-push hook (Husky, alongside the existing locked lint + test + type-check + svelte-check + ruff + hadolint set) failing closed on offending paths from `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all`. Per DevOps decision rules a new fence is a present-options decision and needs a catch criterion **and** a test proving it catches. **Design questions to settle at implementation (record; do not pre-decide):** (i) **must `AM`/`MM` fail as well as `??`** — the QA-battery case was `AM`, so untracked-only would have caught one of three; this is the question that decides coverage; (ii) scope — `supabase/` + `workers/` only, or whole-tree with an ignore list; (iii) an escape hatch for deliberate scratch files that does not make the fence trivially bypassable; (iv) a **paired test proving the fence fails on a real violation** — a fence whose pass condition can be satisfied without the discipline holding is theater.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: **none — there is no Husky pre-push set.** This line previously named one; `git ls-tree -r main .husky/` returns exactly one entry, `pre-commit`, so this item CREATES the pre-push hook rather than extending an existing one. ⚠ **Two adjacent facts to establish at implementation rather than inherit from this entry, both bearing on its size:** (i) the AC's parenthetical describes the pre-commit set as *"the existing locked lint + test + type-check + svelte-check + ruff + hadolint set"*; as merged, only **ruff** and **hadolint** are wired — `svelte-check` and `eslint` appear in that file **only inside comments** deferring them until SvelteKit scaffolds, and lint/test/type-check are absent. **The AC is left unedited deliberately, because correcting it is a re-scope rather than a dependency fix** — but do not size this item against a set that is largely commentary. (ii) Husky was **not installed** in the checkout until `core.hooksPath` was armed (2026-08-11), so the pre-commit set has no run history to extend; treat *"the hook fires at all"* as something this item verifies, not assumes. Owner: **DevOps**. Intended project Platform / Cross-cutting; intended labels `role:devops` + `surface:ci`, **neither of which exists in the workspace yet**. Non-blocking for the SELF-214 PR. Search-before-create done, no duplicate. Downstream: closes a merge-integrity class CI cannot reach; **S8 is its procedural complement, not a substitute.**

**S5. Dedicated `pfin_provider_sync` login role for `workers/provider-sync` ([ADR-019](DECISIONS.md#adr-019) Condition C2).** [PHASE-7 DEPLOY GATE]
- **Source.** [ADR-041](DECISIONS.md#adr-041) sub-decisions verbatim. SELF-214 gave the ETL a dedicated `pfin_etl` NOINHERIT login role (option (B) ratified over sharing `authenticator`). Two sub-decisions follow: **(i) naming convention `pfin_<workers/ subdirectory>`, effective now** — `workers/provider-sync` → **`pfin_provider_sync`**, which **supersedes the `pfin_worker` name** in ADR-019 C2 (a generic name on the second member of a growing family is how naming drifts; the convention is mechanical and needs no future adjudication). C2's *substance* — a dedicated NOINHERIT role, Sec-joint-review-mandatory on adoption — is **unchanged**; only the name is pinned, and the rename rides C2's existing Sec gate. **(ii) C2 is PROMOTED from "V1.x hardening" to a Phase-7 deploy gate** — an **Architect call on Sec's recommendation**: B8's argument applies **more** strongly to provider-sync than to the ETL it was won for, since provider-sync holds `PLAID_SECRET`, decrypts SD-03 access tokens, and writes the tenant-scoped `account`/`account_trans` surface — the higher-value target, and the one still sharing `authenticator` with PostgREST. Leaving it on the shared credential after ratifying (B) for the lower-value container is not a defensible end-state.
- **AC.** `pfin_provider_sync` created as a NOINHERIT login role (member of `authenticated` + `service_role` only; drops `authenticator`'s inert `anon` membership) and set as provider-sync's `PFIN_DB_USER`, with its password provisioned in the **same Phase-7 deploy pass that switches on `pfin_etl`** — the cluster reaches a consistent role-graph in one operation rather than carrying a half-applied convention across releases. `pfin_etl` and `pfin_provider_sync` stay **deliberately separate, not converged** — collapsing them reintroduces the shared-credential blast radius and loss of independent revocation that option (A) was rejected for. **Sec joint-review-mandatory** (ADR-019 C2's condition, unchanged). Discharges the remainder of **[ADR-023](DECISIONS.md#adr-023) condition C1**: C1's rotation coupling narrowed from three consumers to two at ADR-041 (the ETL is out; PostgREST + provider-sync still bound), and **C1 is fully discharged only when `pfin_provider_sync` replaces `authenticator` in provider-sync's `PFIN_DB_USER`**.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: `pfin_etl` (`055`) as the shipped precedent + the Phase-7 deploy pass where the credential is first provisioned (per [ADR-021](DECISIONS.md#adr-021) greenfield, it does not exist until deploy). **Explicitly NOT SELF-214 scope.** **Blocked-by:** Phase 7 arrival. Owners: Architect (role migration), DevOps (`secrets-manifest.yml` + `.env.example` + runbook), Sec (joint-review gate).

**S6. V1-scope ruling on the incumbent FMP financial-statement tables (PM + Architect; F/CTO decides).** [scope question — not a defect]
- **Source.** SELF-214 Sec joint-review. Eight `pfin` tables are referenced by `workers/etl/src/pfin_back_etl/core.py` but created by **no V1 migration** — independently verified 2026-08-02 (`create table` grep across `supabase/migrations/`: zero hits for all eight). Incumbent code carried in at the [ADR-019](DECISIONS.md#adr-019) monorepo fold and never reconciled; the path is dead against V1 under any credential and fails loud (`UndefinedTable`), so there is **no security consequence and it blocks nothing**. **THIS IS NOT A GRANTS TASK** — Sec and Backend both initially scoped it as one, and **you cannot `GRANT` on a table that does not exist**. It is a product-scope question, which is why it routes to PM rather than Architect. **PM finding — the eight are NOT homogeneous, and splitting them collapses most of the question:**
  - **Class A — already superseded in V1; the scope question is CLOSED and only dead code remains (2 tables).** `pfin.cpi` → superseded by **`pfin.cpi_u_index`** (`053`, SELF-230), whose own header says it reuses "the proven incumbent BLS CPI fetch logic." `pfin.asset_cat` → superseded by **`pfin.user_taxonomy`** (`009`/`010`) + **`pfin.user_asset_category`** (`022`); `010` explicitly restores the incumbent `asset_cat` `notes` content. For these two the V1 answer already exists — the residue is a **dead-code retirement in `core.py`**, not a product decision.
  - **Class B — the genuine open question (6 tables).** `equity_profile`, `reporting_period`, `income_statement`, `balance_sheet_statement`, `cash_flow_statement`, `earning` — FMP **company-fundamentals** data.
- **AC.** An F/CTO ruling on Class B, framed as **V1 / later-V1.x / V2+ / never**, recorded as an ADR. **PM position (to be argued, not assumed):** **none of the 32 locked [PRD §2](docs/PRD/index.html#sec-2) V1 stories requires company fundamentals** — V1 is net worth, allocation, cash flow, estimated taxes, and the monthly report; fundamentals serve *security analysis*, which sits on the advisor/fiduciary axis that [PRD §6.3](docs/PRD/index.html#sec-6-3) / [ADR-007](DECISIONS.md#adr-007) made a **permanent non-goal** for TLH recommendations. That is a strong prior for **V2+ or never**, but it is **not the same non-goal** and must not be waved through as one — fundamentals could serve a read-only display use with no advice attached. **This is an incumbent-exceeds-V1 decision and must NOT be auto-accepted via "selective adoption"** (memory `feedback_incumbent_exceeds_v1_review`): it gets a P-flag with A/B/C options + an ADR. Class A needs no ruling — only a retirement issue.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: [ADR-019](DECISIONS.md#adr-019) (the monorepo fold that carried the code in without reconciling it). Owners: **PM** (frames the V1/V2/never options + the PRD §2 trace), **Architect** (migration cost if any class lands in V1; `core.py` retirement shape), **F/CTO** (decides). No Sec gate (no live surface — the path is dead). Downstream: either 6 new tables enter a milestone, or `core.py`'s fundamentals paths are retired and the dead-reference class closes. **⚠ Downstream gate — S24(a) must be met or explicitly ruled unnecessary BEFORE this entry's implementation lands.** The dead calls retired here are the only thing keeping `update_table_all`'s missing exception isolation unreachable: removing the **last** of them arms a CPI-U reconciliation failure to abort `update_table_eod_price` (→ NAV → net worth). **Retiring only Class A does not arm it** — six Class B calls still sit between the CPI-U gate and `eod_price` — so the trigger is the last dead call leaving the sequence, i.e. **Class A retirement plus the Class B ruling, both implemented.** Added 2026-08-10 as the reciprocal half of S24; without it the arming commit's author has no reason to look.

**S7. Discharge [ADR-011](DECISIONS.md#adr-011) Decision 1 clause (d) for the W-1 NAV worker (audit-log deferral).**
- **Source.** [ADR-040](DECISIONS.md#adr-040) verbatim — clause (d) is recorded as **EXPLICITLY DEFERRED, not satisfied, not waived** (Sec joint-review B3, recorded at Sec's requirement). [ADR-011](DECISIONS.md#adr-011) Decision 1 commits non-JWT writes to a four-clause discipline and states a new privileged-context surface **MUST** adopt it at design time; it names **"Lock 7 NAV worker"** under *Confirmed reusability*, so this surface is one of the four D1 was generalized from, not merely covered by it. Clauses (a)/(b)/(c) are satisfied at ship; **(d) explicit audit log capturing the tenant-resolution chain is NOT** — `emit_audit_log` raises `NotImplementedError` and the worker emits no audit row. **ADR-040 states a tracked follow-up is required to carry the unblocking work; this entry is that record.** *(Absent from Sec's ranked six — a ranking-scope artifact, not a de-prioritization; see the section preamble.)*
- **AC.** The W-1 worker emits a same-transaction audit row capturing the **tenant-resolution chain**: run identity, which tenants were enumerated, per-tenant success/failure, and the fact of a checkpoint having been **attempted and failed** (today a tenant that errors leaves no durable trace at all). Scope note — `nav_daily` is itself immutable and already records the tenant-resolution *outcome* (`users_id`, `nav_date`, `nav_value`, `created_at`); what is missing is the *chain*, so **risk is forensic, not exploitative** — which is why this is a recorded deferral rather than a ship-block, and must not be restated as one. Sec joint-review-mandatory (ADR-011 Decision 2 audit-class).
- **Dependencies.** **Blocked-by:** the `plaid_sync_audit`-class audit table **plus** the general same-transaction audit-log insert helper — the **reserved, already-allowlisted 4th DEFINER slot**, still unauthored (its tracker is named in [ADR-040](DECISIONS.md#adr-040) / [ADR-026](DECISIONS.md#adr-026) as the manual-accounts issue's Task #7; the ID is deliberately not repeated here. **⚠ Verified 2026-08-03 against live Linear: that manual-accounts issue is CLOSED (Done 2026-07-06, PRs #141–144), so its "Task #7" is not a workable tracker** — the audit-log helper was deferred *out* of it at close (see [`MILESTONES.md`](MILESTONES.md) V1.0 close notes) and currently has **no live tracker of its own**. That is not a citation typo: the reference is historically accurate about where the work was scoped, and wrong only about whether that scope is still open. Re-derive or create a tracker when the Linear free-issue limit lifts; until then this §7 entry IS the tracker). **Authoring it moves no ledger** — the SECURITY DEFINER allowlist is committed at 4 (authored 3), and the §10 catalogued-instance ledger is separate and unchanged. Owners: Architect (audit table + DEFINER helper), Backend (`workers/etl` emit path), Sec (joint-review).

**S8. Record the working-tree-vs-`main..HEAD` lesson in the `finish-feature` / `merge-pr` skills.** [process control]
- **Source.** Architect proposal at the SELF-214 review, concurred by team-lead and PM. The pre-push-gate lesson — **compare the working tree, not `main..HEAD`** — is a process control, and a **skill** is where someone encounters it at the moment it matters; an ADR is not read at merge time. Compounds the standing memory `feedback_verify_paired_artifacts_before_push`.
- **AC.** Add the check to the `finish-feature` and `merge-pr` skill bodies: before push/PR/merge, verify the **working tree** (`git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all`), not only `git diff --stat main..HEAD` — an untracked or staged-then-modified file is invisible to the latter, which is exactly how `055` nearly merged omitted. **Explicitly subordinate to S4:** the hook is the structural fence, this is the procedural complement. **It must not be treated as a substitute** — a procedural-over-structural trade is what Sec ruled against elsewhere in this review, and if S4 lands this note becomes genuine belt-and-suspenders rather than the false kind S3 is about.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: none. Owner: **team-lead / DevOps** (skill definitions). Distinct from S4 (the hook itself). Two-way door.

**S9. Record three battery-design lessons in `supabase/tests/rls/DESIGN.md`.** [process control]
- **Source.** QA's request at the SELF-214 review. Distinct from S8 (different file, different owner, different content): S8 is about *what to check before merging*; this is about *how to build a battery that cannot lie to you*. All three are failure modes where a battery reports success it did not earn.
- **AC.** Record, each with the SELF-214 instance that produced it: **(i) leg-independence** — an inversion suite proves the legs are *independent*, not merely that the fences have teeth; a battery that only asserts "fence fires" cannot distinguish one fence doing all the work from each leg holding on its own. **(ii) shape-agnostic assertions degrade silently** — an assertion written loosely enough to survive a pending decision keeps passing after that decision lands, having quietly stopped testing the thing it was written for; the degradation is invisible because the test still goes green. **(iii) the partial-run guard** — **"nothing failed" and "nothing ran" are indistinguishable unless you count.** A battery must assert its own expected assertion count, or a setup error that skips the suite reads as a clean pass. This one generalizes past RLS and is the strongest of the three.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: the existing two-tenant pgTAP battery + `supabase/tests/rls/`. Owner: **QA** (`supabase/tests/rls/DESIGN.md`). Two-way door. Downstream: **directly strengthens S1** — the C3 invariant assertion is only worth booking if the battery carrying it cannot silently no-op, which is exactly lesson (iii). Sec generalises the point past S1: it holds for **every assertion-type entry in §7** — *"an invariant assertion's entire value is that it goes RED at the right moment; a harness that can abort past it and still report clean makes it **worse than absent** — absent, nobody is reassured; present-but-skippable, everybody is."* S1 is flagged only because "highest value *if it fires*" is the profile that suffers most from a silent no-op.

**S10. ~~Capability-verify `compute_and_checkpoint_user` end-to-end under a real `pfin_etl` login before Phase-7 scheduling.~~ — ✅ DISCHARGED 2026-08-09.** [was: PHASE-7 DEPLOY GATE]
- **⚠ Deviation, stated rather than buried.** The local Supabase CLI stack offers no TLS while `build_database_url` **hard-coded** `sslmode=require` *(true as written at discharge; **fixed since, at `a85c9f5` — see S11**, which is why this bullet is past-tense now and was not simply deleted)*, so the run patched **that parameter only**, in a scratchpad runner that is **not committed**. Everything else was the production path. **TLS transport is therefore NOT covered by this discharge** — S10 does not ask for it, and the deploy-time §6.1 checks are unchanged. **That half is unchanged by S11's fix and remains true today:** S11 made the parameter configurable, which is a *verifiability* fix; it did not put a TLS-terminating stack under the discharge run, so no TLS transport has ever been exercised end to end by this worker. Do not read S11's discharge as closing it.
- *Body removed at the 2026-08-20 §7 hygiene sweep (F/CTO ruling: discharged content is deleted, the PR trail is the record). Verdict + citation retained above; history in the cited PR(s) and the sweep PR.*

Make the transport parameter configurable — env-driven with `require` as the **default**, so production is unchanged and unspecified environments still fail closed. **Do not** simply relax it: `require` is correct for production and `utils.py` documents TLS as a property of this function. ✅ **Done.** Pair with a note in the `workers/etl` README naming the local-stack path. **⚠ NOT done** — the env plumbing is documented in `workers/etl/.env.example:178–185`, which is arguably the better home, but **the README named in the AC carries no `sslmode` mention at all** (grep-verified at `832cfb7`).
