**S19. The ETL loader accepted only the PRODUCTION BLS name, so a developer's `.env` had to hold a production-named credential.** [convention gap — auditability, not exposure]
- **Source.** Raised by Sec at the S12 review, 2026-08-09; landed with S12 (PR #369) because it rides the same `load_api_keys()` split. **Number allocated at insert (next-free after S18), not carried from the proposal** — an ID baked into a committed doc before allocation is the follow-up-number-that-names-nothing failure.
- **⚠ It is an EXTENSION beyond S12's AC, not part of it, and MUST NOT be closed by S12 passing.** S12 rules **where the raise happens** (point of use, not load). This rules **which name the key carries**. They share a function and nothing else; closing this because S12 is green would retire an unmet requirement.
- **Mechanism.** The secrets manifest's convention rests on **distinct NAMES carrying the tier**. The code defeated that at the local boundary: **`BLS_API_KEY_TEST` had no reader**, and the loader took the production name unprefixed — so a developer machine necessarily held a **production-named** credential whatever value was in it. The manifest's third-tier block states plainly that the non-overlap invariant **does not reach local dev and cannot**; this is the loader half of that boundary.
- **✅ What shipped.** A per-key env-name preference: **`BLS_API_KEY_TEST` preferred when present, falling back to `BLS_API_KEY`**, so deployed containers — which set only the production name — are unaffected. `.env.example` now documents the TEST name as the one a developer sets. A test pins that the override is **per-key and does not generalise**, since it was ruled for BLS specifically.
- **⚠ IT IS NOT A FENCE AND MUST NOT BE DESCRIBED AS ONE.** Nothing can inspect a developer's `.env`, and nothing should try. CI cannot observe a local machine. What it buys is that the production name is **absent from developer machines entirely**, so *"is there a production-named credential on this laptop?"* becomes a question with a **checkable right answer of NO** — the discipline made auditable **by shape rather than by trust**. Anyone extending this should not add a check that pretends otherwise.
- **✅ CODE HALF DISCHARGED 2026-08-09 (PR #369); ⚠ THE PRACTICE HALF IS NOT, AND THE ENTRY STAYS OPEN BECAUSE OF IT.** The loader now prefers `BLS_API_KEY_TEST` and falls back to `BLS_API_KEY`, and `.env.example` documents the TEST name as the one a developer sets. **But the whole point of this entry is that the PRODUCTION NAME IS ABSENT FROM DEVELOPER MACHINES** — and it is not yet: the local `.env` still holds the key under `BLS_API_KEY` (established by checking the variable NAME only, never the value). **Outstanding: rename that one line to `BLS_API_KEY_TEST`**, which the loader already prefers. No code impact.
- **⚠ Deliberately NOT struck.** A strikethrough reads as fully closed, and the code half alone does not deliver the property: *"is there a production-named credential on this laptop?"* still answers **YES**. **Marking it done because the code shipped would be the exact shape of claiming coverage a change does not provide** — this entry's own subject is a convention that holds only if practice follows it.
- **Owner.** Backend (the loader — **done**) + DevOps (the manifest side, PR #367 — done) + Sec (tier scoping) + **F/CTO (the one-line `.env` rename — the outstanding half)**. **Not blocking.**

**S20. ~~A BLS period published with no value was dropped SILENTLY, so a gap in `pfin.cpi_u_index` was undetectable by construction.~~ — ✅ DISCHARGED 2026-08-10 (PR #380).** [was: observability defect — the drop was correct, the silence was not]
- *Body removed at the 2026-08-20 §7 hygiene sweep (F/CTO ruling: discharged content is deleted, the PR trail is the record). Verdict + citation retained above; history in the cited PR(s) and the sweep PR.*

**S21. `fetch_cpi_df` discards value-null periods from its own return value, so `pfin.cpi_u_nonpublication` cannot be populated by any writer — and the fix has TWO HALVES THAT MUST SHIP TOGETHER.** [gap — blocks ADR-049 from doing anything; ⚠ splitting it is a regression]
- **Source.** Found 2026-08-10 during the [ADR-049](DECISIONS.md#adr-049) migration's Sec joint-review (PR #389), as blocking condition C3. Number allocated at insert (next-free after S20), not carried from the proposal.
- **Mechanism, measured at `workers/etl/src/pfin_back_etl/utils.py:513–515`** — inside `fetch_cpi_df`, before the return: `n_before = len(df)` / `dropped = df.filter(value.is_null())` / `df = df.drop_nulls(subset=["value"])`. **The `2025-10` row — the exact subject of `063` — never leaves the transport.** It is logged and discarded.
- **⚠ WHY THIS WAS WORSE THAN AN OMISSION, and the reason it blocked a merge.** `063`'s header initially instructed a future writer to reuse the CPI-U mapper's month projection but not its value filter. That is **correct and unexecutable**: the rows are destroyed one layer earlier, so there is nothing to apply the projection to. A writer following the documented requirement gets an empty record, **has discharged the stated obligation, and has no reason to look further.** An omission invites a question; a false sense of completion answers it in advance.
- **⚠⚠ THE TWO HALVES ARE ONE ITEM. Landing the first alone makes things strictly worse.** **(i)** Lift or opt-out the transport drop so a consumer that wants value-null periods can receive them. **(ii)** Land the **count reconciliation**: *periods returned by transport = rows upserted to `053` + rows recorded in `063`*, **failing loud on imbalance**. Today `063` is **correctly empty** — empty is its only reachable state. The moment (i) ships without (ii), "empty" silently changes meaning from *correct* to *broken*, **and no test, constraint, or layer would notice** — the two worlds are identical at every layer. **Do not schedule these as two tickets.**
- **The enforceable half is already half-built and must not be reinvented.** `fetch_cpi_df` computes returned / kept / dropped, and its own note states *"THE RECONCILIATION IS THE LOAD-BEARING HALF, NOT THE PER-ROW LINE. A per-row warning still says nothing about a SYSTEMATIC drop… Counting in and out catches the class."* One assertion catches both obstacles **without either being individually remembered**, and catches a future drop added for some third reason.
- **⚠ Also required: the raw token.** `cast(pl.Float64, strict=False)` overwrites the column in place, so the literal `'-'` is destroyed before the drop. `063.published_value_raw` is nullable and its comment anticipates this, so the schema is already correct — but the transport cannot currently supply the token either.
- **⚠ The parser fence rule, ruled by Sec 2026-08-10 and easy to get backwards.** A non-publication writer **is** the "second live consumer" that `fetch_cpi_df`'s note names as its fence trigger. But the two consumers **agree on grain and are exactly opposed on value**. So: **grain fence permitted (both want it; optional) · value fence FORBIDDEN (it would destroy this table's subject) · the existing value drop must be LIFTED or made opt-out — which runs opposite to "add a fence."**
- **Test venue.** `workers/etl` pytest, not the DB layer. **No database test can see a process that never ran** — QA stated this as explicit non-coverage rather than leaving it implied.
- **Owner.** Backend (`workers/etl`). **Not blocking any merge; blocking ADR-049 from having any effect.** ⚠ "ADR-049 has landed" must not be read as "the record is working."

**S22. Stalled-ingest detection is assigned to NO OWNER anywhere.** [gap — surfaced by withdrawing a false belief, not by new work]
- **Source.** Found 2026-08-10 in team-lead cross-check of the ADR-049 Sec verdict (PR #389).
- **Mechanism.** `fn_cpi_u_index_for_period`'s trailing-edge bound is data-derived (a period is a gap only if a *later* period exists), which is inside [ADR-049](DECISIONS.md#adr-049) Decision 3's *"or a stricter bound"* grant and consults no clock. Its accepted cost, stated in the migration: **it cannot detect a stalled ingest** — a dead ETL yields `beyond_coverage` forever, indistinguishable from "not yet published."
- **⚠ How the gap was concealed.** The verdict accepting that trade cited ADR-049 D2 as *"already assigning stalled-ingest detection to a run log."* D2's actual words are *"two operational states that **a run log already narrows**"* — a subordinate clause justifying the rejection of option C′, which **assigns nothing.** Sec conceded the overstatement on cross-check. **The ACCEPT survives on other grounds; the assignment never existed.** So the helper's disclaimer — *"ingest-freshness monitoring is NOT this function's job"* — is **accurate and pointing at nobody.**
- **The generalisable shape:** a correct scope-boundary statement reads like a routing statement. *"Not my job"* is only safe when someone else's job says otherwise.
- **AC.** Name an owner and a venue for ETL run-freshness monitoring. Do **not** solve it inside the helper — the boundary is right. **This entry must not close as a comment in a migration.**
- **Owner.** Backend + DevOps (venue TBD). Not blocking.

**S23. Two defects in `053_cpi_u_index.sql`, both confirmed by direct inspection, both needing a vehicle it cannot supply itself.** [refinement + latent defect]
- **Source.** Found 2026-08-10 during ADR-049 migration authoring; Sec confirmed both and ruled them a **separate vehicle** that does not block PR #389.
- **(a) The `source` column comment is FALSE as written.** It claims the column exists so a future series *"can coexist without a schema change."* `053`'s PK is **`cpi_period` alone**, so two series would collide on period — they cannot coexist. ⚠ Being a `comment on`, it **has a database representation**, so per the `apply-migration` rule it can only be corrected by a **new comment-only migration** (the `052` shape), never by editing the merged file.
- **(b) `053` has no first-of-month CHECK**, though its comment says the period is normalized to first-of-month. Only `cpi_u_index_value_finite` exists. `063` enforces one; `053` does not.
- **⚠ (b) has a live interaction with `064` and is the half with teeth.** A mis-keyed row such as `2025-10-15` would be **invisible to the exact-match branch**, **selected as the carry source** by the carry-forward branch, **and would move the coverage edge** in the bounds branch — **one malformed row changing another period's value AND its classification at once.** `064`'s CONTRACT block records this as an inherited precondition: all three branches depend on it and none can check it.
- **AC.** One comment-only migration correcting (a); one migration adding the CHECK for (b), with a pre-flight scan for existing violations. **Verify no violating row exists before adding the constraint** — the table holds 137 real rows.
- **Owner.** Architect (authoring) + Sec (joint-review — `053` feeds financial figures). Low priority, not blocking.

**S24. `update_table_all` has NO exception isolation, so any raising ingest aborts every ingest sequenced after it — and the S21 CPI-U reconciliation gate is a new raise site whose reachability is held open by a DIFFERENT ticket.** [latent defect + refinement — blocks nothing today; ⚠ the "blocks nothing" half expires on S6's schedule, not on ours]
- **Source.** Raised as FLAG-2 (a) + NOTE-1 (b) at the Sec joint-review of the **S21** CPI-U transport lift + reconciliation gate, 2026-08-10. Both are **pre-existing**; neither blocks S21. Backend, who owns the surface, asked they be filed as one entry rather than fixed inline — they are one file, one owner, one pass. Number allocated at insert (next-free after S23), not carried from the proposal. **⚠ Sec's original framing of (a) was measured wrong on reachability, and the corrected version is the one filed here** — the correction is the reason the entry is worth having, not a footnote to it.
- **(a) Mechanism, measured on `main` at `583a41c` 2026-08-10 and RE-MEASURED at `f393ca1` 2026-08-11 — unchanged.** `update_table_all` in `workers/etl/src/pfin_back_etl/core.py` calls **ten** ingests in sequence with **no `try`/`except` anywhere in its body** — ten bare statements and a `return`. Any raise from any ingest aborts the remainder, including `update_table_eod_price`, which is **last of the ten** and feeds `pfin.eod_price` → NAV → net worth. S21 introduced a new raise site into that sequence: `CpiReconciliationError` out of `update_table_cpi_u_index`, which is **second**. So the shape Sec named is real: a malformed BLS **CPI** response could stop the nightly **price** ingest.
- **⚠ Cited by name-anchor deliberately — and this entry's own history is the argument for it.** The proposal that seeded S24 cited `core.py:818`/`:819` for the first two calls. At `583a41c` that was **wrong**: they were at `:745`/`:746`, inside `update_table_all` at `:740` — the proposal had measured a worktree with S21 applied and reported it as `main`. At `f393ca1`, after S21 merged (#412), they are at **`:818`/`:819`** — *the originally-cited numbers, now correct.* ⚠ **The number became right without the reasoning that produced it ever becoming right**, and a reader spot-checking it today would confirm it and learn nothing. **These anchors moved 73 lines in under 24 hours.** Re-measure before acting; **trust no line number in this entry, including this one.**
- **⚠ (a) is NOT reachable today, and the reason is a second pre-existing defect. Filing only this half would be the failure mode.** Of the ten calls, **seven target tables no V1 migration creates** (`create table if not exists pfin.` grep across `supabase/migrations/`, 2026-08-10 and **re-run at `f393ca1` 2026-08-11, unchanged**: `pfin.asset` / `pfin.cpi_u_index` / `pfin.eod_price` exist; `pfin.cpi` / `equity_profile` / `reporting_period` / `income_statement` / `balance_sheet_statement` / `cash_flow_statement` / `earning` do not — the ADR-049 stack added `065`/`066`, which create no tables, and `063`'s `pfin.cpi_u_nonpublication` is not in this sequence). The **first** call, `update_table_cpi`, targets `pfin.cpi` — so `update_table_all` dies at call #1 with `UndefinedTable` and **never reaches the reconciliation gate at all**. `workers/CLAUDE.md` documents the same dead path independently. Consequence: **`eod_price` is already unreachable through this entry point, by a pre-existing defect, with or without S21.** The blast radius is already fully realized; S21 **cannot widen it through `update_table_all`**. Not urgent, and **not a reason to hold anything**.
- **⚠⚠ But that is a stay of execution, not a fix, and it expires on someone else's schedule. This is why the entry exists.** Those dead calls are the subject of **S6** (V1-scope ruling on the incumbent FMP financial-statement tables). When S6 is *implemented*, the shields leave the sequence and the widened trigger set goes live — **in a commit authored by someone who is not thinking about CPI reconciliation.** Left only as a note here, the hardening gets discovered by an outage rather than by the schedule.
- **⚠ The arming precondition is more specific than "fix `update_table_cpi`", and the correction is load-bearing.** Removing `update_table_cpi` **alone does not arm this**: six more dead calls (`equity_profile` … `earning`) sit between the CPI-U gate and `eod_price`, so the abort point merely moves from call #1 to call #4 and `eod_price` stays unreachable. **The arming event is the LAST dead call leaving `update_table_all`** — S6's Class A retirement **and** its Class B ruling, both implemented. **A partial S6 landing does not arm it.** Stated this precisely so whoever takes S6 can answer *"did I arm S24?"* mechanically instead of by judgement.
- **AC (a).** Either **(i)** exception isolation in `update_table_all` so one ingest's failure cannot abort the ingests independent of it — with the failure still surfacing **loudly**, not swallowed (⚠ a bare per-call `except: pass` converts this into a *silent partial nightly* defect, which is worse; **S20**'s lesson was that the drop was correct and the **silence** was not) — **or (ii)** an explicit ratified decision that fail-fast-on-first-error **is** the intended nightly posture, recorded with its reasoning. **Do not default to (i) because it looks like the safe one**: ordering may be load-bearing between some pairs, and that is Backend's call to make, not this entry's. ⚠ Whichever is chosen, **`eod_price`'s independence from a CPI failure is the property to preserve.**
- **⚠ Cross-reference that must be kept true, not merely named — this is a requirement whose precondition is held by a different ticket.** **S6 must not land its implementation without (a) being met or explicitly ruled unnecessary.** Recorded **reciprocally in S6's Dependencies**, because a cross-reference living only in the *downstream* entry is invisible to the person who arms it. **PM owns S6, so keeping that reciprocal true is PM's job.**
- **Where the widened trigger IS reachable today:** `run_cpi_backfill.py`, or a direct call to `update_table_cpi_u_index`. There, raising is the **desired** behaviour — that runner does nothing else, so there is nothing to abort. **No change wanted there**; recorded so nobody "fixes" it.
- **(b) NOTE-1 — `year` is not guarded non-null before `pl.date(...)`.** The CPI-U mapper filters `month` non-null and in 1–12, and `series_value` non-null and finite — but **not `year`**. A null `year` makes `pl.date(pl.col("year"), pl.col("month"), 1)` yield a null `cpi_period`, which hits the period column's NOT NULL and aborts the ingest. **Pre-existing; not introduced by S21.**
- **⚠ On "both mappers" — TWO-SITED as of `f393ca1`, and the retracted version is kept because the retraction is the lesson.** **Measured on `main` at `f393ca1` 2026-08-11: there are two mappers**, `_map_cpi_u_index_df` and `_map_cpi_u_nonpublication_df`, and **neither guards `year`** — both filter `month` non-null and 1–12 and stop there, so the gap is **symmetric across both**. ~~*As first filed at `583a41c`: exactly one mapper on `main`, the complement still on Backend's unmerged `fix/etl-cpi-nonpublication-writer`.*~~ **That branch merged at #412**, hours after this entry was written. **Backend deliberately did not guard one half in isolation**, because doing so would falsify the complement mapper's own docstring claim — *"GRAIN — IDENTICAL … applied BEFORE any value predicate"*, the exact-complement-on-value / same-filter-on-grain contract — which **Sec verified as true**, and which is **still the binding constraint**: the mappers' value predicates are exactly opposed (`series_value` null-or-non-finite vs non-null-and-finite) while their grain filters are byte-identical, so guarding `year` in one and not the other breaks the symmetry the docstring asserts. Filed so **both are guarded in one pass**.
- **⚠⚠ This entry's own "blocks nothing" caveat half-expired within a day — on someone else's merge, exactly as predicted.** S24 was filed warning that a precondition held by a different ticket would lapse in a commit whose author was not thinking about this entry. **(b)'s precondition then did precisely that, same day, and the trigger was the very branch the entry was describing.** Recorded because it is the cleanest available instance of the shape S24 exists to warn about, and because it dates the rest of these measurements: **every dated claim here is a claim about a tree, not about the world.**
- **AC (b).** Add the `year` non-null guard to **both** CPI mappers in a **single change**, preserving the complement/grain symmetry the docstring asserts. ~~*Superseded: the earlier AC said to wait for `fix/etl-cpi-nonpublication-writer` or fix within it — that branch has landed and the instruction is spent.*~~ **Do not land the asymmetric half**; that constraint is unchanged and is the reason this is one AC and not two.
- **Owner.** **Backend** (`workers/etl`). Sec raised; Backend owns the surface. **Blocks nothing today.** What changes that: **(a)** goes live when S6's implementation removes the **last** dead call from `update_table_all` — *still pending, and still the arming event to watch*; **(b)** is **already two-sited** as of `f393ca1` (#412), so its scope is now fixed at two mappers rather than pending — ~~*as first filed, this read "becomes two-sited when `fix/etl-cpi-nonpublication-writer` lands."*~~ Neither is a one-way door.

### §7.7 — close-gate joint-review findings

*Findings raised at the `is_active` close-gate Architect + Sec joint review (8 passes), 2026-08-02/03, staged here per [ADR-017](DECISIONS.md#adr-017) Decision 2. **Specs transcribed from Sec's §16 copy-ready block**, which both agents agreed is the source of record for this filing (in preference to Architect's §21 table). The working docs live in `temp/` (gitignored — **which is why these entries exist**: seven joint-review passes and three live defects existed only in untracked files and a message thread, the exact `feedback_verify_paired_artifacts_before_push` shape).*

*Four framing notes. **(1) Placement — this is a separate section from §7.6, and the grounds are mechanical.** Three checkable properties, none of which depends on either section's title. **(i) Distinct numbering scheme.** §7.7 runs `F1`–`F11`; §7.6 runs `S1`–`S24`. The sequences are not interchangeable, and **`§7.7` is referenced from more than twenty places in this file** (measured at `f6ef017` before this note was written: 23 — 1 heading, 13 body, 9 inside the dated changelog footer). ⚠ **Stated as a floor, not a figure, because this note itself cites `§7.7` several times and would falsify its own number** — the count only grows, and the argument it supports (a renumber breaks references) strengthens as it does, so a floor is the honest and the robust form. Merging would force either a renumber of `F1`–`F11` into the `S`-sequence, breaking those references, or two numbering schemes inside one subsection — **worse than two subsections**. Keeping them apart costs one paragraph; merging costs a re-file. **(ii) Distinct origin.** §7.7 is the `is_active` close-gate Architect + Sec joint review — **one bounded event** (8 passes, 2026-08-02/03). §7.6 is explicitly a **stream** that accretes from successive reviews. A bounded filing and an open stream have different append rules, and that difference is the thing the section boundary encodes. **(iii) Its own conventions.** §7.7 records **Sec's §16 copy-ready block as its source of record in preference to Architect's §21 table**, and cites by title rather than by label (note (2)). Those rules govern this section and not §7.6. ⚠ **The previous rationale was RETIRED 2026-08-11 because it was falsified, not because it was wrong when written.** It read: *"§7.6's own heading and preamble scope it to 'SELF-214 Sec joint-review follow-ups,' and most of the entries below are close-gate findings, not SELF-214 follow-ups."* **That was true and load-bearing when filed.** F/CTO retitled §7.6 to *"Sec joint-review follow-ups (SELF-214 onward)"*, which both **removed the quoted heading** and **shrank the scoping mismatch the argument rested on** — §7.6 is now openly a stream of Sec joint-review follow-ups, and these are Sec joint-review findings. **The conclusion survived; its stated argument did not.** Replaced with the mechanical grounds above precisely because those cannot be falsified by a retitle. Filing close-gate work under a scoped header would still be the **precedent-without-its-surrounding-conditions** shape this review caught five times — that hazard is unchanged; only the evidence for the boundary moved. The **`nav_daily` tenant-enumeration** entry is the one genuinely `nav_daily`-originated defect; it is homed here for provenance coherence and **cross-referenced from §7.6's preamble**, since it feeds **S7**'s AC. **(2) Cite entries in this section BY TITLE, never by `F#`.** The `F#` prefixes are positional labels in a growing list and are not stable identifiers (the ADR-040 ordinal-label ruling); the same reasoning is why **no entry title carries a count**. ⚠ **FLAG 2026-08-11 — this section's `F1`–`F11` sit in a namespace [ADR-051](DECISIONS.md#adr-051) deliberately left unaudited, and the collision is KNOWN, not an oversight.** ADR-051 Decision 1 rules that **permanent** labels take a namespaced prefix while **per-PR finding** labels stay bare, and renamed the ARCH §4 Observability gates to `OBS-F<n>` on exactly that ground. Its **Consequences** (not Decision 3 — Decision 3 is the migration-scope enumeration) state verbatim: *"This ADR does not audit the tree for **other permanent-label populations sharing an ephemeral namespace**. `C<n>` (Sec joint-review conditions) and `RF<n>` are the obvious candidates and are unexamined — flagged rather than assumed clean."* **`BACKLOG.md` appears nowhere in ADR-051** (measured at `f6ef017`) — neither in Decision 3's routed-to-owners list nor its not-retro-edited list — so this is a **third such candidate, wholly unexamined.** ⚠ **And it is a sharper case than the two named**, because ADR-051 rejected alternative (δ) (prefix the per-PR labels) on the grounds that *that* population is *"unbounded, generated by every review forever, and has no stable home territory to name; the bounded population is the one to move."* **§7.7's `F<n>` is bounded and has a stable home** — it resembles the population ADR-051 **renamed**, not the one it left bare, while wearing the bare form. **No rename is proposed here**: renaming labels referenced from 23 places is the same re-file cost declined twice already, and the audit question is routed separately. Note (2)'s cite-by-title rule is the working mitigation — **follow it**, and the label form stops mattering. **(3) The six entries filed in the original 2026-08-03 batch were drafted against the close-gate packages, BEFORE [ADR-042](DECISIONS.md#adr-042) was ratified later that day** — that ADR retires `pfin.account.is_active` entirely (three-concept model; migrations `056`/`057`/`058`). **The reconciliation pass has run and is COMPLETE** (Architect 2026-08-03, with Sec ruling on the V2-grantee surface): three entries carry a **Disposition** bullet recording the outcome, and **the outcome was not uniform — one was discharged, one INVERTED, one lost its original argument while keeping its subject.** Do not assume a finding survives a ratify just because its subject still exists. **Sec's decomposition, which sharpens this and prevents over-filing:** *discharged* is **not a failure mode at all** (a finding fixed by unrelated work is good news you want to notice so nobody does it twice); *kept-its-subject-lost-its-argument* is **the already-named "requirement outliving its own precondition" variant** — do not double-file it; **only INVERSION is genuinely new** — the words stayed true and the behaviour they describe reversed, and **under V1 both forms are behaviourally indistinguishable, so the flip has no footprint.** Nothing goes red, nothing reads wrong, and it surfaces only when sharing ships. That is what **CP8** exists for. Entries added after that ratify are marked as such. **(4) The Linear free-issue limit is a BOOKING constraint, not a CAPTURE constraint** — §7 is the ratified home per [ADR-017](DECISIONS.md#adr-017) Decision 2 and needs no Linear seat, so nothing below is blocked on that unresolved F/CTO cost decision. Promote at the first milestone-rotation after the limit lifts. **No live Linear IDs are cited** — a merged PR referencing one auto-closes it.*

**F1. `pfin.eod_price` admits zero and negative prices — live NAV defect.** [flag]
- **Source.** Close-gate joint review 2026-08-02 (Architect found the `fx_feed` zero case; Sec sharpened to sign-flip). `019:220` declares `price numeric(20,4) not null` with `constraint eod_price_price_finite check (price <> 'NaN'::numeric)` as its **only** value constraint. **Impact.** A zero `fx_feed` rate zeroes a foreign-currency cash leg in `fn_compute_nav`; a **negative** rate flips its sign, so NAV silently *subtracts* money that exists. `= 0` and `abs(…) < ε` both miss the negative case.
- **AC.** Add `check (source <> 'fx_feed' or price > 0)`. **Source-conditional, not blanket** — a delisted security legitimately prices at `0`, and `future` is in the `asset_type` vocabulary (`016:202`) where negative settlement is real (WTI, April 2020). An asset-type-conditional rule is **not expressible as a CHECK** — `eod_price` carries no `asset_type` column (`019:528` / `019:563` reach it only by joining `pfin.asset`) — which is why the expressible constraint is the one that matters. Backfill review required: existing rows must be checked before the constraint lands, since a `NOT VALID` add would skip exactly the rows at issue. **Named residual (deliberate, Sec-ruled — do not close in V1).** A zero-or-negative **non-fx** price still zeroes or flips a *holdings* contribution and stays admissible. Not closeable declaratively. **A trigger policing a price domain that legitimately includes negatives would be guessing which negatives are real, and a fence that guesses is the class this project keeps removing.**
- **Dependencies.** Ingest-path review (`eod_price` is `service_role`/ETL-written, `019`). Owner: Architect (DDL) + Backend (ingest) + Sec (joint-review). Independent of the close gate — under D3 the gate is immune; this protects NAV.

**F2. `nav_daily` tenant enumeration skips instead of writing zero — live defect. DISCHARGED at the `is_active` drop; AC absorbed as an acceptance criterion of that slice.** [flag — discharged, do not schedule separately]
- *Body removed at the 2026-08-20 §7 hygiene sweep (F/CTO ruling: discharged content is deleted, the PR trail is the record). Verdict + citation retained above; history in the cited PR(s) and the sweep PR.*

**F3. The V2-grantee account-join on `fn_compute_nav` — fail-closed is the BACKSTOP; V2 must make the unresolvable state UNREACHABLE. RE-SCOPED: the finding inverts rather than survives.** [V2 precondition]
- **Disposition (Architect + Sec, 2026-08-03, post-[ADR-042](DECISIONS.md#adr-042); Sec RULED — this is Sec's V2 surface).** The original entry described a **fail-closed under-report**: `050`'s securities leg uses `coalesce(acc2.is_active, false)` over a LEFT JOIN, so a V2 grantee who sees the holdings (`rd_access`-scoped) but not the account row (owner-only) gets `acc2` NULL → `false` → **excluded**. Re-pointed naively to `acc2.closed_at is null`, that same invisible row gives `NULL is null` → **TRUE → included: fail-OPEN, over-reporting.** **In V1 the two are identical**, because the account row always resolves — which is exactly why the flip would be **chosen silently at the re-point** if nobody wrote it down. **Sec ruling: PRESERVE FAIL-CLOSED**, for three reasons, the first deciding. **(1) Fail-closed fails visibly; fail-open fails silently.** A grantee whose NAV is too low notices and complains — *that is how this entry came to exist*. A NAV that is too high because it counted positions from an account we could not see **looks exactly like a correct NAV**. **(2) Absence is not a determinate answer** — `closed_at is null` on an unresolvable row asserts *"not closed"* **on the basis of no information**. **(3) Fail-open's correctness is derivative** — it is safe only while the close gate holds (a closed account is empty, so including it costs nothing): true today, **unnamed as a dependency**, and false against any row the gate never validated.
- **Reframing — Sec's correction, and the load-bearing half. This entry is NOT "decide the V2 behaviour."** Both agents initially framed it that way and both were wrong. **The account row is unresolvable at all only because of a predicate mismatch that is already a separate obligation**: `account_select` is owner-only while the holdings legs use the `rd_access`-JOIN. **The correct V2 fix is to make those predicates agree**, after which the account row always resolves and **the default is never exercised by anyone**. So the entry reads: **fail-closed is a backstop, and V2 must make the unresolvable state unreachable** — *not* "pick a default." **That framing matters because it stops the under-report being recorded as an accepted cost.**
- **Source.** Close-gate joint review 2026-08-02 (Architect). The read predicates differ: `account_select` (`025:201–208`) is **owner-only** (`users_id = auth.uid()`); `fn_holdings_as_of` reads `holdings_checkpoint` (`019:441`) + `account_trans` (`019:450`, `019:460`), both **`rd_access`-JOIN**-scoped (`025:437` / `025:254`).
- **AC.** Reconcile `050`'s `left join pfin.account` against the ACL-scoped holdings legs. Under V2 sharing a non-owner grantee sees the holdings and not the account row → `acc2` NULL → `coalesce(acc2.is_active, false)` → **every shared holding silently drops from their NAV**. **Why V1 is safe and why that expires.** The two predicates coincide **only because `account_users` holds nothing but creator-grant rows**. Dormancy is the guarantee, not the FK.
- **Dependencies — TWO DIFFERENT TIMELINES. Do not collapse them (Sec correction, 2026-08-03).** Owner: Architect + Sec.
  - **SHIPS IN `059`, V1, NOW — not deferrable.** The legible conjunct **`acc2.account_id is not null and acc2.closed_at is null`** goes into the as-of re-point, establishing fail-closed as the backstop, **commented so that removing it visibly flips the default.** **This is a V1 DDL requirement, NOT a V2 obligation, and it is NOT on the B5 gate.** Its *reasoning* references the gate only because that is when its backstop role ends. **Sec's own correction, and the reason it matters: filed as a third V2 obligation this reads as deferrable to the sharing PR — and deferring it means `059` ships without the conjunct, which is the exact silent flip the ruling exists to prevent. A misfiled timeline would have defeated the finding.**
  - **B5 hard-gate — V2, same PR** (`003:152` already carries the trigger: *"MUST land in the SAME PR that first grants any authenticated write here"*). Two obligations: (i) **`rd_access` in the WITHHELD column set** (the entry below); (ii) **reconcile the `account_select`-vs-`rd_access`-JOIN predicate mismatch**, which is what makes the unresolvable state reachable at all. **Rider on (ii):** if (ii) is not fully discharged, **the fail-closed path must become observable** — a control that fires silently is one you learn about from user complaints.
  - *Named rather than numbered, per the ordinal-label convention — and the naming is what exposed the error: **ordinals conceal heterogeneity**, so three items in a numbered list read as three of the same kind when one of them shipped on a different timeline entirely.*

**F4. `rd_access` must be in the WITHHELD column set of the B5 hard-gate — V1-latent.** [V2 precondition]
- **Source.** Close-gate joint review 2026-08-02. The containment argument for the **`fn_compute_nav(_, true)` under-reporting** entry (§9.1) rests on **`{can UPDATE} ⊆ {can SELECT}`**: passing `account_update` entails being able to read the ledger the close fence measures, because a direct owner necessarily holds an un-deletable creator-grant row with `rd_access` (`003:191`, AFTER INSERT; `authenticated` holds SELECT only, `003:162`).
- **AC.** When the Lock-3-mod-1 column-level UPDATE restriction lands (`REVOKE UPDATE; GRANT UPDATE (nickname, notes)`), **`rd_access` must be in the withheld set, never the grantable set.** If an owner can ever revoke their own `rd_access`, **containment inverts and the close fence reopens as a live attack** — a caller could blind themselves to the ledger and walk the gate open. **Also record the coupling itself.** The containment is **emergent across four policies and declared nowhere**; relaxing `account_update`'s aal2 conjunct degrades the fence silently with every artifact still individually defensible (the ADR-040 assembled-statement shape). **Needs a QA assertion, not a comment:** aal1+`totp` is denied the UPDATE, and a caller without `rd_access` cannot reach the fence.
- **Dependencies.** **Sec-held V2 obligation #1**, same `003:152` B5 hook as the **`fn_compute_nav(_, true)` under-reporting** entry. Owner: Architect + Sec + QA.

**F5. Land the ADR-039 `p_active_only` semantic amendment as a comment-only migration. RE-SCOPED: the disclosure/control-surface argument is GONE.** [process control]
- **Disposition (Architect, 2026-08-03, post-[ADR-042](DECISIONS.md#adr-042)).** The original framing — `p_active_only` as a **disclosure control** whose removal would silently disable the excluded-value line, hence joint-review-mandatory — **does not survive the ratified model: no ignored class exists, so there is nothing to disclose**, and the joint-review-mandatory framing drops with it. What survives is a **different obligation on the same surface**: the [ADR-039](DECISIONS.md#adr-039) **semantic amendment**. `p_active_only` changes meaning from *current-state `is_active`* to *not-closed-as-of `p_as_of`*, and its documented temporal constraint (*"sound only at `p_as_of = current_date`"*) is **STRUCK, not reworded** — the parameter becomes temporally sound in both settings. **Consolidation (Architect):** the same `052`-shape comment-only migration carries **both** this and the `046:167` count correction — one mechanism, one file. **Still never an edit to merged `050` or merged `046`**, per §7.6 **S3**'s scoping guard.
- **Disposition CHANGE (Architect, 2026-08-03; recorded here rather than done silently, because it moves a booked mechanism). The `p_active_only` comment now rides `059`, NOT the `052`-shape file.** `059` re-points `fn_compute_nav`'s **body**, and a `comment on function` **must be explicitly re-emitted there anyway** — so splitting the body from its documentation across two migrations opens a window in which **the catalog describes behaviour the function no longer has**, which is the standing *a migration must not leave behind documentation its own change falsifies* rule. Same file, same moment, no window.
  > **⚠ CORRECTION TO MY OWN REASONING, kept because the disposition survived a premise that did not.** I first justified this as *"`059` rewrites the comment anyway."* **It does not — `create or replace function` PRESERVES the existing comment**, which is precisely what this section's own ledger-count-strip entry already records (*"a `comment on …` is a database object, so it survives `create or replace` and needs its own re-emit"*). The right conclusion is **stronger** than the wrong reason suggested: because `059` must carry an explicit `comment on function` regardless, `059` is where the statement belongs. **The disposition was right for a reason I had not yet read.**
  >
  > **And the corollary I got wrong in the same breath: the `052`-shape file does NOT "shrink to `046:167` alone."** The §10-ledger-count-strip entry below has already booked **six** catalog comments onto that same migration. The file loses one rider, not all but one.

  **What `059`'s `comment on function` must state** (F/CTO instruction, 2026-08-03), beyond the struck temporal constraint: **(i)** that `p_active_only` becomes a **provable no-op on value** under the gate's invariant plus the as-of re-point — and **why it nonetheless stays** (it is a PostgREST signature; `false` is the meaningful *all-accounts-including-closed* reading the `037` memo path takes via the 1-arg wrapper), because **a provably-no-op parameter reads as dead code and the next reader's instinct is to delete it**; **(ii)** per the symmetric rule, **what would make it load-bearing again** — any weakening of its four dependencies (the close gate's three legs · the transfer-in fence · the position-neutrality of the two *exempted* tables · the re-point remaining as-of rather than current-state); **(iii)** that the boundary choice `closed_at::date > p_as_of` versus `>=` is **behaviourally free ONLY because of the invariant** (the account holds zero on its closure date either way) — it looks arbitrary, is not, and would stop being free under any schema where the invariant weakened.

- **⚠ CP6 catch inside this entry (Architect, 2026-08-03) — the Disposition and the AC contradict each other and the AC is the stale half.** The Disposition bullet rules the disclosure/control-surface argument **GONE**; the **AC bullet below still instructs the migration to record the sentence *"joint-review-mandatory because removing the parameter silently disables a disclosure control."*** Left as-is, whoever executes the AC ships the struck framing into a **catalog comment**, where it reads as live state. **Bodies get read by people following an argument; ACs get read by people executing one** — and the executable half is the one that went stale. The AC's remaining instructions (no neighbouring citation; §10 untouched) stand. **Not rewritten here** — the AC's replacement wording is Sec's per this entry's own Dependencies line, and rewriting it unilaterally is how the two halves diverged in the first place.

- **Source.** Close-gate joint review 2026-08-02. Under remedy (c), `excluded_value = fn_compute_nav(as_of, false) − fn_compute_nav(as_of, true)` is the **sole zero-derivation path** to the number that makes a GL↔NAV divergence visible.
- **AC.** Land a **`052`-shape comment-only migration** (`052_self227_audit_trace_comments.sql` is the precedent for comment-only corrections as a new migration — **not** an edit to merged `050`, per §7.6 **S3**'s scoping guard) recording: *changes to `fn_compute_nav`'s signature or `p_active_only` semantics are **joint-review-mandatory because removing the parameter silently disables a disclosure control**.* **State the property with no neighbouring citation** — an *"on the same basis as RT-NN"* analogy is the PR #66 layer-attribution drift vector. **§10 catalogued ledger stays 3** (RT-22 / RT-26 / RT-27); RT-22 + RT-26 attributions untouched; no surface becomes "four-layer." **Conditionality.** True under **both** packages: `p_active_only` survives on §3e containment even under Package 2, where it becomes redundant only *for disclosure*.
- **Dependencies.** Owner: Architect (migration) + Sec (wording). No ledger move.

**F6. Land the review checkpoints into the skill files.** [process control]
- **Source.** Close-gate joint review 2026-08-02/03, §18 — the *precedent-without-its-surrounding-conditions* pattern fired **five times** (once endemic in `042`; four agent-authored under review conditions, with the pattern named and in view). Both agents agreed this is the process note worth recording above any individual finding, because it is the only one that predicts *where the next defect comes from* rather than describing where the last one was. **This title deliberately carries NO count.** It said "three" and there are now seven; the set grew because **each new check acquired its own failure mode** (CP5 misses consistency, CP6 misses broken patterns, CP7 misses incomplete ones), and **Sec's position is that the regress does not terminate.** A count in a title is a maintenance liability — the ordinal-label ruling applied to titles rather than to references. Sec's own §16 heading carried the identical defect ("five findings", enumerating six) and was fixed by **removing** the number, not correcting it.
- **AC.** Add the checkpoints to the skill bodies, since a skill is where someone encounters a control at the moment it matters — an ADR is not read at authoring time (the **§7.6 S8** reasoning). **CP1 — precedent surroundings.** *When copying a precedent, read the source's **surroundings** — its table comment, RLS policies, grants, section heading — not only the artifact being lifted. A precedent's conditions live around it, and reading the thing you are copying does not show them to you.* → **`apply-migration`**. **CP2 — constraint over comment.** *Before writing a comment that asserts an invariant, check whether the invariant is **declaratively expressible**. Where it is, the comment is the weaker artifact by definition — this project has already ruled that **code implies enforcement** ([ADR-040](DECISIONS.md#adr-040) B9), so an executable statement that enforces nothing is worse than a comment saying the same thing.* → **`apply-migration`** + **`spawn-sec-joint-review`**. **CP3 — test a principle against its full enumeration.** *Before adopting a principle extracted from a set of instances, test it against the **full enumeration it claims to summarize**, not against the cases that suggested it. A generalization is itself a copy — of the instances it is drawn from — so it inherits CP1's failure mode.* → **`brief-drift-catch`** (the correction was **away from** `apply-migration`: this is verify-before-you-generalize, the same discipline as verify-before-you-relay, and it applies well beyond migrations). **CP4 — absence is not a value.** *For any surface where a consumer distinguishes states, **write the explicit zero**, or make the absent case **structurally distinguishable** from the zero case. Never let a missing row carry meaning: omitting a row where an explicit zero belongs makes "we didn't measure" indistinguishable from "we measured zero" — a **detectability** failure, which is why it lands on audit and forensic surfaces rather than at random.* → **`apply-migration`**. **CP5 — citation sweep.** *Before forwarding any document to ratify, `grep -n` **every ordinal-label prefix the document uses** (`F#`, `S#`, `#N`, `B#`…) and **resolve each occurrence against its intended sequence**, rather than eyeballing the one that was flagged.* → **`brief-drift-catch`**. **CP6 — consistency sweep (body vs summary).** *Extract **every quantitative claim a summary block makes** and resolve it against the body. Run it **on your own artifact after any ruling that moves a number**, not only before forwarding — a document goes stale the moment a ruling is made elsewhere, and nothing prompts a re-read because the file was not edited. **Bodies get read by people following an argument; summaries get read by people checking a number**, so correcting the reasoning and leaving the count is the worst available outcome.* → **`brief-drift-catch`**. **CP7 — adversarial probe on a clean result.** *Any check returning **"clean"** on its first run gets **one probe with a known-present instance — the hardest known variant, not any instance** — before the clean result is believed. A check that fails open reports success while proving nothing, and CP5 and CP6 are themselves greps, so a failing-open sweep **disarms both while reporting success**. **For a completeness sweep, prefer a pattern that over-matches and filter by hand**: over-matching is auditable, **under-matching is silent and its silence reads as absence**.* → **`brief-drift-catch`** **and** **`apply-migration`** — the only checkpoint routing to both, because the same failure occurs at the DDL layer: **a guard that never fires has never been shown to fire**, so a migration-time assertion needs a seeded violator proving it aborts. **CP8 — re-derive, don't re-read.** *After a ratify that changes a mechanism, for each open finding written against it ask **not** "does this still parse?" but **"would this finding's assertion still be TRUE if I derived it fresh from the new mechanism?"** Re-reading confirms a claim is well-formed; **re-deriving confirms it is still true.** The distinction is the whole checkpoint: a relayed claim, an unverified ID, and a restated open item all survive re-reading and all fail re-derivation.* → **`brief-drift-catch`**. **CP8 stands as its own checkpoint — Sec ruled the substance, team-lead ratified the freeze 2026-08-03.** The stopping condition was always a **bar** (cheap, mechanical, leaves a re-runnable artifact), never a **number**; CP8 clears it. **Refusing a genuinely new class to preserve a count would be the count-in-a-title liability this entry exists to strip** — the same principle already applied to this title, to the §7.7 heading, and to the citation convention. **Freeze the bar, not the number.** **Known limit, and it is CP8's own:** re-derivation confirms each claim is **true**; it is **silent on whether two true claims describe the same thing.** See the DEFINER-referent trap (`self209_close_gate.sql`) — authored DEFINER = 3, allowlist = 4, both correct, **and the act of reconciling them is the defect.** **Stopping condition — the set is NOT claimed complete.** A checkpoint qualifies only if it is **cheap, mechanical, and leaves a re-runnable artifact**; the regress does not terminate, so treat seven as the current set, not the closed one. **Governing disposition preference, which is why the set is ALLOWED to be incomplete:** *where a blanket disposition is available, prefer it over an enumerated one — precisely because you cannot trust your own sweep's completeness.* An enumeration covers what the sweep found; a blanket supersession covers **what it never found, by construction.** It is the only move in this sequence that **ends a branch of the regress rather than extending it**, because it removes the dependency on the check being complete instead of improving the check. **Not an eighth checkpoint — it is not a check.**
- **Dependencies.** Owner: **team-lead** (all are skill files; neither Architect nor Sec owns them). No ledger move. Two-way door. **Canonicity caveat — read before building.** The canonical checkpoint text is also slated to land in [ADR-042](DECISIONS.md#adr-042)'s process section via a separate doc-only PR. **If both land, ADR-042 is canonical and this entry should reduce to a reference (Path B — reference, do not restate), exactly as `supabase/CLAUDE.md` treats the §10 ledger.** Transcribed in full here at Sec's explicit direction because a §7 entry must be self-contained enough to promote to Linear verbatim; the duplication is known, not accidental, and this note is the tiebreak rule.

**F7. `pfin.account.currency` is user-writable and silently restates historical NAV — record the event now, mark the affected range later.** [live defect] *(added 2026-08-03, post-[ADR-042](DECISIONS.md#adr-042) ratify)*
- **Source.** [ADR-042](DECISIONS.md#adr-042) Decision 4 (the closed-account half is fenced there; **this is the open-account half, deliberately out of that ADR's scope**). `015:538` adds `currency text not null default 'USD'`; `015:541` records that it *"Feeds the `fn_compute_nav` cash-leg FX multiplier."* `003:124` grants `authenticated` a **table-level** UPDATE on `pfin.account` **with no column list** — which is why it covers columns added later — and `pfin` is Data-API-exposed, so a direct `PATCH /rest/v1/account?account_id=eq.N {"currency":"EUR"}` reaches it without touching `+page.server.ts`. The app's `updateAttributes` Zod schema omits `currency`, but **app-layer is not a control**. Live today, independent of the close gate.
- **AC.** (1) A change to `pfin.account.currency` writes a row to `pfin.account_event` ([ADR-042](DECISIONS.md#adr-042) Decision 5) recording account, actor, `effective_date` **distinct from** `created_at`, and the from/to values — via the `event_type` CHECK-widen path (`030` `transaction_type` precedent; **widening is Sec-joint-review-mandatory**, and the `event_type` and value-vocabulary CHECKs widen **together**). (2) The recording is **NOT conditional on the marking half shipping** — *record now, mark later.* (3) **Marking half — routed as RATIFICATION, not implementation.** [ADR-013](DECISIONS.md#adr-013) D1 declares the **surface** set illustrative, but its governing trigger is *stale-account data*; a restatement is a **different cause**, so extending D1 to it is a larger move than the one D1's own provenance required F/CTO ratification for. **PM/UX define the presentation; F/CTO ratifies the extension. Do not build against D1 as existing authority.** (4) The event must distinguish **"believed then, still believed"** from **"believed then, since corrected"** — a provider restatement and a user's denomination fix currently produce **identical artifacts**, and a trajectory reader cannot tell them apart, or tell that anything happened at all. **Explicitly NOT in scope.** [ADR-040](DECISIONS.md#adr-040) is **working as designed**: a frozen `nav_daily` checkpoint records *what was believed at the time*, and recomputability was deliberately traded away. The permanent frozen-vs-fresh divergence is **not** the defect — **the missing marker is.**
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: `pfin.account_event` must exist ([ADR-042](DECISIONS.md#adr-042), migration `057`) — that is what makes this cheap and is why F/CTO took *record-now*. **Sec gate:** the `event_type` widening. **Not blocked on** the marking half. Owners: Architect (DDL + widen) + Backend (write path) + Sec (joint-review); PM/UX + F/CTO on the marking half per AC (3). **Urgency is a closing window, not a severity adjective.** *The information needed to mark retroactively is destroyed by not recording it now* — with no record of **when** the change happened, the affected range cannot be identified and that history is **permanently unmarkable**. The window closes **per user action, not per calendar day**: accrual is ≈zero today (greenfield, pre-deploy, effectively one user) and becomes real at **public signup**. **Consolidate rather than repeat** — this shares one deadline with [ADR-042](DECISIONS.md#adr-042)'s **`058`→`059` operational-quiescence window** — which exists precisely *because* `closed_at` and `is_active` coexist across it while the operator dispositions each pre-existing inactive account **through the real gated control** (an operator step, not a migration). **State it once as a single pre-public-signup gate**, not as a caveat repeated per finding.

**F8. Stale Decision-3 family counts across merged artifacts — POINTER ONLY; the finding is canonically recorded at [ADR-042](DECISIONS.md#adr-042)'s Consequences, not here.** [pointer — do not restate]
- **Source.** Close-gate joint review 2026-08-03. **This entry deliberately does NOT restate the counts.** The finding — that `046` and [ADR-037](DECISIONS.md#adr-037) D4's superseding bracket carry a stale Decision-3 family count — is **already committed in [ADR-042](DECISIONS.md#adr-042)'s Consequences**, whose own text reads *"This survives nowhere else in committed text — not in §7.7 — so it lives here."* **Two committed homes for one finding will drift and hand the next reader two different counts**, so §7 carries a pointer and nothing more (team-lead ruling, 2026-08-03). Read ADR-042 for the numbers; **do not copy them here, and do not cite a count from this entry.**
- **AC.** Nothing to build. Three routing facts that are *not* in ADR-042 and would otherwise be lost: **(i) `046` carries the count TWICE, with different dispositions.** The `--` line comment is **not a database object**, so no migration can reach it — "correct when `046` is next touched" is the only option, and Architect confirmed a real ride exists (the queued [ADR-039](DECISIONS.md#adr-039) doc-nit about `046`'s `netWorth.ts` claim, which itself has **two sites**: `046:65` header and `046:167` body — *fixing one and missing the other is the shape this review hit repeatedly*). The `comment on function` at **`046:167` IS a database object** and is correctable by a `052`-shape comment-only migration **without editing merged `046` at all** — the same migration carrying the `p_active_only` semantic amendment. **(ii) `046:167` carries three stale claims in one string** — the D3 count, the ADR-039 netWorth nit, and `RT-26 stays 4` / `§10 stays 3`, which want re-checking while someone is in there. **(iii) `supabase/CLAUDE.md`** — **Architect-owned and riding the ADR-042 migration PR, NOT queued here.** It is the only citation in the set that will actively mislead someone tomorrow: every other instance is a dated artifact where the point-in-time defense holds, but this is an **operating doc agents read every session**. Its D3 count, SECURITY DEFINER text and `is_active` conventions all move with ADR-042 together, so a queued follow-up would land after the facts had moved.
- **Dependencies.** Owner: **Architect** (all sites). No ledger move. **Blocked-by:** the [ADR-042](DECISIONS.md#adr-042) four-slice correction landing, since the D3 canonical count moves to 16/13 with it.

**F11. `account_event.reason_code` — open F/CTO ratify; POINTER ONLY, and it blocks `057`.** [pointer — do not restate]
- **Source.** [ADR-042](DECISIONS.md#adr-042) Decision 5 reserved this explicitly — *"Starting vocabulary — PROPOSED, grounded not invented. **PM to confirm, F/CTO to ratify**"* — and it was never ratified. **PM has now confirmed; the F/CTO ratify is outstanding and `057` cannot land without it**, since the migration's `account_event_reason_required` CHECK makes closure impossible without a reason and no trigger can invent one. **The vocabulary and rulings are NOT restated here** — they land as an **ADR-042 Decision 5 amendment** (Architect-authored, F/CTO-ratified), which is where a decision belongs; §7 is a work queue. Reading this entry is not a substitute for reading the amendment.
- **AC.** Nothing to build. Three items carry ratify weight, in ascending order: (1) confirm the vocabulary (one rename, one addition, `other` included); (2) confirm reason is **mandatory** — safe *only because* `other` is included, per ADR-042's own append-only asymmetry; (3) **the axis decision, which is the only irreversible one** — `reason_code` conflates *why the account ended* with *where the value went*, and a second `disposition` column **cannot be added later without being NULL for every prior row** in an append-only table. **The one-way door is COLUMN EXISTENCE, not requiredness** — requiredness is a CHECK and relaxes or tightens freely, so the two must not be decided as one.
- **Dependencies.** Owners: **Architect** (amendment + DDL), **F/CTO** (ratify), **PM** (product framing — done). Blocks `057`. **Urgency is bounded and belongs on the existing gate:** the one-way door's cost is proportional to closures written before the column exists, which today is **≈zero** (greenfield, pre-deploy, effectively one user) and becomes real at **public signup**. **So (1) and (2) unblock `057` now; (3) joins the single pre-public-signup gate already shared with the `currency` finding and the `058`→`059` quiescence window** — state it once there, not as a fourth caveat.

**F10. PRD §2.4.1 / §2.4.2 describe the retired `is_active` column — re-point after `059`.** [PM; **DISCHARGED 2026-08-04** — both passages rewritten in this PR]
> **DISCHARGED.** `059` merged with PR #319, and the re-point landed per the AC below. **Both blockers had to clear, and only one of them was noticed at dispatch:** the `059` half was tracked, while *"blocked on an Architect ruling… whether ADR-042 supersedes or preserves ADR-013 A1–A3 / PM-1"* was **not** carried into the go-ahead — it was found by reading this AC verbatim before drafting, which is the only reason the PRD wording was not guessed at. It turns out **discharged**: ADR-042's own *Preserved, not superseded* section rules it explicitly — *"This ADR preserves ADR-013 A1–A3 (connection-level); it supersedes PM-1's account-level display half by way of the 3-concept model, not by way of the gate."* §2.4.1 now states that split and cites it, so the trace is checkable rather than asserted. **The lesson is about the shape, not this instance:** an entry with two blockers, one of them named only in the AC body, reads as single-blocked from every summary of it. **Downstream and NOT discharged here — `docs/DESIGN/flows/phase-2-flows-2.4-cross-cutting.md` still describes the retired pattern in four places**, including F-2.4.F and the F/CTO-ratified Flag PM-1 resolution (2026-05-27, Option 1) that ADR-042 has now superseded. That file is UX-owned Phase 2 territory, not PM's, and is filed separately rather than swept here.
- *Body removed at the 2026-08-20 §7 hygiene sweep (F/CTO ruling: discharged content is deleted, the PR trail is the record). Verdict + citation retained above; history in the cited PR(s) and the sweep PR.*

**F9. Sweep the `(N items)` counts out of the §7 section headings.** [process control]
- **Source.** Team-lead ruling 2026-08-03, extending Sec's no-counts-in-titles ruling from entry titles to section headings. **§7.7's heading count went stale twice in one day** (6 → 7 → 8) — the argument proving itself faster than it was made. It has been **dropped from §7.7 only**, which leaves §7.7 diverging from its six siblings (§7.1–§7.6 all carry `(N items)`). **A stale count in a heading is worse than a one-section divergence** — the divergence is visible and self-explaining, whereas a stale count reads as authoritative. This entry exists so the inconsistency is **temporary and tracked rather than permanent and unexplained**.
- **AC.** Drop `(N items)` from the §7.1–§7.6 headings, matching §7.7. **Do NOT replace the counts with corrected counts** — that reproduces the liability one cycle later, which is the enumerated-correction regress this section already documents twice (ADR-037 D4's bracket; the checkpoint entry's own title). The list is adjacent to its heading in every case, so anyone needing a count can read one that cannot go stale. **Scope fence:** section headings in §7 only — **not** §5, **not** the dated footer log entries (point-in-time records of what was filed on a given day, the S3 leave-the-record-alone class), and **not** counts that are load-bearing claims rather than inventory labels (e.g. the §10 catalogued-instance ledger, where the count *is* the commitment).

**Strip the §10 ledger count from the catalog comments that assert it, and from the two role briefs.** [process control]

- **Source.** ADR-042 close-gate, 2026-08-04. Architect measured **eleven database objects** whose `comment on ...` asserts *"§10 catalogued ledger stays/unchanged at N"*. Five were re-authored by that branch and stripped there; **six remain**, correct today. Sec's escalation added two more instances **in the role briefs themselves** — RN>`.claude/agents/security-reviewer.md` (renamed to `security-engineer.md` on 2026-08-11; `RN>` per WORKFLOW.md § marker guidance) and `.claude/agents/architect.md` both enumerate the ledger as **RT-22 + RT-26**, i.e. **two**, while [ADR-011](DECISIONS.md#adr-011) Decision 4 has read **three** (RT-22 / RT-26 / **RT-27**) since RT-27 was catalogued 2026-07-19.

- **Why this is not cosmetic.** The stale briefs would have produced a **confidently wrong review**: Architect corrected a catalog comment from `2` to `3`; had Sec cross-checked against their own brief rather than against `DECISIONS.md`, they would have reported the correction as the error. **A role definition reads as authoritative in exactly the way a stale comment does**, and it is consulted by the agent whose job is to catch stale comments. The only thing that prevented it is the standing rule that §10-adjacent review must read Decision 4 **verbatim and live, never from recall** — which paid for the first time that night, on its home surface, against its own reviewer.

- **The ad-hoc-workaround signal.** Three separate parties independently carry a private note that the *"§10 ledger 2"* refrain is stale — Architect, Sec, and the team-lead's own memory. **Three patches over one defect in the shared source** is the argument for fixing the source rather than adding a fourth.

- **AC.** Both classes take the **same disposition, which is the one already ruled for entry titles and section headings**: *point at the canonical enumeration and carry no number.*
  1. **Six remaining catalog comments** — re-emit via a comment-only migration (the `052` shape; never an edit to a merged migration, per the §7.6 S3 scoping guard). **A `comment on ...` is a database object**, so it survives `create or replace` and needs its own re-emit rather than riding a function redefinition.
  2. **Two role briefs** — replace the parenthetical enumeration with a pointer to ADR-011 Decision 4. **Owner: F/CTO** (agent definitions are operating instructions, not a doc fix an agent should make to itself).
  3. **Do NOT substitute the corrected number.** The ledger went 2 → 3 in July and will move again; a corrected count re-arms the identical trap one cycle later. This is the enumerated-correction regress this section documents three times over.

- **Dependencies.** Two-way door. **No ledger change** — the ledger is correct at 3, Decision 4's structure is intact, and nothing in the close-gate altered it. **The defect is entirely in artifacts that quote it.** Sec explicitly scoped this as *not a finding against the ledger*.

- **Instrument caveat, stated because it bounds the AC.** The count of eleven is **Architect's measurement against the live catalog**; Sec noted they could not verify it independently because a source grep measures *emissions* — a `comment on` statement in a migration file — rather than *objects*, and a later migration can supersede an earlier emission on the same object. **The catalog is the only instrument that answers "how many objects assert this today."** Whoever takes this should re-measure against `pg_description` rather than trusting the number above, which is dated to 2026-08-04.

### §7.8 — Connection lifecycle

*One missing capability with four symptoms, surfaced by F/CTO questions during the ADR-042 close-gate review, 2026-08-04. Filed as **one** entry rather than four because they share a single cause: **nothing in the application ever writes `pfin.account.linked_source_id`.** Splitting them would produce four partial fixes to one gap. Verified against `main` @ `e72f652` — measurements are dated and expected to rot; the capability claim is not.*

**Connection lifecycle: disconnect, re-point, and add-accounts-to-an-existing-Item.** [V1 gap; not currently staged anywhere else]

- **Source.** F/CTO, during the ADR-042 close-gate review. Two questions, one cause. ADR-042 Decision 1 records aggregator linkage as concept 1 and states *"No new work; a link/unlink control is explicitly deferred"* — a deferral that was never converted into a staged item, so it survived only as a sentence saying "not now."

- **Measured state (dated — `main` @ `e72f652`).**
  - **`linked_source_id` is never written by any application path.** It is read throughout `api/src`; the only write-adjacent reference is `updateAttributes.server.test.ts:82`, which asserts a posted `linked_source_id` is **rejected** by `.strict()`. There is no control, no server action, and no worker path that changes it.
  - **The DB primitive already exists.** `015`'s matched-tenant fence on `pfin.account.linked_source_id` (Decision-3 canonical instance **#6**) states in its own comment: *"Covers UPDATE (**re-link path**), not just INSERT."* The fence was built for a control that was never built.
  - **Account identity is `(linked_source_id, provider_account_id)`** — the `021` partial UNIQUE index, and `042`'s ON CONFLICT arbiter.
  - **`linked_source` is unique on `(provider, external_connection_id)`** and carries **no uniqueness on `(user, institution)`**. One row per Item; nothing prevents two Items at one institution.
  - **The connect flow always mints a fresh link token.** `/accounts/connect` → `PlaidLinkConnect` → `/api/plaid/link-token`, which takes no existing-connection input. **No institution comparison exists anywhere in the connect path.**
  - **Plaid update mode is already implemented and in production** — `workers/provider-sync/src/http/linkToken.ts` passes an existing Item's `access_token` and omits `products`, per Plaid's update-mode rule. It is wired **only** to re-auth (`/api/reauth/start` → `kind: 'link_update'`).

- **The four symptoms.**
  1. **Cannot disconnect** an account from its aggregator and return it to manual.
  2. **Cannot move an account to a different aggregator** — live under [ADR-027](DECISIONS.md#adr-027)'s ratified Plaid-primary / SimpleFIN-secondary split, where an account may need to change provider.
  3. **A relink duplicates every account.** A new Item is a new `linked_source` row with a new `source_id`, and Plaid issues fresh account ids on a new Item — so **both halves of the identity change at once**, the `021` arbiter cannot match, and a second set of accounts lands beside the originals while the old Item keeps polling.
  4. **Re-selecting a connected institution mints a second billed Item.** Choosing an already-connected institution from *add connection* creates a new Item rather than reusing the existing one. **This has a recurring dollar cost** — Plaid bills per Item — and it is the symptom most likely to be hit repeatedly, because *"go add that account I skipped"* is a normal thing to do.

- **AC.**
  1. **Route symptom 4 through update mode rather than a fresh Link session.** The mechanism exists and is in production; what is missing is the entry point. Plaid update mode with account-selection is the supported flow for *"add accounts to an existing Item"* and creates no new Item and no new billing.
  2. **Detect an existing connection to the chosen institution** before minting a link token, and offer update mode instead. There is no institution comparison in the connect path today.
  3. **A re-point control** that updates `linked_source_id` on an existing account, composing with `015`'s #6 fence rather than bypassing it. **Sec joint-review-mandatory** — it writes a Decision-3 FK-shaped column across an isolation boundary.
  4. **A disconnect path** returning an account to manual (`linked_source_id = NULL`). Note the `021` index is **partial** (`WHERE linked_source_id IS NOT NULL`), so unlinked accounts leave the dedup surface by construction — confirm that is intended rather than incidental before relying on it.
  5. **Symptom 3 needs a re-identification step**, not just a re-point: on a new Item the operator must be able to say *"this existing account is that provider account on this new connection."* Both identity halves change, so no automatic match is available.

- **Dependencies.** Owner: **Architect** (control design + the #6 composition) with **Backend** (connect-flow routing) and **Sec** (joint review on AC 3). **Not blocked by ADR-042** — concept 1 is orthogonal to the closure work, and none of this waits on `059`. Independent of the `toggleAccount` removal in [ADR-042](DECISIONS.md#adr-042) Decision 1b, which governs *which accounts are imported*, not *whether a connection exists*.

- **Standing claim, measurement-independent.** The four symptoms are one capability. **Any fix that addresses fewer than all four leaves the same gap open under a different name** — that is why this is one entry, and why the *"link/unlink control is explicitly deferred"* sentence in ADR-042 should be re-pointed here rather than left as a bare deferral.
- **Dependencies.** Owner: **PM** (`BACKLOG.md`). Two-way door. No ledger move. Trivial, but deliberately booked rather than done inline — it touches six section headings that other artifacts may cite, and it is not close-gate work. **The observation worth preserving, and the reason this family is a pointer rather than an enumeration:** `supabase/CLAUDE.md` **already carries the blanket disposition** — *"read the canonical enumeration in [ADR-011](DECISIONS.md#adr-011) D3 live, **it grows**"* — **in the same sentence as a restated count.** It warns against trusting restated counts while restating one. And [ADR-037](DECISIONS.md#adr-037) D4's bracket is *itself* a correction (it superseded 14/12 with 15/13) that is now **two steps stale** — **the enumerated correction became the next stale citation.** That is the regress demonstrating itself in the artifacts, and it is the concrete case for the *prefer-a-blanket-disposition* preference recorded under **"Land the review checkpoints into the skill files."**

### §7.9 — `is_active` retirement: application-layer landing

*Filed 2026-08-04 because it existed nowhere else. The classification below was performed during the ADR-042 close-gate review and lived only in a message thread — the same **decided-but-not-recorded** failure this review catalogued twice in one evening, on the largest artifact of the day. Measurements dated to `main` @ `681a11b`; the capability claims are stated separately and do not depend on them.*

**Application-layer sites that break when `pfin.account.is_active` drops, and the deploy-skew window.** [V1 gap; owner unassigned at filing]

- **Source.** Backend reachability sweep during the ADR-042 close-gate; deploy-skew surface raised by Sec, declined by Architect as not-theirs, and **left unowned** — *a risk declined by the right person with nobody assigned is indistinguishable from a risk nobody found.*

- **Two distinct problems, deliberately filed together because they share a cause.**

  **(a) Hard breaks at `059`.** Sites naming `is_active` in SQL text or a PostgREST column list. These are not semantic inheritance and not comments — the column is gone and the call fails. Measured behaviour: PostgREST returns **HTTP 400 / `42703`** for a dropped column in both a `select` list and an `eq` filter — an error *object*, not a throw. **The worst instance degrades silently:** the account-count query on the net-worth path returns an error object, the caller's guard treats it as "no accounts", and **every user with a real NAV sees the new-user empty state with nothing surfacing.** That reads as a UX bug days later rather than as a failed migration.

  **(b) Deploy skew.** `059` drops the column atomically, which is safe for the *database*. It says nothing about **application processes still running against the old schema**. This is deployment sequencing — migration-then-deploy ordering, or a release that ships both together — and it is **a different owner from the migration content**.

- **⚠ The classification is the work, and it is not a text match.** A grep for `is_active` across `api/src` and `workers/` returns **10+ files out of 259 searched**, and **they do not all refer to the same column.** At least three columns carry that name: `pfin.account.is_active` (**retired**), `linked_source.is_active` (**preserved** — connection lifecycle, ADR-013 A1–A3), and `user_taxonomy.is_active` (**preserved**). Connection-status files are almost certainly the second. **Whoever takes this must classify by which table each query reaches, not by the token** — this exact conflation fired four separate times during the review, in both directions, including once in a guard-rail written to prevent it.

- **AC.**
  1. **Classify every match by target table.** Text matching is insufficient and has already produced wrong answers in both directions.
  2. **Fix (a) in the same PR as `059`.** Name what breaks if it follows: the nightly NAV cron fails at tenant enumeration; the dashboard shows every user the empty state with no error; accounts list, account detail and both connection surfaces lose their rows.
  3. **Two write paths must stop writing the column *before* `058` merges**, not with `059` — the account-detail toggle and the connections toggle. In the `058`→`059` window one click either aborts the migration (the biconditional assert fails after the operator step has run) or makes NAV count an account whose writes the transfer-in fence refuses.
  4. **A test that stays green while its production query starts failing must move with it** — at least one fully-mocked test asserts the old filter was called and will pass after the real query 400s.
  5. **Comments that go FALSE, not stale**, headed by one that is a *prohibition* rather than a description: a block labelled load-bearing forbids using the as-of path with a past date. The re-point **strikes that constraint**, so post-`059` it forbids the path that just became correct. **A false prohibition is undetectable by construction** — nobody tests the path they were told not to take, so its absence leaves no artifact. The root contract in `api/CLAUDE.md` must be corrected in the same PR, or the leaves get re-seeded from it.

- **Dependencies.** Owner: **Backend** (classification + the app-layer fixes), **DevOps** (deploy sequencing for (b)). Not blocked by anything in ADR-042; (3) is required *earlier* than the rest. **Does not gate `059`'s correctness** — `059` is correct and this changes nothing about it.

- **Standing claim, measurement-independent.** The surface is **non-empty and includes the net-worth path**. Its exact size is **unmeasured at filing** and is the first deliverable, not an assumption to carry forward.
