`closed_at is null` on an unresolvable row asserts "not closed" from no information; fail-open's safety is derivative of the close gate and unnamed as a dependency) **and corrected the framing both agents had wrong: this is NOT "decide the default."** The unresolvable state exists only because `account_select` is owner-only while the holdings legs are `rd_access`-JOIN-scoped — **V2 must reconcile those predicates so the state is unreachable**, with fail-closed as a backstop. **That framing stops the under-report being recorded as an accepted cost**; the **`p_active_only`** entry **loses its disclosure/control-surface argument entirely** (no ignored class exists, so nothing needs disclosing) and survives only as the **ADR-039 semantic amendment** — the *"sound only at `p_as_of = current_date`"* constraint is **STRUCK, not reworded** — carried by one `052`-shape comment-only migration that also fixes `046:167`. **Lesson recorded in the preamble: do not assume a finding survives a ratify just because its subject still exists.** **(ii) NEW — stale Decision-3 counts entered as a POINTER ONLY** per team-lead ruling (b): the finding is canonically committed at ADR-042's Consequences and is **not restated here**, because two homes drift and hand the reader two counts. The entry carries only what ADR-042 lacks — that **`046` holds the count twice with different dispositions** (the `--` comment is unreachable by any migration; **`046:167` is a `comment on function`, a database object**, fixable by a `052`-shape migration without editing merged `046`), that **`046:167` carries three stale claims in one string**, and that **`supabase/CLAUDE.md` is Architect-owned and rides the ADR-042 migration PR rather than queueing here** — it is the one citation that will actively mislead someone tomorrow, since every other instance is a dated artifact where the point-in-time defense holds. **(iii) The section heading no longer carries an item count**, extending Sec's no-counts-in-titles ruling from entry titles to the heading — it went stale twice in one day. **(iv) CP5 self-check caught three stray `F#` citations in this file** — including inside the note that declares the cite-by-title convention — and a **CP5 catch on an incoming disposition**: `059` was cited as the slice dropping `is_active`, but ADR-042 **as merged** listed only `056`/`057`/`058`. **Resolution (Architect, confirmed): the citation was ahead of the artifact, not wrong.** Sec's two-phase adjudication split the last slice **after** ADR-042 merged, so `059` is real and the ADR's own header and build sequence were stale — corrected to four slices in the same pass. **`058` leaves `is_active` untouched; the column drops at `059`**, and the `058`→`059` quiescence window is genuine (both columns coexist while the operator dispositions accounts through the gate). Numbers now pinned. **The interim phrasing — "the slice that drops `is_active` (`058` per the ADR's own slice list)" — was verifiable-today and correct-under-either-answer, and is the pattern to reuse for anything transcribed before its underlying file settles.** **The flag was the right hook precisely because the mismatch was an ordering artifact rather than an error**, which is the case for flagging assumptions rather than resolving them silently in either direction. **(v) NEW — §7 heading-count sweep booked** as its own entry, per team-lead: §7.7's heading count went stale twice in one day, so it is dropped there and the six sibling sections are booked for the same treatment rather than left permanently divergent.] [2026-08-03 (follow-up 3) — **Sec corrections after the §7.7 batch merged (PR #299).** **(i) TIMELINE SPLIT on the V2-grantee entry — the correction that matters most in this section.** Sec had described the fail-closed backstop as *"the third obligation"* on the B5 hard-gate; **it is not a gate obligation at all.** It is a **V1 DDL requirement shipping in `059`, now** — the legible conjunct `acc2.account_id is not null and acc2.closed_at is null`, commented so removing it visibly flips the default. **Filed as a third V2 obligation it reads as deferrable to the sharing PR, and deferring it means `059` ships WITHOUT the conjunct — the exact silent flip the ruling exists to prevent. A misfiled timeline would have defeated the finding.** Correct shape: **two** gate obligations at V2 (`rd_access` withheld · reconcile the `account_select`-vs-`rd_access`-JOIN mismatch) **plus a rider** (if the reconcile is not fully discharged, the fail-closed path must become **observable**), **plus one V1 shipping requirement in `059`.** **Naming rather than numbering is what exposed it** — *ordinals conceal heterogeneity*, so three items in a numbered list read as three of the same kind when one shipped on a different timeline entirely. **(ii) CP8 added — "re-derive, don't re-read."** *After a ratify that changes a mechanism, ask not "does this still parse?" but "would this assertion still be TRUE if I derived it fresh from the new mechanism?"* Routed to `brief-drift-catch`. Sec ruled it real on the substance: **the freeze was on a bar (cheap, mechanical, re-runnable artifact), not on a number** — refusing a genuinely new class to preserve a count is the count-in-a-title liability this very entry strips. **Team-lead ratified the eighth 2026-08-03** — *"freeze the bar, not the number"*; the stopping condition was always a bar (cheap, mechanical, re-runnable artifact) and CP8 clears it. **CP8's own limit recorded with it:** re-derivation confirms each claim is true and is **silent on whether two true claims describe the same thing** — the DEFINER-referent trap (authored = 3, allowlist = 4, **both correct, and reconciling them is the defect**) is the case no checkpoint reaches, because it contains no wrong claim anywhere. **(iii) The ratify-survival lesson sharpened, and narrowed.** Of the three dispositions only **INVERSION** is a new class: *discharged* is not a failure mode (good news worth noticing so nobody redoes the work), and *kept-its-subject-lost-its-argument* is the already-named **requirement-outliving-its-precondition** variant — **do not double-file it.** Inversion is novel because **the words stayed true and the behaviour reversed**, and under V1 both forms are behaviourally indistinguishable, so **the flip leaves no footprint**. **(iv) NEW — the PRD §2.4.1 / §2.4.2 re-point booked as BLOCKED-BY `059`** (team-lead ratified sequencing 2026-08-03). Both passages describe the retired column; §2.4.1's *"unless §2.4.2's inactive-flag pattern covers the use case"* clause gets **rewritten, not deleted**, because ADR-042 **relocated** that capability (aggregator-side selection at connect time) rather than withdrawing it. **The PRD must not describe an end-state the schema has not reached** — a requirements doc naming a column that does not exist yet is worse than one naming a column about to be retired: the second is known-stale, the first is simply false. **Booked rather than held in conversation because "wait" is the disposition that gets lost.**] [2026-08-03 (follow-up 4) — **§7.7 F5 disposition change + a CP6 catch inside the same entry** (Architect, riding the ADR-042 Amendment 2 migration PR). **(i)** The `p_active_only` comment **moves off the `052`-shape file and onto `059`**, which re-points `fn_compute_nav`'s body and must re-emit the `comment on function` explicitly regardless — splitting body from documentation across two migrations opens a window where the catalog describes behaviour the function no longer has. **Recorded with the correction that produced it:** the move was first justified as *"`059` rewrites the comment anyway"*, which is **false** — `create or replace` **preserves** comments, exactly as this section's own ledger-count-strip entry already states. **The disposition survived a premise that did not**, and the corrected reason is the stronger one. Same pass corrected a second over-claim of mine: the `052`-shape file does **not** shrink to `046:167` alone — the count-strip entry has six catalog comments booked onto it, so it loses one rider, not all but one. **(ii) CP6 catch:** F5's Disposition rules the disclosure/control-surface argument **GONE** while its **AC still instructs the migration to record that struck sentence into a catalog comment**, where it would read as live state — *the executable half was the stale half.* Flagged in place, **not rewritten**: the replacement wording is Sec's per F5's own Dependencies line. **(iii)** F5 now also carries what `059`'s comment must state — why a provably-no-op `p_active_only` nonetheless stays, what would make it load-bearing again (its four named dependencies), and the `>` vs `>=` boundary as invariant-dependent rather than arbitrary.] [2026-08-12 — **Option C component-history ratify placed** (F/CTO 2026-08-12; companion [ADR-054](DECISIONS.md#adr-054) lands in the same PR, Status Proposed — its Decision 5 carries TWO OPEN questions closing at the doc-PR review): §7.1 gains **A9** (NAV component-checkpoint capture substrate, capture-only, per-account leaf per ADR-054 Decision 3); **§7.1 heading count dropped** — this PR falsified "8 items", executing that section's slice of the §7-heading-count sweep booked at the 2026-08-03 follow-up 2 note; §5.1 gains the V2 subcomponent-visualization + incumbent-sheet-history-backfill deferral; **§7.13 added** — CoA / hierarchical-accounts design question (PM product framing + Architect schema-direction framing, F/CTO-directed 2026-08-12). No live Linear IDs in any added text.] [2026-08-12 — **§7.14 added** (SELF-217/218 build-day follow-ups: `053` positivity CHECK with Sec's four binding conditions incl. the NaN/Infinity-additive rule; scratch-harness ACL parity; TBC fence widening; `067` cost magnitude; `062` series-semantics doc candidate; `workers/etl` packaging). A9's Decision-5 citations CLOSED in the same window (ADR-054 Accepted at the PR #444 double gate).] Edit via `/start-doc-update <slug>` per ADR-009 Decision 9.*

### §7.10 — Cron-liveness branch harness (Sec condition on PR #340)

*Filed 2026-08-09 as Sec's stated condition on the A1 scheduled-dep-audit PR. **Filed here rather than as a PR-body follow-up number, because those are not trackers** — the standing rule after a follow-up "#N" was mistaken for a Linear ID.*

**The `scheduled-audit-liveness` job's failure branches have no committed harness — they were exercised once, at authoring.** [V1 gap; owner DevOps, Sec reviews non-vacuity]

- **Source.** Sec's merge condition on PR #340 (`scheduled-audit-liveness` in `.github/workflows/security-scan.yml`). PR #340's own body records the branches as *"exercised, three via a `gh` shim for states the live API will not produce on demand"* — **exercised at authoring, not committed.** Nothing re-runs them.

- **⚠ What is already covered, so this entry is not re-specified later as bigger than it is.** The **parser self-test is committed and runs on every invocation** — a case table over `status_of()` asserting parsed values for malformed and well-formed HTTP status lines. That half is done and needs nothing. **The gap is the FATAL branches** (404-with-repo-reachable, unregistered-workflow-with-no-history, unexpected-HTTP, state-not-active, run-history-lookup-failure, and the parser-self-test failure path itself).

- **⚠ THE HARNESS MUST ASSERT MESSAGES, NOT EXIT CODES — this is the whole point of the entry, and the job already explains why in its own body:**

  > *"This job has many branches that all exit 1. Against a fail-closed instrument a broken probe produces a false NEGATIVE that is invisible in the probe's own result: every malformed input still fails, so the probe looks like it works. The fence's best property is exactly what hides a broken harness. Asserting `"exit 1"` would therefore verify nothing here — only the parsed value discriminates."*

  A harness that asserts *"the job exited 1"* against a job whose branches all exit 1 is the **fence-that-cannot-fail** shape, arriving through the test instead of the fence. It would read GREEN in a coverage review while distinguishing nothing.

- **Why this is worth building at all, given the job is small.** This job exists because **a cron can stop existing without failing** — public-repo scheduled workflows auto-disable after 60 days of inactivity, and scheduled runs can be dropped under load. The liveness job is the observer for that. **An observer that has silently broken is worse than no observer**, because its silence is indistinguishable from health — which is the same argument that justified the job, applied one level up to the job itself.

- **Scope discipline — do NOT extend this to A1's own audit branches.** A1 (`scheduled-dep-audit.yml`) already carries its own inversion probe, which builds a throwaway tree pinned to a known-vulnerable package and asserts the same command exits non-zero. That probe is committed, runs every invocation, and was **validated in production on 2026-08-09** when A1's first scheduled run caught a real advisory. Widening this entry to cover it would duplicate working coverage.

- **AC.**
  1. **A committed harness that reaches every FATAL branch and asserts on its MESSAGE**, not on its exit status. Enumerate the branches by measurement at build time rather than from this entry — `grep -c 'echo "FATAL' ` over the job block — because a count written here goes stale silently and this file is a derived surface.
  2. **Prove the harness can fail.** For each branch, show the assertion RED when that branch's message is altered or its guard removed. **Reproduce the failure when authoring; do not port a green result** — the RT-31 leg (i) standard, which is the closest precedent in this repo.
  3. **The three branches reachable only via a `gh` shim stay shim-driven**, and the shim is committed with the harness. A branch that cannot be reached in CI is a branch with no coverage, regardless of how it was checked once.
  4. **The harness must itself be non-vacuous.** If it produces an empty branch list it must fail loudly, never pass — the same trap `(i5)`/`(T0)` guard elsewhere: an empty offender list is produced both by a healthy tree and by an instrument that has stopped matching.
  5. **Do not assert the parser self-test's cases a second time.** They are already committed; re-asserting them creates a second home for one claim.

- **Not blocking.** A1 is validated end to end as of 2026-08-09 and its own probe is committed. This hardens the observer, and the failure it guards against is silent degradation over months rather than anything live today.

### §7.11 — Open findings from the 2026-08-11 verification-discipline session

*Filed 2026-08-11. **These are RECORDED, not worked** — the session that produced them consumed itself on process at the expense of product, and the correct response was to write them down and stop. Each entry states the finding and its routing; none is investigated further here. **The heading carries no count**, per the ruling that a count in a §7 heading goes stale on the first append.*

*⚠ **The "deliberately not duplicated here" carve-out is VOID — the items it excluded are in §7.12 below.** It read: the `app_id` blind spot, `053`'s first-of-month grain hole, the sweep buckets, the rejected `marker_basis` option, S22, `test_staging_update.py`'s unqualified `drop table` and `db-tests.yml` never going red live in `MILESTONES.md` "Pending", and a second home would drift. **`## Pending` was removed on 2026-08-12** — no purpose, no size bound, no owner, ~90% of an auto-load that was being truncated to nothing. There is no first home left for them to drift from.*

**`067` — the catalog-comment completion.** [pointer]
- **Source.** Sec's concession during the `066` review, after initially blocking it. `066`'s **catalog** comment names the GRANT and the ACL and is **silent on the COMMENT**, though the DROP takes both — the same omission corrected in `066`'s header, surviving in the copy a `\d+` reader with no repo actually sees.
- **Disposition.** A **dedicated** `052`-shape comment-only migration. **Ride-along was considered and rejected**: the trigger for a ride-along is plausibly the very migration the sentence warns, so waiting gates the fix on the failure it prevents.
- **Tracked in Linear.** Recorded here only so it is visible from the repo. Owner: **Architect**. Routing: **Sec** (catalog surface on a function feeding financial figures).

**§10 catalogued instances — does a catalogued instance require an OPERATING assertion, or is an enforcement MECHANISM sufficient?** [ruling needed]
- **Source.** Architect, from the six-week dead-hook finding. Academic **today** — every catalogued fence lives in GitHub Actions, which self-reports by construction, goes red per PR, and gates on branch protection. **But that is a property of where those fences happen to live, not of anything [ADR-011](DECISIONS.md#adr-011) Decision 4 requires.** Nothing stops a future catalogued instance being attributed to a layer that cannot report it operates — at which point the layer attribution is an unverified claim, and §10's defense-in-depth argument rests on layers being real.
- **Routing.** **Sec joint-review-mandatory; F/CTO ruling needed.** Recorded, deliberately **not answered** — it is a Decision 4 scope question and answering it in a backlog entry would be the wrong surface.

**`guard-logic-matrix` — a CI watcher for the shared-path commit guard.** [needs F/CTO ratify]
- **Source.** DevOps, ruled **for**: the harness is hermetic and deterministic, and fails **2/6** against a guard-less `pre-commit`, which is what makes a green run mean anything. Cost: one required context (**17 → 18**).
- **⚠ THE NAMING CONDITION IS THE LOAD-BEARING PART, and it must survive into whatever ships.** The job proves the guard's **LOGIC** is intact. It does **NOT** prove the guard is **ARMED** anywhere — armed-ness is untestable in CI. A check named or read as *"the guard is protecting us"* would **recreate the green-while-dead failure one level up**, which is the exact defect the harness exists to answer.
- Owner: **DevOps**. Required-context changes are F/CTO's ratify.

**`WORKFLOW.md`'s self-declared version has stopped tracking `CHANGELOG.md`.** [refinement]
- **Source.** Architect, found while landing the verification rules. `WORKFLOW.md:4` reads `Current version: v1.44`; `CHANGELOG.md`'s newest entry is `v1.191`.
- **The finding is not the gap, it is the silence.** Either the header is stale by a wide margin or the convention was retired — **and nothing in the repo says which.** **A version field nobody watches is the same shape as a fence nobody armed**: a self-description that stopped tracking reality while still reading as authoritative.
- **Not fixed at discovery, deliberately** — the correct next number lives in `CHANGELOG.md`, and asserting one would have invented a version the series passed long ago. Owner: **team-lead** (CoS-owned surface).

**Five artifacts describe the pre-commit hook set at five different accuracies.** [refinement]
- **Source.** Architect + DevOps, from the dead-hook finding. As merged, **only `ruff` and `hadolint` are wired**; `svelte-check` and `eslint` appear in `.husky/pre-commit` **only inside comments** deferring them until SvelteKit scaffolds, and lint/test/type-check are absent.
- **The spread:** [`MILESTONES.md`](MILESTONES.md) is exact (*"ruff + hadolint; JS deferred to Phase 6"*). [`WORKFLOW.md`](WORKFLOW.md) lists the **intended** set and is silent on what is live. `BACKLOG.md`, [`CHANGELOG.md`](CHANGELOG.md) and **`.claude/agents/devops.md`** all describe a lint/test/type-check/svelte-check set that does not exist.
- **⚠ The role-definition instance matters most: it regenerates the error through the agent that owns the surface.** A wrong description in a doc misleads a reader once; a wrong description in an agent definition is re-asserted every time that agent works.
- **Adjacent, not duplicate:** §7.6 S4's Dependencies line already carries this correction *scoped to S4's own sizing*. This entry is the artifact-spread; S4's is the dependency. Owner: **team-lead** to route (spans DevOps + doc surfaces).

**Two authoring rules produced by this session that have no home yet.** [refinement]
- **(i) A citation must state the RELATION it asserts — contains / governs / is-an-instance-of / is-adjacent-to — not merely the target's name.** The generalisable result of the ADR-011 Decision 1 sweep: *"ADR-011 Decision 1"* attached to a `security invoker` read path and survived review precisely because a bare citation asserts nothing checkable. A named relation is falsifiable; a bare name is not.
- **(ii) The no-count discipline needs its boundary stated, because the rule as written invites the error in both directions.** A count over a **growing** list is a maintenance obligation and gets dropped. A count over a **closed, bounded historical measurement** carries no obligation and is **load-bearing evidence** — *"14 sample files were counted as 14 active hooks"* is a **plausible** number, which is why the wrong answer was believed; stripping it removes the part that explains the failure. **Two agents over-applied the discipline in opposite directions on the same day**, which is the argument that the boundary belongs in the rule rather than in the reader.
- Owner: **team-lead** to route to a home (candidates: `brief-drift-catch`, `WORKFLOW.md` Verification discipline). **Recorded here so they are not lost between skills.**

**⚠ The `063`-applied contradiction — REPO-SETTLED, DB-UNVERIFIED, and the surviving claim is auto-loaded.** [defect · verification gap]
- **Source.** Two blocks of the removed `## Pending` disagreed. One asserted `063` was **never applied** and the local DB's max migration was `062`; the other asserted `063`–`066` applied, with a row in `pfin.cpi_u_nonpublication`, proven under a real `pfin_etl` login.
- **⚠ A deletion is a decision even when the reasoning was neutral.** #437's body says the contradiction was flagged rather than adjudicated — **that is true of the reasoning and false of the outcome.** The never-applied block was routed DELETE as stale session narrative; the other side's claim survives as the words *"the substrate is complete and measured"* in `MILESTONES.md` `## Active Feature` — which is **above the autoload cutoff**, so it is injected into every session, main and subagent alike.
- **Repo half, settled** (measured 2026-08-12 in the working tree): `supabase/migrations/` holds `063`–`066`, max `066`, 66 files. **The "local max is `062`" claim is false about the repo.** The `v1.190` `## Recent activity` entry — a dated record — independently states the chain was applied and measured under a real login.
- **DB half, NOT settled.** Nothing here reaches `psql`. **Do not assert it in either direction.**
- **What closes it.** Under the real login: `select max(version) from supabase_migrations.schema_migrations;` and `select count(*) from pfin.cpi_u_nonpublication;` — Backend's to run, not Architect's.
- **Why it is worth five minutes.** The cost is asymmetric. If applied, nothing happens. If not, the **auto-loaded** head tells every future session the substrate is ready — and **SELF-218, the named next deliverable, is the first consumer of exactly that substrate.**

**⚠ `AUTOLOAD-CUTOFF` detects DELETION and is blind to DISPLACEMENT — a fence whose stated bound is wrong.** [defect]
- **Source.** Architect, on its own work in #436/#437. The hook warns loudly when **both** anchors are gone — the marker and the `## Pending` heading. That covers deletion, which is the failure that had just happened.
- **The failure it cannot see is the likelier one.** Insert a section above the marker and the head silently grows; move the marker up, or add a section between `## Recent activity` and it, and **the head silently shrinks.** Both anchors are present, the grep finds one, no warning fires — **and a truncated head is byte-indistinguishable from a correct one at the receiving end.**
- **⚠ Why this ranks above a missing fence: it reads as covered.** The marker's own text says *"Moving this line changes what every session sees"*, which is a request for care — and this repo's standing rule is that **a guard depending on care is decoration.** A guard was written against the failure just seen rather than the failure most likely next.
- **Checkable fix shape** (not implemented, deliberately out of the dispatched scope): have the hook assert the emission contains its expected `## ` headings and warn when one is absent. That is decidable inside the hook and fires on displacement in **either** direction.

**Three `## Pending` blocks were deleted on a container-level "already recorded" check.** [verification gap]
- **Source.** Architect, on its own routing in #437. The 13-PR Phase 3 streak summary, the four memory-codification batches, and the two WHAT-LANDED blocks were deleted as redundant. **What was verified was that the containers exist** — `CHANGELOG.md` carries `v1.37`–`v1.48`, `v1.187`, `v1.189`; `MEMORY.md` indexes the memories. **No entry was opened to confirm it holds the content the block held.**
- ⚠ **The routing file said "spot-check two entries before deleting". That spot-check never happened before the delete landed** — the instruction was written and then not executed by its own author.
- **What settles it.** Open `CHANGELOG.md` `v1.37` and `v1.48` and compare against the deleted block, recoverable at `git show 05103fd:MILESTONES.md`. Recoverable content, so this is a cost-to-recover rather than a loss — but **"already recorded" is doing load-bearing work in a merged PR body and is weaker than it reads.**

**§7.11's carve-out note promises §7.12 for an item that went elsewhere.** [defect]
- **Source.** Architect, on its own edit in #437. §7.11's preamble now says the seven previously-excluded items *"are in §7.12 below"*. **Six are. The `app_id` blind spot is not** — its constraint was routed into `.github/required-contexts.tsv`'s expected-posture block, which is the better home, and the sentence promising §7.12 was never adjusted.
- **What settles it.** One sentence in §7.11 naming the manifest as `app_id`'s destination.
- **Why it is worth recording rather than shrugging at:** it is the exact class the removal was fixing — **a pointer that was true when drafted and went false because of a decision made later in the same PR.**

**⚠ "Run hook index 2" is written into two merged PR bodies, and an index is a position, not an identity.** [defect]
- **Source.** Architect. #437 and #438 both instruct the reader to run `.claude/settings.json` hook **index 2** to verify the auto-loaded head. **It was index 1 this morning** and moved when #435 landed a hook ahead of it.
- **The next hook added at the front breaks the instruction silently:** the reader measures the `core.hooksPath` arming hook, which **correctly emits nothing**, and concludes the ledger hook is broken. That is exactly the wrong turn taken on 2026-08-12 — and it is now documented as if it were the fix.
- **What settles it.** Select the hook by its `statusMessage` (the one naming the MILESTONES head) rather than by position. Nothing needs changing in the merged files; the next person writing that check simply must not copy the phrasing.

**Three smaller items from the same handoff.** [pointer]
- **ADR-027's content is unverified.** §7.12's relocation entry says the aggregator pivot *"belongs in ADR-027, which exists"*. **What was measured is a string match — the anchor appears 44 times in `DECISIONS.md`, which says nothing about what it states.** Read it before acting on the entry.
- **The `## Pending` heading now carries a suffix** (`— RETIRED, awaiting three rulings`). The hook's fallback anchor is `^## Pending`, so it still matches by prefix. **Reformat that heading to start differently and the fallback goes silent** — harmless while the marker stands, which is why two anchors exist, but it is a dependency nobody would guess.
- **⚠ Four other agents' surfaces were edited on 2026-08-12 and none of those owners has seen the text:** `BACKLOG.md` §7 (PM), `.github/required-contexts.tsv` and `scripts/verify-shared-path-guard.sh` (DevOps), `workers/etl/src/pfin_back_etl/utils.py` (Backend). All were inside the dispatched bounds and all are flagged in the PR bodies. **If any of those agents is spawned next, that text is the first thing to show them.**

### §7.14 — SELF-217 / SELF-218 build-day follow-ups (2026-08-12)

*Recorded per the recorded-unless-runtime-effect rule; cited by title; no counts in this heading. Sources: the two Sec joint-reviews (GREEN verdicts on `4260c18` and `b728ad6`), Architect/QA/Backend session findings. Detail lives in PRs #443 / #445 and the review threads.*

**`053` positivity CHECK on `pfin.cpi_u_index.cpi_value`.** [✅ DISCHARGED 2026-08-29 — promoted to Linear as SELF-343 (created that day; contrary to one ledger phrasing, no promotion had happened at the recalibration) and shipped at PR #584 / migration `095`, Sec joint-review GREEN at `9609c12` with all four conditions verified verbatim + the guard widened to bar +Infinity (Sec-accepted as already-mandated by condition (1)). The three-battery break `095` induced (`067`/`071`/`073` poison fixtures) was Sec-caught and repaired per the corrupt-the-control shape without deleting a watcher. Entry retained for its Widened and Dependent-list sub-bullets, which remain standing constraints on BOTH finiteness fences. Falsified-premise doc comments left by `095` are booked at §7.30.]
- **Source.** Sec joint-review of SELF-218/`067` (2026-08-12): `053`'s CHECK is finiteness-only, so a poisoned `0`/negative/NaN/Infinity print reaches the deflator; `067` guards internally (both CPI legs strictly positive else NULL) but the base table holds no fence of its own.
- **AC (Sec's four conditions, binding).** (1) Predicate is **finite AND strictly positive** on the column — `> 0` alone re-admits NaN and Infinity (`NaN > 0` and `Infinity > 0` are both TRUE in Postgres numeric ordering). (2) **ADDITIVE** to `cpi_u_index_value_finite`, which must survive by name — never a replacement. (3) The same PR (or an earlier standalone one — Sec: guard hardening is **no-later-than** the CHECK PR, earlier is better) `create or replace`s `067`'s guard with an explicit NaN clause AND **re-issues its `comment on`** (create-or-replace preserves comments — the DIVISION SAFETY paragraph would otherwise describe a guard the function no longer has). (4) The paired QA leg uses the **corrupt-the-control idiom** (drop the constraint in a savepoint, seed the poison, assert NULL, roll back) — once the CHECK holds, the poison is un-seedable, and unreachable-by-construction is a reason to KEEP a leg, not skip it (`067`'s header rule).
- **Dependencies.** None upstream; Sec joint-review mandatory (financial-correctness constraint on the divisor of an inflation-adjusted figure).
- **Widened (Sec, SELF-221 joint-review 2026-08-13): the additive-never-replacement requirement now names BOTH fences.** `054` carries `nav_daily_value_finite` (NaN/±Infinity bar on `nav_value`), and it is what makes `071`'s `v_a_nav > 0` guard complete — same mechanism as `053`-behind-`067` (`NaN > 0` is TRUE). Two `> 0` guards in this family are sound only because a finiteness CHECK stands behind each; any future change to EITHER finiteness CHECK inherits condition (2) verbatim — additive, survives by name, never a replacement.
- **Dependent list extended (Sec, §2.1.4 backend joint-review 2026-08-14): `073 fn_nav_reference_dates` is the THIRD dependent of `053`'s `cpi_u_index_value_finite`** (after `067` and `071`) — its strictly-positive CPI guards are NaN/Infinity guards only because `053` bars them upstream. Any change to that CHECK now touches three consumers. **Extended again (Sec, SELF-255 joint-review 2026-08-31): `096 fn_historical_expenditures` is the FOURTH dependent** — same division of labour, documented in `096`'s own header; any change to EITHER finiteness CHECK now touches four consumers on the CPI side.

**Scratch-harness ACL parity (permissive-direction hazard).** [QA + DevOps] [✅ FIX HALF DISCHARGED 2026-09-03 at PR #594 (merge `e2034c4`) — the template-DB tooling mirrors the bootstrap in both directions: parity verified against the LIVE bootstrapped DB (Sec's own baseline probe: `pfin` relacl byte-identical across 39 relations; the 2026-08-12 NULL-`proacl` symptom does not reproduce), and the hazard DEMONSTRATED by inversion probe (a `--no-privileges` build drops 85 GRANT/REVOKE lines; the censuses + diff live at `scripts/fixtures/` as the regression fixture). Recipe: `docs/db-template.md`. Retained for the companion USAGE-vs-EXECUTE mechanism note below, which remains a standing observation.]
- A scratch DB built with `pg_dump --no-privileges` loses the bootstrap's **REVOKEs as well as its grants** — measured 2026-08-12: `auth.uid()`'s `proacl` was NULL, EXECUTE defaulted to PUBLIC, making the harness MORE permissive than production, where denial assertions pass vacuously. Fix: mirror the bootstrap in both directions (grants present AND revokes present) in any scratch-verification harness; companion mechanism on record: schema `USAGE` is parse-time — RLS policy quals invoke by OID and need only EXECUTE, while test-typed `auth.uid()` needs `USAGE`, which is why product and test code diverge on the same harness.

**TBC fence scope widening over the `workers/etl` runner files.** [DevOps; fence-boundary change → F/CTO ratify + Sec review]
- Sec (SELF-217 review, measured): the TenantBoundConnection CI fence's production scope is `workers/etl/src/` and EXCLUDES the five root-level runner files, including the one SELF-217 added — no live violation, but the gap sits over exactly the hand-written-one-off class. Widen to `workers/etl/` minus `tests/`, with a paired inversion fixture.

**`.gitignore` + logging hygiene follow-through.** [DevOps]
- `*.log` added to `.gitignore` in this PR (no tracked `.log` existed); the SELF-217 importer already writes its full-figure run log inside the ignored `temp/nav-history/`. Residual: the log path is **cwd-relative** and `logging.FileHandler` raises if the directory is absent — recorded in the seeding-run preconditions on the Linear issue.

**`067` daily-grain cost magnitude post-import.** [Architect + PM]
- The imported decade moves `067`'s per-point helper loop from a handful of points to thousands at `'daily'` grain (~100× per request). Security property unchanged (the clamp still bounds to caller-owned rows) — this is an availability/product question. The per-distinct-month optimization is explicitly parked in `067`'s header with its revisit condition; the related staleness-rendering disposition rides the chart-UI issue per [ADR-053](DECISIONS.md#adr-053) Decision 7.

**`062` series-semantics documentation candidate.** [Architect; comment-only migration shape if adopted]
- The two landed-body properties that silently broke a carefully-built fixture — `062` never emits a period-end after the caller's newest checkpoint, and `'daily'` emits per calendar day, not per checkpoint — are now stated in the `067` battery's header where a fixture author will find them. Whether `062`'s own catalog comment should also carry them is open; if adopted, it is a `052`-shape comment-only migration.

**`workers/etl` packaging shape.** [Backend + DevOps]
- `pyproject.toml` has no `[build-system]` table and no `[tool.uv] package = true`, so `uv sync` treats the project as a dependency container and never installs `src/pfin_back_etl`; imports are invocation-dependent (`uv run --with-editable .` or `PYTHONPATH`), which cost one CI red on PR #445 (fixed file-locally with a location-derived bootstrap). Decide the durable packaging shape once, with the CI invocation as the reference environment.

### §7.15 — Phase-7 deploy-gate bookings (re-homed assertions)

*Assertions whose binding point is the Phase-7 deploy pass, re-homed from issue ACs so they survive issue closure. Tag convention `[PHASE-7 DEPLOY GATE]` per the §7.6 precedent; gates that arose as Sec follow-ups keep their §7.6 homes — one home per gate, no copies.*

**NAV-chart render-latency assertion (§2.1.2.d chart AC5 re-home).** [PHASE-7 DEPLOY GATE]
- **Source.** F/CTO ratify 2026-08-12 — Option A of the AC5 re-anchor. The original criterion pinned its measurement to Hetzner cax21, which is reference-only infrastructure per [ADR-021](DECISIONS.md#adr-021) greenfield (not the V1 deploy target); **the anchor moved, the budgets did not.** The chart issue's AC5 carries the matching re-homed text; this entry is the home that survives that issue's closure.
- **AC.** Measured on the **V1 deployment environment**, before the [PRD §2.1.2](docs/PRD/index.html#story-2-1-2) chart surface is declared deploy-complete: monthly-60-month default render within **800ms p95**; daily-90-day render within **1.5s p95**. The budgets are the ratified user-experience numbers and are **not re-litigatable here** — a miss on the deploy environment routes to F/CTO as a product decision (relax the budget vs optimize), never a silent re-anchor. QA's non-binding local-dev measurement (recorded at issue review) is early-warning evidence, not the binding measurement.
- **Dependencies.** **Blocked-by:** Phase 7 arrival — per ADR-021 greenfield the deployment environment does not exist until deploy, so there is nothing to measure before it. Owners: QA (measurement) + DevOps (environment) + PM (budget custody). **Deploy blocker for the chart surface, not a merge blocker** (the ratified deploy-blocker-not-merge-blocker shape).

**NAV-delta-panel latency assertion (§2.1.3.a backend AC6 re-home).** [PHASE-7 DEPLOY GATE]
- **Source.** F/CTO ratify 2026-08-13 — AC6 of the multi-horizon NAV-delta-panel reconciled AC set. The drafted criterion carried no measurement anchor (an unanchored latency number is not a testable acceptance criterion); re-homed per this section's ratified pattern. **The budget did not move — only the anchor.** The panel issue's AC6 carries the matching re-homed text; this entry is the home that survives that issue's closure.
- **AC.** Measured on the **V1 deployment environment**, before the [PRD §2.1.3](docs/PRD/index.html#story-2-1-3) panel surface is declared deploy-complete: `pfin.fn_nav_delta_panel()` returns within **200ms p95**. The budget is the ratified user-experience number and is **not re-litigatable here** — a miss on the deploy environment routes to F/CTO as a product decision (relax the budget vs optimize), never a silent re-anchor. QA's non-binding local-dev measurement (recorded at issue review) is early-warning evidence, not the binding measurement.
- **Dependencies.** **Blocked-by:** Phase 7 arrival — per [ADR-021](DECISIONS.md#adr-021) greenfield the deployment environment does not exist until deploy. Owners: QA (measurement) + DevOps (environment) + PM (budget custody). **Deploy blocker for the panel surface, not a merge blocker.**

### §7.16 — §2.1.3.b / §2.1.4 build follow-ups (2026-08-14)

*Recorded per the recorded-unless-runtime-effect rule; cited by title; no counts in this heading. Sources: the two Sec joint-reviews of this build day (072 AMBER→GREEN at `649ce8d`; 073 GREEN at `354e69f` carried to `cb7653f`), Architect/QA session findings. Detail lives in PRs #452 / #453 / #455 / #456 and the review threads.*

**`071` battery leak-canary subject change (family consistency).** [QA; battery-only]
- **Source.** Sec flag on both the 072 review (booked) and the 073 review (fixed on-branch for 073): a corrupt-the-control leg that probes `pfin.nav_daily` directly proves the policy fences the *table*, not that the *function's* read is fenced by nothing but that policy — it cannot catch a future redundant local `users_id` predicate (the `062`-measured hazard: such a predicate keeps a cross-tenant battery green over a policy broken open). `073`'s battery now aims its canary at the function itself; **`071`'s merged battery is the only family member still probing the table.**
- **AC (Sec's catch criterion, transcribed).** Change `071` `(LEAK1)`'s subject to a call of `pfin.fn_nav_delta_panel()` inside the broken-policy savepoint, keeping a fail-closed `ok(<val> is not null and <val> <> <A's own value>)` negative-predicate form (bare `isnt()` is IS DISTINCT FROM and passes on NULL — measured). The deterministic function-level assertion Sec sketched on the 072 review (anchor_checkpoint_date on the month row under sabotage) is an acceptable alternative shape.
- **Dependencies.** None; battery file is freely editable (not applied history). Pairs naturally with the next battery-touching PR on that surface.

**`072` header/catalog indexical defect (the "here" that re-points on copy).** [Architect; catalog half needs a `052`-shape comment-only migration]
- **Source.** Architect self-report 2026-08-14: `071`'s header sentence "struck for the FOURTH time in this family (049 R2 / 051 / 067 / here)" was carried verbatim into `072`, where the indexical "here" re-points — the enumeration silently omits `071` while still claiming "fourth". The catalog comment carries the same defect in shorter form. **New arrival route for the count-drift class: a stable count whose ENUMERATION moved because it contained an indexical — verbatim carry, the discipline that protects everything else, is what broke it.** `073` already avoids inheriting it (cites the enumeration, no ordinal).
- **AC.** File-header `--` half: correctable in place under the apply-migration in-place conditions at the next touch. Catalog half: has a database representation — fold the correction into the NEXT comment-touching migration on `pfin.fn_nav_delta_panel` (the `052` regenerate-and-diff shape) rather than spending a migration on it alone. Replacement shape: enumerate instances or cite `071`'s header; no ordinal.
- **Dependencies.** Rides the next `072`-object comment migration; no urgency (no runtime effect; the defect is a wrong count in prose).

**Scratch-DB recipe caveat: `pg_dump --schema-only` carries no role definitions.** [QA; runbook/DESIGN.md line]
- **Source.** Sec's C5 equivalence ruling on the 072 review: the schema-dump scratch recipe is sound on the SAME cluster (real `authenticated`/`anon`/`service_role` roles exist), but against a DIFFERENT cluster the ACL legs go vacuous or error — the dump carries no roles or memberships. Companion to §7.14's scratch-harness ACL-parity entry (that one is about `--no-privileges` dropping REVOKEs; this one is about roles not travelling at all).
- **AC.** One line in the QA battery-design doc (`tests/rls/DESIGN.md` when its §7.6 S9 entry lands, or wherever the scratch recipe is next written down): same-cluster only, or create the roles first.

**Battery-design lessons from this build day (candidates for the S9 `DESIGN.md` batch).** [QA]
- **pgTAP `isnt()` is fail-open on NULL** (IS DISTINCT FROM; `isnt(NULL, x)` passes) while `ok()` fails on NULL — a negative assertion over a subquery needs an explicit NULL guard, proven in three states (empty must fail / not-yet-detected must fail / detected must pass). Measured twice this session.
- **A suite whose fixtures derive from the run day needs legs exercised at synthetic run days** — the 073 battery's first draft carried a leg red for five months of the year, green only because it was August; the fix (override `fn_server_today` in scratch across 24+ dates incl. every month-end, then restore) needs no `p_as_of` parameter and does not touch the ratified no-client-clock design.
- **Fixture-vs-fixture disagreements are informative**: every Architect-vs-QA "contradiction" this session resolved into two different fixtures (seeded-vs-clean DB, pinned-vs-arrears CPI coverage, hardcoded-vs-file-built), not two readings of one fact — reproduce the OTHER party's exact fixture before contradicting their measurement.

The F/CTO checkpoint must be RE-CONFIRMED at execution — the 2026-08-15 in-principle authorization was mooted by the Option-B ruling. Source/AC retained above for provenance; not executable from this entry.

**Recovery-scope note (no action):** `pfin.account` / `pfin.linked_source` / `pfin.user_settings` remain empty by design — `seed.sql` never covered them and the incident's recovery scope was users/taxonomy/CPI/NAV only. ### §7.19 — PRD + ARCH recalibration pass against the post-GL architecture (2026-08-16)

*Source: F/CTO direction 2026-08-16, verbatim: "In the future, we will need to recalibrate the items from the PRD, because we added the GL feature set and architecture partway through the design and *after* the PRD and ARCH docs were created." §7.6 framing note (1) applies — capture here; this is a standing hygiene item, not a milestone booking.*

**Systematic sweep of PRD §2 stories + ARCH sections against the GL-era schema — an amendment list per doc, not per-issue firefighting.** [PM lead; Architect co-owner for ARCH + design-conflict classification; owning agent per routed item]

- **Source.** The PRD and `docs/ARCH/index.html` were authored before the double-entry GL feature set landed (migrations ~030–039: transaction-type vocab/trade constraints, reclass_history, lot_match, journal, basis-adjust constraints, fn_gl_entries, GL completion, manual-trans/split write, stock-split entry) and before the account_trans/journal/lot_match substrate those migrations define. The measured symptom trail — four schema-impossible drafted-AC instances to date (SELF-223; SELF-229; SELF-228's `p_scope` reconciliation; SELF-234's supersession at the 2026-08-16 Option-C ruling) plus the five-issue V1.2 `p_users_id`/`p_scope` signature family caught in the 2026-08-15 PM reconciliation — is one pattern, not five accidents: **drafts written faithfully against doc text whose schema premises the GL era invalidated.** Per-issue AC-amendment passes fix instances; this entry retires the generator.
- **AC.**
  1. **PRD sweep** [PM]: every §2 story checked against the GL-era schema (migrations listing at execution time, read live — not the ~030–039 snapshot above). Output: a per-story amendment list, each conflict classified as (a) *text amendment* (PRD wording updated, no design change), (b) *ADR-worthy design conflict* (doc and schema genuinely disagree — routed to Architect for an options memo, F/CTO disposes), or (c) *already-superseded* (a later ADR/ratify resolved it — the PRD text gets the pointer, no re-litigation).
  2. **ARCH sweep** [Architect]: same triad classification over `docs/ARCH/index.html` sections against the same live schema baseline.
  3. **Drafted-but-unstarted issue ACs** [PM]: every AC in Linear-Backlog issues and §7 staged entries that copies a schema identifier is re-verified against DDL (the copied-not-composed rule) or marked SIGNATURE-PROVISIONAL; the sweep's baseline sha is recorded in the amendment list.
  4. **Routing discharged, not just listed**: each (a) lands as a doc amendment by its owning agent; each (b) gets an ADR-flag with owner named; each (c) gets its pointer edit. The pass is complete when the amendment list is empty or every residual is owned.
  5. Amendment lists are tracked artifacts (not `temp/`), one per doc, carrying the baseline sha.
- **Dependencies.** None blocking. **Standing trigger points:** (i) before each milestone-rotation promotion (the §7→Linear promotion inherits the sweep of the entries it promotes — AC 3 runs at minimum); (ii) before any PRD-anchored parity claim is asserted in a ratify or ship-gate. A full pass (AC 1+2) is F/CTO-scheduled; the trigger-point minimum (AC 3) is not optional at those boundaries.
- **Known pre-sweep finding (2026-08-26 securities-edit sitting), queued for AC 1 — a second dead end, on the cash path:** **a spurious cash transaction cannot be removed in V1** — no delete/remove/reverse-only action exists; reverse-and-replace is a correction primitive (always writes a replacement; replacement must move non-zero money), so an accidental duplicate entry has no removal path, only repurposing into a transaction the user does mean to record. Structurally the same shape as the securities dead end the sitting ruled on. The closing **void** primitive (reverse-only, no replacement) is teed up as its own ratify item at the securities-edit close-out doc PR (staged at §7.29); this bullet remains so the AC-1 sweep checks §2.4.3's removal language against whatever F/CTO rules. Sitting record: [`docs/records/self340-securities-edit-options.md`](docs/records/self340-securities-edit-options.md).
- **Known pre-sweep finding (2026-08-26 securities-edit sitting), queued for AC 1:** **V1 has no way to record a sale of anything** — no manual-sale writer exists (the manual purchase path is BUY-only) and `lot_match` is write-dormant until M4-GL. Bigger than the sitting that surfaced it; the AC-1 sweep should check §2.4.3 / §2.4.5 (and any story implying disposal) against the actual `account_trans` writer set, and GL-substrate milestone scoping should confirm the manual-sale writer's home (it is a named dependency of §7.3 G3). Sitting record: [`docs/records/self340-securities-edit-options.md`](docs/records/self340-securities-edit-options.md).

### §7.22 — post-SELF-233/328 follow-ups (2026-08-17)

*Source: the SELF-233 and SELF-328 Sec joint reviews (PRs #488/#489). Three items, each with a named owner; none blocking; each returns to Sec per its own trigger.*

**1. `z.coerce.number()` on FK-shaped ID fields → strict typed parse.** [Backend] Sec-measured at the SELF-233 review: `z.coerce.number()` accepts `true`→1, `[5]`→5, `"0x10"`→16, `"5e2"`→500, `" 7 "`→7 — so in one `.strict()` schema `target_percent` rejects scientific notation by name while `sub_cat_id` accepts `"5e2"`. Pre-existing house pattern, SEVEN sites (schemas/planning-target.ts ×1, classification.ts ×2, transaction.ts ×4 — re-grep at fix time, don't trust this enumeration). No cross-tenant path (the 074 #17 trigger and RLS hold), but Lock-14 mod #1's literal text is "strict typed-input validation." Fix shape (Sec): `z.number().int().positive()` (no coerce), or an explicit string branch `z.string().regex(/^\d+$/).transform(Number)` where a form posts strings — a positive pin, not a widened coercion. One PR, all sites, Sec joint-review (Lock-14 surface).

**2. GOLDEN3 — in-file proof that FENCE1c bites.** [QA, at the next `078`-battery touch] Sec flag from the SELF-328 review, deliberately NOT a condition ("satisfying my conditions ends a review"). Shape supplied: a second savepoint-scoped mutation deriving from live prosrc — `regexp_replace(v_def, 'case ep\.source', 'CASE ep.source', 'g')` — asserting count(kernel_block)=2 while FENCE1a stays 3 and FENCE1b stays 1; completes the per-leg mutation matrix GOLDEN2 started and guards the "simplify count(kernel_block) to count(*)" rot path.

**3. RT-23 acceptance-text reconciliation to the post-ADR-056 shape.** [Sec — their doc] The RT-23 catalog entry's numeric-battery acceptance text still specifies a CURRENCY regex (`/^\$?[0-9]{1,12}(,[0-9]{3})*(\.[0-9]{1,2})?$/` — accepts `$` and thousands commas) for a surface that post-ADR-056 is `numeric(5,2)` percent with no currency field; the implementation is stricter and correct, but an auditor comparing build-vs-text reads a false gap. Sec drafts the replacement (offered at the SELF-233 review); lands as a SECURITY doc edit with its own diff-verify.
