### §7.24 — GL-split ([ADR-058](DECISIONS.md#adr-058)) landing bookings (2026-08-18)

*Source: the F/CTO-directed taxonomy-vs-GL structural brainstorm (2026-08-18) and its Sec touchpoint, as recorded in [ADR-058](DECISIONS.md#adr-058). Each item is a finding that ADR produced and does not itself discharge; **the ADR's own follow-up inventory is the authoritative list** and this section is its §7 half. Numbering here is §7-local: the ADR inventory's items 4–8 map to items 1–5 below in order, and its item 3 (GL-native P&L) books to **§5.3** instead. Items the ADR deliberately does NOT book here: the rename PR's PM riders (they travel with that PR), the `009` stale `comment on table` correction (it rides this work's first migration, which re-emits that comment anyway), and the two build-spec open items (recorded inside the ADR itself). Closure annotations for **§7.13** and **§5.7** travel in the same landing PR.*

**1. The posting-template / `ledger_account` construct.** [decision item; Architect → its own ADR; Sec joint-review MANDATORY]
- **Source.** [ADR-058](DECISIONS.md#adr-058) Decision 1's ratified scope, F/CTO verbatim 2026-08-18: *"split only: follow on for template construction"*. `pfin.posting_prototype` is the **home** this construct will attach to; ADR-058 builds the home and not the construct.
- **AC.** Its own ADR carrying template header + legs + amount rules, with its own options round. It also carries the **`ledger_account` global-only** commitment — ADR-058 records the short answer (commit global-only permanently) as **out of scope and explicitly unruled by Sec**, and it must be **decided before that table ships**. ⚠ **A template that computes amounts is a small rules engine in the money path**, so Sec joint-review travels *with* the proposal rather than following it. Design inputs to carry in, named so they are not re-derived: ADR-058 Decision 8's two don't-foreclose constraints (a prototype must be able to name accounts that are **not permanent**; nothing may complicate `fn_gl_entries`' as-of parameterization), and the **short-position classification rule**, which ADR-058 settles **with** this ADR rather than before it.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: the split migration (the table must exist) **and item 2 below, which is upstream of any template DDL**. Downstream: GL-native P&L (**§5.3**, V2).

**2. [ADR-031](DECISIONS.md#adr-031) Decision 3's event-class-vs-flow-class partition amendment.** [decision item; F/CTO ratify + Sec joint-review; **GATES the template stream**]
- **Source.** [ADR-058](DECISIONS.md#adr-058) Decision 1: making the Cat the authoritative class source for *every* row — rather than only for the single `standard` single-contra branch — would amend a **Sec-conditioned ratified decision** ([ADR-031](DECISIONS.md#adr-031) Decision 3 as amended, Amendment 1 item 2, which carries Sec Condition A binding on M1-evt). ADR-058 does not re-open it and **nothing in ADR-058 presumes its outcome**.
- **AC.** An F/CTO ratify pass with Sec joint-review, deciding whether the Category becomes the authoritative accounting-class source for every row or stays confined to the `standard` branch. ⚠ **There is NO MIGRATION for this to ride** — it is not a schema change, so it has no natural vehicle and will not surface on its own; it must be scheduled as its own decision item or it will be discovered by a template author assuming an answer. **It gates the template stream: it is upstream of any posting-template DDL**, because a template design that assumes the Cat is authoritative for every row has already presumed this outcome.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: none — decidable today, ahead of all three migrations. Downstream: item 1 cannot author DDL ahead of it.

**3. Totals-equality watcher at the `fn_subcat_market_value` / `fn_compute_nav` seam.** [QA + Architect]
- **Source.** [ADR-058](DECISIONS.md#adr-058) Decision 3, consequences 3–5: the binding contract at the storage-taxonomy/GL seam is `Σ fn_subcat_market_value(as_of, include_real_estate := true) = fn_compute_nav(as_of)` (`076:38–40`), and it is **asserted and only HALF-WATCHED** — valuation *method* parity is watched structurally (`078` leg (S1); `079` V1–V3), but **no battery compares a `fn_subcat_market_value` total to a `fn_compute_nav` scalar**.
- **AC.** A battery leg asserting the totals equality. ⚠ **The named failure class is COVERAGE DIVERGENCE, not method divergence** — a row set and a denominator derived from different predicates, under-summing on a financial surface, failing **OPEN**. ADR-058 recommends it because the split, the `element` column and the rename each create a fresh coverage-divergence opportunity and this is the one seam all three land on. **Wrinkle to encode rather than to report later as a defect:** since the assets-only ruling the **rendered** §2.2.2 table deliberately does *not* foot to NAV (Liabilities are excluded from both row set and denominator; NAV includes them) — the invariant belongs to `fn_subcat_market_value` **as a whole**, not to what §2.2.2 renders.
- **Dependencies.** None — buildable today, and worth more standing *before* the three migrations than after.

**4. No repo-wide `relrowsecurity` watcher over `pfin` (Sec F8).** [DevOps + QA]
- **Source.** Sec touchpoint 2026-08-18, finding **F8**, routed to BACKLOG by Sec explicitly **not** as a gate on [ADR-058](DECISIONS.md#adr-058). `025`'s battery is per-table behavioural, not an enumerative sweep, so **a new `pfin` table shipped with a missing policy or a missing aal2 clause would be caught by review or not at all**.
- **AC.** A catalog sweep asserting `relrowsecurity` across every `pfin` relation, with an **explicit allowlist** for any relation deliberately exempt.
- **Dependencies.** None. ⚠ Its absence is *why* ADR-058 Decision 1 makes `posting_prototype`'s structural battery legs (the `074` S1/S2/S3a/S3b model) **non-optional** — those per-table legs are standing in for a watcher that does not exist, and building this one does not retire them.

**5. `080:174`'s `select distinct ut.users_id from pfin.user_taxonomy` becomes the wrong user set post-split (Sec F10).** [Backend / Architect, at the next seed delta]
- **Source.** Sec touchpoint 2026-08-18, finding **F10**. That derivation is [ADR-057](DECISIONS.md#adr-057)'s ratified backfill user-set shape; after the split it names the **storage-side** user set, so any future **prototype-side** seed backfill reaching for it delivers to the wrong population.
- **AC.** The next prototype-side seed delta derives its user set from `pfin.posting_prototype`, not from `pfin.user_taxonomy`; ADR-057's by-construction reasoning is re-applied per table rather than copied across tables. ⚠ **It fails CLOSED** — a user simply misses a category — which is the direction that does not announce itself, **and it is the idiom the next seed PR will copy**. That is why it is booked rather than left to the reviewer.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: the split migration. Not actionable before it.

---

*Extension 2026-08-19 — bookings produced by the RENAME PR (#502), not by the ADR. ⚠ **Items 6–11 are NOT ADR-inventory items** and are deliberately numbered after the block above so the preamble's mapping (the ADR inventory's items 4–8 → items 1–5) stays true. Sources are the Sec joint-review record for that PR, F/CTO's ratify of 2026-08-19, and Architect's and Backend's authoring measurements. The working documents those came from live in a gitignored directory and do not survive; that is why the substance is transcribed here rather than pointed at.*

**6. [ADR-058](DECISIONS.md#adr-058) Amendment 1 — TWO corrections to Decision 7, and it does not exist yet.** [Architect; rides the split PR]
- **Source.** (i) F/CTO ratify 2026-08-19, the amended equality predicate; (ii) Sec joint-review finding **B3** on PR #502.
- **AC.** One appended Amendment 1 to ADR-058 carrying **both** corrections, so D7 is not amended twice:
  - **(i) The equality-assertion predicate.** D7's literal *"the database's asset-domain Cat set equals `CAT_GROUP_ORDER` exactly"* is **unsatisfiable as written** — the DB asset set has six members and `CAT_GROUP_ORDER` has five, because `Real Estate` is excluded by design (`076` excludes it at `p_include_real_estate := false`; `nonReAllocation.ts` filters it independently). Found independently by Architect and Backend. F/CTO's ratified replacement, transcribed: *"DB asset-Cat set MINUS `'Real Estate'` equals `CAT_GROUP_ORDER`, both directions, exclusion named and justified inside the assertion itself"* — a visible constant, never a silent filter. ⚠ **The decision to ship the assertion is UNCHANGED; only its stated predicate was loose.**
  - **(ii) The "all 11 others" figure.** `grep -c` counts LINES. Per occurrence: `028` = 3, `035` = 6, `037` = 6 → **15**, not 11. ⚠ **D7 itself warns, about the PRD count, that `grep -c` counts lines — and then does not apply the rule to its own migrations measurement.** `082`'s header inherited the figure faithfully and is corrected there; **fixing only the copy leaves the source still minting it.** The separation CLAIM is unaffected — Sec verified it per occurrence (`035:220/228/247/255`, `037:310/318/334/342`); nothing asset-domain hides in the 11-vs-15 gap. `041` is where the defect hides, because there lines and occurrences coincide, so the one file a reader spot-checks agrees with itself.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: none — writable today. ⚠ **Sec's N3 warning, adapted: Amendment 1 does not exist yet and now carries BOTH corrections, so if the split PR slips, TWO known-wrong statements sit in the canonical record rather than one.** This is the entry on this list whose cost grows with delay.
- *[Annotation 2026-08-19 — **CLOSED.** [ADR-058](DECISIONS.md#adr-058) **Amendment 1** is appended and carries BOTH corrections, landing with the split PR (migration `084`) as this entry specified. Item 1's replacement predicate is transcribed from F/CTO's ratify verbatim; item 2's per-occurrence figures were **re-measured** at authoring rather than carried from this entry — `028` 3, `035` 6, `037` 6 — and agree with what is booked here. ⚠ Two collisions are recorded in the amendment so a later reader does not conflate them: `041`'s lines and occurrences coincide at 15, which is why the discrepancy hid; and `041`'s asset-domain figure (15) equals the corrected non-`041` occurrence total (15) by coincidence, counting different things.]*

**7. Venue env-var rename — `QA_SELF238_*` → venue-generic.** [QA + Backend; own commit, announced]
- **Source.** Architect decision at PR #502, taken and stated rather than deferred silently.
- **AC.** Rename `QA_SELF238_POSTGREST_URL` / `QA_SELF238_JWT_SECRET` to venue-generic names (e.g. `QA_PGRST_URL` / `QA_PGRST_JWT_SECRET`) across **both** server test files — `nonReAllocation.tenant-isolation.server.test.ts` and `nonReAllocation.catGroupOrderEquality.server.test.ts` — in its own commit, **announced to QA before landing**.
- **Why it was NOT done in the rename PR.** These vars configure a **throwaway Postgres+PostgREST venue**, not a fixture belonging to the issue they are named after; they are the established convention and one pair drives both tests. ⚠ Renaming them **changes an operator-facing procedure QA already has configured**, so doing it inside a PR about a different rename means a QA run can fail for a reason unrelated to what is under review. The misnomer has no runtime effect. **Mitigation already landed:** the new test file's header states the vars name a VENUE, so the name stops hardening while this booking waits.
- ⚠ **Rejected shape, recorded so it is not re-proposed:** venue-generic names with the old pair as a fallback — two sources of truth for one venue, and the fallback branch would be untested wherever the new names are set.
- **Dependencies.** None.

**8. `054`'s membership watcher re-shapes to EFFECTIVE PRIVILEGE — plus the live dual-grantor state it is currently detecting.** [QA + DevOps; F/CTO authorization for the remediation half]
- **Source.** Sec joint-review finding **F1** on PR #502, verbatim on the ruling: *"Urgency: LOW. Routing: book it; do not gate the rename on it."*
- **The measurement, taken on F/CTO's dev cluster and not relayed.** `pfin_etl` holds **each of its two ratified memberships TWICE, under two grantors** (`postgres` and `supabase_admin`) — four `pg_auth_members` rows where two are ratified. `054`'s legs `(h12)` and `(h18)` are RED locally because of it. Resolved against a **fresh** run after a report discrepancy Sec surfaced: **RED = `(h12)` / `(h14b)` / `(h18)`; GREEN = `(h13)` / `(h14a)` / `(h15)` / `(h16)` / `(h17)`.** `(h14b)` is that file's own documented EXPECTED-DIFFERENT-LOCALLY half and is unrelated. ⚠ **The NEVER-EXCUSED half `(h14a)` (`rolcanlogin = f`) is GREEN** — the role is inert, which is the leg that bounds the blast radius.
- **AC, two halves.**
  - **(a) The durable fix (Sec's).** Re-shape the assertion from *raw membership rows* to *effective privilege* (`pg_has_role` / the effective ACL set), so a benign duplicate grant cannot redden it. Sec's rationale, transcribed: duplicate grants add **no privilege** — they are idempotent in effect — and *"the real cost is watcher habituation: a leg that is permanently RED on F/CTO's cluster and GREEN in CI is a leg that gets excused, and the next time it goes red for a real reason nobody will look."*
  - **(b) The remediation.** Clear the duplicate grants with a **per-grantor** `REVOKE … GRANTED BY <grantor>` — never a `string_agg(distinct …)`-shaped tolerance in the leg, which would make the assertion permanently excuse the drift it just caught. ⚠ **Touches role grants on the dev cluster: F/CTO authorization required before anyone runs it.**
- ⚠ **Not PR-caused** — reproduces identically on a `001..081` control stack. **CI is unaffected** (fresh cluster). **LOW urgency; not a ship gate.**
- *[Annotation 2026-08-19, from the split PR (`084`) — **the fresh-cluster claim above is CORROBORATED, not falsified**, and this annotation exists because it was briefly reported as falsified. Recorded in full because the retraction is the reusable part.* **What happened:** a full pgTAP run against a scratch database carrying `001..084` reproduced these three legs RED (25, 28–29), and a `001..083` control with unpatched tests reproduced them **identically** — so they are confirmed **not** `084`-caused, which is the half that stands. **They were then reported as evidence that "CI is unaffected (fresh cluster)" was wrong. That inference was incorrect.** ⚠ **The scratch recipe builds a fresh DATABASE on the SHARED CLUSTER, and `pg_auth_members` is a CLUSTER-LEVEL catalog** — role memberships do not live in a database and do not travel in a per-database dump, which is the very reason the recipe puts the scratch on the same cluster (so the real `authenticated` / `anon` / `service_role` roles exist). Measured at the time of writing: `pfin_etl` holds **four** membership rows — `authenticated` and `service_role`, each granted twice, by `supabase_admin` and by `postgres`. **Every scratch DB on that cluster inherits that state, so no scratch on it can ever serve as a control for a cluster-level assertion.** CI spins a genuinely fresh *cluster* and the `DB tests (pgTAP RLS battery)` lane was verified **green on `main` at `9e895a4`** on the same day, which is exactly what this entry predicts. ⚠ **One nearby observation eliminated so it is not later mistaken for flakiness in these legs:** the same lane shows a `failure` on `main` at `386a65b`, three minutes earlier — inspected, and it is a Docker **port-bind collision** (`failed to bind host port for 0.0.0.0:54322: address already in use`) from two runs racing, not a test result. **Method rule this yields, which is the durable output:** *a scratch database is a control for schema-level and data-level claims only; for anything reading `pg_auth_members`, `pg_roles`, or grantor identity, the control has to be a fresh CLUSTER, and a same-cluster scratch will silently reproduce the very drift it is supposed to be isolating.* Resolution of these three legs stays with **(a) the effective-privilege re-shape** above — that work has to understand the failure in order to re-shape the assertion, and this annotation narrows what it must explain: not "why does CI differ from local" but "why does a cluster-level duplicate grant redden a leg that measures privilege."]*
- **Dependencies.** None. (a) and (b) are independent; (a) is worth doing even if (b) never runs.

**9. Occurrence sweeps must state their quoting coverage AND verify the path exists.** [method guidance; Architect + whoever authors the next sweep]
- **Source.** Architect flag at PR #502, **closed for this rename by Backend's re-sweep** — booked as guidance, not as open work on the rename.
- **What happened, and it is two failure modes rather than one.** ADR-058 Decision 7 records consumer reach as *"`workers` **0**"* and *"`web/src` **0**"*, both from a **single-quoted** `'Equity'` grep.
  - **`workers`** — Python source uses double quotes. Three `== "Equity"` comparisons exist in the folded ETL. Re-measured: all three bind to `pfin.asset_cat`, a table **absent from V1** (no `create table` for it anywhere in `supabase/migrations/`; the only bare occurrences there are prose references to the incumbent). **Verdict unchanged — NOT AFFECTED — but for the reason *"different system"*, not *"wrong label"*, and the measurement had never been exercised against Python's quoting.**
  - **`web/src`** — Backend's finding, verified independently against the tree: **that directory does not exist**, having been folded into `api/src` earlier. ⚠ **A sweep over a path that does not exist returns 0 and reads exactly like evidence of absence.** Backend covered what "web/src" now means with a **case-insensitive** re-sweep across all of `api/src`, which found nothing beyond the files already in the PR.
- **AC.** A sweep that reports a count states (i) its **quoting coverage** and (ii) that its **paths resolve**. A `0` from an unresolved path is not a finding.
- **Dependencies.** None.

**10. Split-PR sweep riders — stale `'Equity'` fixture rows and one stale comment.** [QA + Backend; rides the split PR]
- **Source.** Sec joint-review findings **F2** and **N2** on PR #502, both explicitly non-blocking there.
- **AC.**
  - The `('asset','Equity', …)` **fixture rows** still seeded by the `076` / `077` / `078` / `080` batteries. These are **user-authored rows, not seeded ones**, so nothing breaks today and no leg is wrong — but they carry a retired label into every future read of those files.
  - `api/src/lib/server/queries/subcatMarketValue.ts:12` — *"§2.2.3 is a pure Equity drill-down"*. Comment only, no runtime effect, no fail-open. Sec's reason for recording it, transcribed: *"removing this exact ambiguity from the vocabulary is Decision 7's stated purpose, and the split/`element` PRs will be read against it."*
- **Dependencies.** Rides the split PR's sweep. Not urgent alone.

**11. The `fn_gl_entries` reachability finding has a TRACKED home — do not re-derive it.** [pointer entry; no work]
- **Source.** Sec joint-review finding **B1** on PR #502 (the AMBER blocking condition), discharged as header text.
- **Where it lives.** `supabase/migrations/082_taxonomy_rename_marketable_securities.sql`, the **ONE DERIVED OUTPUT DOES CHANGE** block. Tracked, versioned, and read by exactly the person who needs it.
- **The substance, in one line so this entry is searchable.** `fn_gl_entries` joins `pfin.user_taxonomy` with **no domain predicate** (`037:225` annotation, `037:349` split-child), and `023` / `029` each record in their own DOMAIN NOTE that matched-DOMAIN is **app-layer only** in V1 — so an asset-domain row is a permissible `sub_cat_id` target at the DB layer, and the rename moved its emitted contra from the Equity branch to Suspense. **Fail-safe in this direction.** ⚠ **The reusable half is the REVERSE direction: an asset Cat renamed INTO a cashflow-class name would traverse the identical unfenced join and mis-post SILENTLY.** [ADR-058](DECISIONS.md#adr-058) Decision 1 closes it structurally once `023` / `029` re-target to `pfin.posting_prototype`.
- **AC.** None — this entry exists so the analysis is findable from the backlog after the review document is gone. **Per Sec, no battery leg is owed in the rename PR;** any leg belongs with the split PR, where the hazard is resolved by construction rather than narrowed.
- **Dependencies.** None.

---

*Extension 2026-08-19 — a booking produced by the SPLIT PR (migration `084`), not by the ADR and not by the rename. ⚠ Numbered after the blocks above on the same convention: item 12 is NOT an ADR-inventory item, and the preamble's mapping (the ADR inventory's items 4–8 → items 1–5) stays true.*

**12. Sec F2's four row counts RE-RUN at the first populated instance — a precondition on the incumbent import, not a discharged fact.** [Architect + Backend; precondition, gates the import]
- **Source.** Sec touchpoint 2026-08-18, finding **F2**, as discharged at migration `084` (2026-08-19). The counts were run before authoring, as F2 required, and **all four came back zero with all four denominators also zero** — every referent table was empty, because no production-shaped V1 dataset exists (greenfield; no V1 deployment; the incumbent is reference-only and is not queried). F2's own wording asks for *"production-shaped data rather than a fresh stack"*, and with respect to those four tables the most-populated V1 instance in existence **is** a fresh stack.
- **AC.** Before the first import that populates `account_trans_annotation`, `account_trans_split`, `user_asset_category` or `account_trans_annotation_history`, **re-run all four counts with F2's predicates**, record them with their venue, and apply F2's rule unchanged: **any non-zero count gets an F/CTO disposition first.** ⚠ **Counts 1–3 fail CLOSED** — every live FK is `on delete restrict`, so a violation aborts rather than corrupts — which means the risk `084` faced was never retired by its zero, only **deferred to whichever instance first has rows**. ⚠ **Count 4 fails SILENTLY**: nothing constrains it, and a non-zero result there is what would make [ADR-058](DECISIONS.md#adr-058) Decision 2's id-preservation invariant demonstrably load-bearing rather than structurally argued. That invariant is ratified on the hazard's structure and **remains empirically undemonstrated** — supported by neither `084`'s zero-over-empty nor by anything else yet.
- **Why booked rather than left in the migration header.** `084`'s header carries the counts, their venue, their vacuity and this re-run condition. **A precondition that lives only in a migration header is discharged by whoever does not read it** — and the reader who needs this one is writing an importer, not reading a year-old migration.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: none. Triggered by the first import that lands rows in any of the four tables.

**13. `085`'s battery TAP tail carries no REF and no CONSUMER.** [QA; rides their next edit to `supabase/tests/rls/085_taxonomy_element_rls.sql` — next-touch rider, not standalone work]
- **Source.** Sec's merge-gate remediation (A) on the ELEMENT PR (PR #510) asked for the raw TAP tail as an observation settling the plan-drift question. It is pasted in BLOCK C **unlabelled**. QA authored a REF/CONSUMER label after the commit had landed, and it named the sha *before* the correction the file itself carries — the sound-quote/false-gloss shape — so it was not committed. Sec's requirement is satisfied by PR #510's body, which names both; this entry is durability — the body is not in the tree; the file is.
- **AC (four clauses; texts per the Architect/QA exchange of 2026-08-20).** At the next edit to that file, label the tail with: **(1) REF** — the sha the run exercised; **(2) CONSUMER** — containerized `pg_prove` with image tag, never bare `psql`; **(3) a note that the annotation postdates the run it describes** — any label added in a later commit is necessarily about an earlier ref, and saying so is what keeps it honest rather than merely accurate; **(4) paste a tail's SHAPE only** (plan line, `ok` sequence, trailing `#` comment, `Result: PASS`) **and elide description text ON PURPOSE, every time** — a description pasted into the file it describes goes stale the instant that description is next edited; the `085` tail was safe **by accident** (its elision fell before the point where `(B5)`'s old and new wordings diverge, author-confirmed elision-for-width, not intent). A truncated tail is evidence of the shape, not of exact strings — do not over-read it.
- **Dependencies.** None. Do not open a commit for it alone.
- *[Annotation 2026-08-20, from the SELF-242 arc — one more thing to settle at that same next-touch edit: an AGGREGATE `pg_prove` run over the full `/tests` tree reported this file as "planned 19 tests but ran 18" while still printing `Result: PASS`; the isolated run of the same file shows 19/19 with every `ok` line present. Observed independently by QA and Backend the same day, on different scratch builds; unexplained — possibly a TAP-parser artifact of the tail's deliberate truncation (clause 4 above), which would make it THIS ITEM's own elision biting its consumer. The reader at risk is whoever trusts the aggregate run's plan accounting as the completeness check — which is precisely the job a TAP-aware consumer exists to do, so "PASS anyway" must not be normalized. Diagnose at the next-touch edit; if clause 4's shape-only paste is the cause, the fix belongs in how the tail is labeled, not in loosening the consumer.]*

**14. The taxonomy-partition watcher — TWO properties owed: an ARMED home, and per-ROW grain (a Cat-name comparison cannot see a mixed-element Cat).** [QA + Architect; seed layer; standing before the next seed delta]
- **Source.** [ADR-058](DECISIONS.md#adr-058) Amendment 2 (as landed, revision 3): the equality assertion's complement leg closes the wholly-liability-element new-Cat case at the assertion level, but (i) the whole assertion file is venue-gated and **skips in the default CI run**, and (ii) the leg compares **Cat NAMES** — `pfin.taxonomy_default` is keyed `unique (domain, cat, sub_cat)`, many rows per Cat with nothing forcing element agreement, so a **MIXED-element Cat** appears in both the raw and filtered sets, the difference omits it, and the leg **PASSES** while that Cat's liability rows leave the row set and the denominator together, silently, on a financial surface. Three grain gaps in this item, one class — element (row vs Cat name), arming (venue-gated vs unconditional), tenant (seed vs per-tenant); item 15 adds a fourth (partition-column, cross-surface). A watcher is only as good as the narrowest grain it actually reads.
- **AC (two properties; one leg can discharge both).** A seed-layer battery leg — venue-free, running unconditionally in CI — asserting the partition **PER ROW, never per Cat name**: every `pfin.taxonomy_default` row is exactly one of (a) rendered — its Cat a `CAT_GROUP_ORDER` member, with `element='asset'`; (b) named-excluded — its Cat in the §2.2.2 assertion's visible-exclusion constants, with `element='asset'`; or (c) `element='liability'` with its Cat in neither (a)'s nor (b)'s Cat set. A row matching none fails; a Cat whose rows straddle both elements fails **LOUDLY by construction** (its liability rows violate the (a)/(b) element requirement). ⚠ Porting the Cat-name comparison to the seed layer would arm the blindness, not the watcher — **the grain change IS the fix**, not a refinement.

  ⚠ **Third grain gap — the WATCHED table is not the READ table (Sec, SELF-239 review).** The equality assertion reads `pfin.taxonomy_default` (the global seed) while `computeNonReAllocation` reads the caller's own `pfin.user_taxonomy`, so a tenant whose provisioned rows carry a Cat label the seed has since renamed is **invisible to the watcher** — the [ADR-057](DECISIONS.md#adr-057) REACH class, lived once at `082`. **The per-ROW partition leg this item books must therefore run over `pfin.user_taxonomy` as well as `taxonomy_default`** — or state, in its own header, which table it does not cover and why an unwatched tenant-side label is acceptable.
- **Dependencies.** None. Worth more standing BEFORE the next seed delta than after it.

**15. §2.1.5's `total_non_re` and §2.2.2's `total_non_re` partition by DIFFERENT COLUMNS — the AC4 anchor equality between the two surfaces has NO watcher.** [QA + Architect; cross-surface seam; distinct from items 3 and 14 — see below]
- **Source.** Architect self-correction at the SELF-239 §3 build round (2026-08-20): the claim "§2.1.5's Total Non-RE anchor also excludes RE" was stated from the name and landed in a commit header as "(Architect-verified)" before being measured. Measured: `051`'s `total_non_re` filters `l.category not in ('real_estate','liability')` where `category` is **`pfin.account.account_type`**; §2.2.2 excludes Real Estate by **taxonomy Cat** and liabilities by **`element`** (`085`). Two independently-defined partitions, and nothing constrains a holding classified under Cat `'Real Estate'` to live in an account typed `real_estate`. The AC4 footing anchor between the surfaces therefore rests on two partitions happening to coincide. No battery references both surfaces' totals (a filtered-grep claim — treat as "not found," never "does not exist").
- **AC.** Either **(a)** a battery leg asserting the cross-surface equality AC4 names as its anchor — §2.2.2's `total_non_re` (Cat/element partition) equals §2.1.5's Total Non-RE (`account_type` partition) — **on a fixture where the partitions CAN diverge** (a Cat-`'Real Estate'` holding in a non-`real_estate`-typed account, and the converse); ⚠ a fully-aligned fixture is vacuous for this seam — the disagreement case is the entire point; or **(b)** an ADR ruling on the Non-RE boundary — either one column authoritative with the other surface re-deriving from it, **or** a finding that the two partitions are correctly different for their own surfaces (§2.1.5 an account-buildup view where `account_type` is the natural grain; §2.2.2 a holdings-classification view where taxonomy is) and the AC4 anchor equality is retired or qualified accordingly — the defect in that outcome being the anchor CLAIM itself, an equality asserted between two quantities never defined to be the same. Routes to Architect either way.
- **Why its own entry.** Item 3 books the `fn_subcat_market_value`/`fn_compute_nav` seam (producer totality); item 14 books one watcher's grain (element / arming / tenant). This is **two surfaces' definitions disagreeing — partition-column grain, the widest of the four** — and folding it into item 14 would file a cross-surface seam under a single-watcher heading where its reader would not look.
- **Dependencies.** None.

### §7.25 — SELF-241 (§2.2.3 US Equity drill-down) landing bookings (2026-08-20)

*Source: the PR #520 Sec joint-review (round-1 AMBER; F-2/F-3 dispositions finalized in the GREEN re-review) + Backend's F-3 reachability measurement (2026-08-20). F-1 (the degenerate-denominator render gate) was cleared IN-PR and is deliberately not booked here — its record is the fix itself plus the `us-equity-allocation.ts` header.*

**1. §2.2.2 / §2.2.3 server degenerate-state contract reconciliation.** [decision item; Architect + Backend; Sec informed] — **Promoted to Linear at SELF-332** (V1.2; 2026-08-20 F/CTO promotion sweep; Source / AC / Dependencies carried verbatim; AC identifiers verified in-tree at `cedbb95` per the §7.19 AC-3 promotion-time rule).
- *Body removed at promotion per the exit rule; the Linear issue carries the verbatim spec and the PR trail is the record.*

**2. Negative per-Sub-Cat `dollar_alloc`: honest-but-unmarked display + the untraced cash leg.** [product/data-quality; PM + Backend; books against the dormant per-row-staleness gap, NOT against the §2.2.3 table]
- **Source.** Sec F-3 (narrowed in the GREEN re-review) + Backend's measurement: `pfin.account_trans.quantity` is SIGNED with no non-negative CHECK (verified across `017`/`034`/`005`); `fn_holdings_as_of` (`019`) filters only `quantity <> 0`, its own comment naming sync-lag/skew V-6 an accepted gap; `fn_subcat_market_value` (`076`) sums with no floor. An oversell or checkpoint/txn skew therefore lands a NEGATIVE Sub-Cat `market_value` straight into that row's `dollar_alloc`. With a POSITIVE total (the F-1 gate correctly silent), the row renders a negative `pct_alloc` arithmetically honestly but with NO signal distinguishing data artifact from intentional short — the schema cannot make that distinction (Sec: a product/UX + data-quality question, not a security control gap). The `<= 0` domain gate on the TOTAL already covers the total-denominator case for every contributing leg, including untraced ones — the payoff of a domain guard over an enumeration of causes.
- **AC.** (a) The per-row staleness/data-quality channel (dormant on §2.2.2 and §2.2.3 alike) decides whether and how a negative-quantity-derived row is flagged — a product call to make alongside the per-row producer's design, not before it. (b) Backend's UNTRACED cash leg — whether a negative `balance_native` in a user-classified US Equity Sub-Cat is a second route to a negative `dollar_alloc` — is TRACED and its answer recorded either way. An open trace, not a gate; written down so it stays untraced-and-known rather than becoming untraced-and-forgotten.
- **Dependencies.** The per-row staleness producer (unbuilt; the same gap on both allocation surfaces).

**3. Route-loader (`+page.server.ts`) test-coverage precedent.** [QA; scope call]
- **Source.** Backend measurement at PR #520, QA-confirmed: ZERO `+page.server.ts` test coverage exists anywhere under `routes/` — the SELF-241 loader shipped untested like every other. QA correctly declined to start a repo-wide precedent unilaterally mid-PR.
- **AC.** A deliberate yes/no: either a loader-test convention (shape + first instance, QA-owned) or an explicit decision that loaders stay thin enough to ride component/dom + integration coverage. One short convention note or ADR line either way.
- **Dependencies.** None.

### §7.27 — SELF-325 purchase-path (PR #538) landing bookings (2026-08-22)

*Source: the PR #538 build arc — Sec joint-review (AMBER→GREEN ×2), QA's live browser walks, and Architect's landing reviews. **1. Unbounded-fetch-shape sweep.** [audit; Backend + Architect]
- **Source.** Backend's find at the F1 fix (Architect concurred, deliberately not swept under the re-freeze deadline — "a sweep run under a deadline becomes a skim"): the shape *a query keyed to entity count but fetching rows proportional to HISTORY, with no explicit bound* predates the fixed instance (`loadPricedFlags`, reachable at TWO ordinary holdings against `config.toml`'s `max_rows = 1000`) and may exist elsewhere; the NAV and allocation aggregate loaders are the obvious neighbours.
- **AC.** Enumerate `.from(...)` / RPC read sites in `api/src/lib/server/queries/**` whose result cardinality scales with row history rather than entity count; each hit gets a bound, a helper-RPC conversion (the `089` pattern), or a recorded why-safe. Over-match and filter; the sweep's filter is stated with its result.

**2. Pre-existing dangling `temp/` citations on `main`.** [doc-hygiene; Architect]

A dangling citation reads identically to a live one until someone follows it. The branch's own 12 were fixed in-arc (delete-or-inline, never a false re-point); these 5 are other arcs' surfaces. Architect's rule, the Source line for the fix: "a doc may describe its moment, but a committed file must not point AT that moment as its explanation."

Vehicle rules per `apply-migration`: these sit in `--` file headers (measured), so in-place correction is the likely route, subject to its three conditions.
- **Dependencies.** None.

Values agree today.

### §7.28 — V1.3 pre-flight recalibration close-out bookings (2026-08-22)

*Source: the V1.3 pre-flight recalibration sitting (F/CTO batch-ratify, 21 items; the full record — sitting log, both audit files, Sec's bounded consult, the re-derived AC sets — is committed at [`docs/records/v13-preflight/`](docs/records/v13-preflight/)). The sitting's build-side outputs are already landed: PRs #544/#545/#546, ADR-062, ADR-063, and all 14 V1.3 issue AC sets re-derived in Linear at baseline `0491830`. These are the findings that outlive the sitting and have no issue to ride.*

**1. §2.3-vs-GL reversal-netting disagreement — both correct, not equal.** [seam-record; Architect + Sec-visible]
- **Source.** E1 (a)'s measured consequence (sitting items 8/8a; evidence at `docs/records/v13-preflight/architect-findings.md`): §2.3 nets a reversal inside its original's Sub-Cat via the `replaces_trans_id` join; the GL posts the same reversal's contra to Suspense (no annotation → the `084` ordered-CASE `else`). The two totals differ by exactly the reversed amounts with an offsetting Suspense balance. Companion fact: a leg of an already-CLOSED balanced journal can be reversed today with neither `037` guard firing (conservation is close-time-only; the freeze guards annotation writes, which a reversal never makes). Worked example (Architect, 2026-08-26): a −100 expense classified Groceries and then edited to −110 makes the disagreement concrete — cash nets to −110 and the trial balance sums to zero, but the GL route reads Expense +100 (original) + 110 (corrected) with the reversal's −100 in Suspense, while the §2.3 route nets the pair inside Groceries and reads −110 — both correct, and equal only through the Suspense-offset identity.
- **AC.** The §7.24 item 3 totals-equality watcher, when built, is scoped to KNOW this disagreement (assert the reconciliation identity — §2.3 total + Suspense-offset = GL total — never bare equality); the §5.3 GL-native P&L design consumes this record. Until then this entry IS the record.
- **Dependencies.** Downstream consumers only (§7.24 item 3; §5.3).

**2. Split-child classification audit-coverage asymmetry.** [audit-class; Architect + Sec]
- **Source.** Measured at the E1 rider (sitting item 10a): `031` audits the 1:1 annotation's reclassifications (immutable history table); `029` split children have NO history — `unsplitTrans` and `writeSplitSet`'s REPLACE path both bare-DELETE the child set, discarding N user-authored `sub_cat_id` values unrecoverably and silently. Judged under ADR-011 Decision 2's audit-class discipline: two halves of one classification feature with opposite audit postures. V1.3 makes the classifications valuable (§2.3 sums read them); the in-issue mitigation (the split-parent reversal refusal states the cost) ships with SELF-248/249, but the asymmetry itself is unaddressed.
- **AC.** A ruling: either a `029`-side history table (the `031` pattern) or a recorded accept-with-rationale. Sec joint-review mandatory (audit-class surface).
- **Dependencies.** None blocking; naturally follows V1.3's classify surfaces landing.

**3. V1.4 tax-value inventory session (F/CTO).** [scope-record; PM] — **carrier: SELF-263, re-scoped at the V1.4 sitting (R5)**
- **Source.** Sitting item 19(1): SELF-245's original AC4 struck as discharged — `041` populated `tax_relevant`/`tax_character` per the existing-system mapping — but nobody has inventoried those values against what §2.5.1's ζ-2 consumer actually needs, and the Equity/Contribution row enters as flag-for-review (`tax_relevant = true`, notes rider per ADR-062). PM's recommendation, F/CTO-adopted: a mid-milestone F/CTO inventory session in V1.4. **Widened at the V1.4 pre-flight sitting** ([R5](docs/records/v14-preflight/sitting-log.md), PM §4 AC1): the asset-side `pfin.taxonomy_default` rows — all `false`/NULL at `041` — gate §2.5.1's capital-gains half and were outside the original booking.
- **AC.** The session happens before §2.5.1 implementation ships and its outcomes are recorded on SELF-263 (the carrier; its seed-delta migration records them — R5). Row set is **both default tables**: every `pfin.posting_prototype_default` row's `tax_relevant`/`tax_character` (cash-flow side) **and** every `pfin.taxonomy_default` row's (asset side). Decisions the session must produce, each with its reason (R5): (i) `Equity / Contribution` per account type; (ii) `Revenue / Bond Premium` = `ordinary` and `Revenue / Dividend` = `qualified_dividend` confirmed or corrected; (iii) the asset-side marking principle stated once; (iv) `long_term_capital_gain_eligible` / `short_term_only` assignments on the asset side. ⚠ **Under R1 (A) the asset-side outcome has no V1.4 consumer** (the capital-gains half renders UNAVAILABLE); it is decided in the same session because it is one F/CTO act and the seed delta is cheap, and it is **not on V1.4's critical path**. SELF-264 carries the hard-gate AC citing SELF-263 (R5).
- **Dependencies.** V1.4 entry — step 1 of the ruled dispatch order; F/CTO time (a sitting, not a dispatch).

**4. Conditional-status ADR sweep.** [doc-hygiene; Architect]
- **Source.** ADR-057's Status read "Proposed — F/CTO ratifies at PR sign-off" for weeks after `077` merged (corrected at ADR-063's leg-1 commit, cause named). Architect's generalization: any ADR whose Status names a discharging event nobody edits afterwards goes stale silently — ADR-062 had to cite ADR-057's rule rather than its status to avoid inheriting the stale claim.
- **AC.** One sweep of DECISIONS.md Status lines for undischarged-conditional wording ("ratifies at", "pending", "gates on"); each hit either updated against the event's actual state or confirmed genuinely pending; result recorded with the sweep's filter stated.
- **Dependencies.** None.

### §7.29 — Manual-entry correction primitives (STAGING; 1 item)

*New staging stream opened at the securities-edit close-out (F/CTO ruling 2026-08-26 context; entry ratified as its own item on the close-out doc PR). Placement decision presented to F/CTO at that PR: (a) **V1.x here [PM lean]** — the dead end this closes is common-error-reachable (a duplicate cash entry is the highest-frequency manual-surface error) and, unlike the securities dead end, was UNRATIFIED — it fell out of the immutability design, nobody decided it; or (b) **V2+** — this entry moves to §5.4 with the acceptance recorded verbatim: "a spurious cash entry is unremovable for all of V1; repurposing is the only mitigation." Entry content is identical under either placement. Exact product-V1.x label pending roadmap sequencing (the §7.3 framing precedent).*

**V1. Void primitive (reverse-only correction: remove a spurious transaction without replacement).**
- **Source.** F/CTO ruling at the securities-edit close-out doc-PR sitting (own ratify item) + the 2026-08-26 Architect measurement: no delete/remove/reverse-only action exists; `004` blocks DELETE for every role; both manual RPCs hard-code `is_reverse = false`; reverse-and-replace cannot zero (`nonZeroAmount` schema). Record: [`docs/records/self340-securities-edit-options.md`](docs/records/self340-securities-edit-options.md).
- **AC.** (1) A void action on a cash transaction posts the reversal row only — no replacement; the ledger's sanctioned removal re-post (immutability preserved; nothing deleted). (2) **Scope fence: cash rows only** — a security-bearing void is §7.3 G3 / M4-GL territory (position/cost_basis leg semantics) and is refused here exactly as fact-edits are. (3) Void date semantics decided at spec-time F/CTO ratify (back-date vs void-date — the same ratify-level choice as G3 AC 5; do not inherit the code's silent back-dating). (4) A voided row's annotation/classification history is retained (Lock 10 / ADR-031); voided rows surface in an audit view, not in aggregations. (4b) **Category-leg decision, explicit at spec time (Architect design input, 2026-08-26):** a void cannot simply omit the second insert — because a row's GL class comes from its own annotation, a void carrying no category leaves its offset in Suspense on the GL route; the spec must decide whether the void row carries the original's category or inherits the §7.28 item 1 ruled reversal shape (no annotation; join-netting on the §2.3 surfaces). Not a live defect — a booked-seam consequence the spec must choose a side of. (5) A split parent must be unsplit before void; a provider-sourced row's void must survive resync (dedup already ingest-level — verify no re-surface path). (6) **Sec-joint-review-mandatory** (new money-flow write semantics: a reversal row with no replacement). (7) QA battery: void → net-zero GL, absent from aggregations, present in audit view; refusal legs for security rows and split parents.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: the securities-edit refusal guard (ships with the Edit-flow fix PR) — the void must not become an edit bypass. Independent of M4-GL/`lot_match` (cash-only fence). Downstream: closes the cash-path removal dead end; the PRD §2.4.3 "correction is not removal" sentence gets its lift-pointer.

All dated authoring-time records where the point-in-time defense holds.

The proof legs: `api/src/routes/reports/monthly/[target_month]/pdf/pdf.escaping.test.ts` (a stored `<script>` in commentary and the owner string rendered inert; `schedule_label` documented there as structurally absent from the monthly-report payload and template) and the worker-side `workers/pdf-render/test/render.test.js` (A4, SELF-348); the byte-level walk at execution log E105 (both payloads readable as literal text in `pdftotext`). Escaping in Svelte does not transfer to the worker.

*Source: F/CTO rulings 2026-09-07 on the seven §F items of [`docs/records/v1final/self365-protocol.md`](docs/records/v1final/self365-protocol.md), recorded there at §G; the dispatch list at its §H names every owner. - **Source.** Record §C.2 / §G.7 — the monthly-report cron's population is ruled T-1 (account-owning tenants only, `select distinct users_id from pfin.account`, pinned by comment on SELF-351); under ADR-036 open signup a confirmed user with no accounts therefore gets no draft, no pending item, no notification, and today no cue.

Not a V1.final blocker: the F/CTO tenant owns accounts.

**2. PRD amendments booked by the rulings (one PR; merged before V1.final (a) / (b) close).** [PM; Sec read-only on P-3]
- **Source.** Record §G.2 (P-1), §G.3 (P-2), §G.7 + D-5 (P-3); §H "PRD amendment bookings" holds the wording.
- **AC.** (P-1) [PRD §3.4](docs/PRD/index.html#sec-3-4)(a)(ii) *"a §3.3 parity test passing for the canonical comparison month"* → *a recorded manual parity comparison for the deploy month, per-cell over §3.3's §2.6 clauses at the stated tolerance*, with the failure-mode sentence following, and §3.3's tolerance-class machinery marked *retained as the V2 harness specification* (item 5 below), not struck. (P-2) §3.4(b) drops *"and parity-tested against the existing-system equivalent"* and *"The §3.3 parity tests cover the parity dimension;"*, keeps *"no ADR-004 / ADR-005 / ADR-006 capability is skipped, partially built, or deferred to V2 silently"*, and adds the deferral-audit reading (a deferral names its ratifying ADR). (P-3) [PRD §7.3](docs/PRD/index.html#sec-7-3) *"V1 ships to a single user (the F/CTO) … closed and invite-controlled"* → open signup + email confirmation per [ADR-036](DECISIONS.md#adr-036), the F/CTO as the first tenant, not the only one; the §2.1 / §2.2 / §2.3 story sentences *"V1 ships to a single user"* swept in the same PR against each story's scope clause; §5.7's *"Multi-user invite-only V2 expansion"* cross-reference re-worded to match. Each amendment carries its ruling date and the record path.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: item 3's ADR (the amendment cites it). Downstream: V1.final (a) and (b) sub-issues (record §B.1 item 5, §B.2 item 4); the close PR (§B.4).

**3. Terse ADR consolidating rulings 1–3 (Phase 6/7 ordering; §3.4(a)(ii) reinterpretation; §3.4(b) parity-clause drop + security-catalog re-home).** [Architect; DECISIONS.md]
- **Source.** Record §G.1–§G.3; the [WORKFLOW.md](WORKFLOW.md) Phase 6 *Exit criteria* amendment landed with this booking (*"All V1 milestones complete except the calendar-gated V1.final close-gate, which closes in Phase 7"*). These are non-obvious decisions: a phase gate now names an exception, a PRD success criterion is re-read from an automated test to a recorded manual comparison, and a live AC clause moved homes — none of which a reader can derive from the tree.
- **AC.** One terse-pattern ADR (ADR-009 D8 shape) with three Decisions: (1) Phase 7 starts in parallel with a Phase 6 that still holds V1.final CURRENT; production stand-up is a Platform / Cross-cutting DevOps issue; the month-1 clock = first full month after tenant accounts are live in production; options not taken named. (2) §3.4(a)(ii) satisfied by A-3 (trace + one manual §2.6 comparison on the deploy month, Backend completeness check as a named precondition); A-1 / A-2 not taken; the harness retained as V2 spec. (3) §3.4(b) = B-2 (delivery trace + silent-deferral audit, no parity clause) and S-2 (the security-catalog check homed at the Phase 6 exit walk). Cites the record by path + sha; the close PR (record §B.4 item 6) cites the ADR by anchor.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: none. Downstream: item 2 (the PRD amendment cites it); record §B.4.

**4. SECURITY §4.6 cutover obligations (record E-4).** [Sec; close-PR content]
- **Source.** SECURITY §4.6 *Shadow-workflow tear-down* — four commitments, two of which have no owner or home on the tree: **read-only archive of the existing system at a Sec-acknowledged access-controlled location** and **a snapshot of the existing system at cutover preserved as an audit artifact**. Both are V1-required and are content of the V1.final close PR (record §B.4 item 2).
- **AC.** Sec names, before the close PR opens: the archive's access-control mechanism and location class (the same routing-flag-(j) question the parity fixtures never resolved — one answer for both), what the cutover snapshot consists of (the incumbent workbook + `Finance_Report` set as of the last counting month, at minimum), where it is held, and who can read it; each of the four §4.6 commitments is then marked discharged or explicitly routed in the close PR. No production rows, no repo storage.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: none (can be answered now). Downstream: record §B.4.

**5. §3.3 parity harness retained as V2 specification (record E-1 / §A.2 A-1).** [PM custody; V2+]
- **Source.** Ruling A-3 (§G.2) closes §3.4(a)(ii) without the automated harness; PRD §3.3's six per-surface tests with their tolerance classes (≤ $1 absolute OR ≤ 0.01 % relative, whichever is greater, for derived dollar values; strict equality for categorical fields; structural equivalence for layout) remain the only written specification of what "matches the existing system" means numerically.
- **AC.** The P-1 amendment (item 2) marks §3.3 *retained as the V2 harness specification*; nothing in §3.3 is struck. If a harness is ever built it needs, in order: a resolved on-tree-or-off-tree fixture location (SECURITY §4.6 routing flag (j) — never landed as a tree fact), a GL-era re-derivation of §3.3's cell lists (some cells are UNAVAILABLE-by-ruling since the GL recalibration, record §A.1), and a Sec review of a fixture holding real figures. Booked as a §5-shaped V2 candidate living here only until the P-1 amendment lands the retention note in the PRD itself; then this item reduces to its header line.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: item 2 (P-1). Downstream: none in V1.

### §7.36 — Stand-up preconditions rulings landing bookings (2026-09-08)

*Source: F/CTO rulings on the three remaining §F items of [`docs/records/v1final/standup-preconditions.md`](docs/records/v1final/standup-preconditions.md) (Q5 / Q7 / Q8, recorded there at §G.4). The three stand-up issues themselves (I-1 / I-2 / I-3) are created in Linear from the record's §H, not staged here. This section holds the one item the rulings created that has no Linear carrier yet.*

**1. Operator allowlist on the Link-token and re-auth start routes — gates "production signup on".** [Backend + Frontend; Sec-visible (read before merge); no milestone yet — PM promotes when F/CTO names one]
- **Source.** Record §G.4 Q5 + §E-3 + §E-10: Plaid's free-Item quota is **per `client_id`** (10 on the Trial plan; removals do not restore), so under ADR-036 open signup any tenant's Link session spends a slot the founding tenant's own institutions draw on. F/CTO ruled: production signup stays **off** (Supabase Auth signup disabled on the self-hosted stack — the production knob is DevOps's to name at SELF-386, record D-12), the founding tenant is created by invitation, the N=2 soak runs with a sole tenant, and **this allowlist is built before signup is switched on**. PRD §7.3 carries it as the re-worded P-3 (record §A.3; §7.35 item 2's PR).
- **AC.** (1) A tenant not on the operator allowlist calling `GET /api/plaid/link-token` (`api/src/routes/api/plaid/link-token/+server.ts`) or the re-auth start route (`api/src/routes/api/reauth/start/+server.ts`) receives a refusal envelope in the route's existing generic shape (no upstream detail, C6-5) **before** any worker admission call is made; a test asserts no `/admission/link-token` request is issued for a non-allowlisted tenant. (2) The allowlist is an **operator** config surface — not a tenant-editable setting, not a role concept in the data model (§7.3 / ADR-036 have tenants, not roles); its storage shape and where the check sits (api route before the relay vs. the worker admission endpoint) are Architect's call with Sec's read, stated in the PR body with the losing side. (3) Membership is by tenant identity from the validated session only (SC3-C1), never from the request. (4) The connect and re-auth surfaces render **one** copy string for a non-allowlisted tenant — PM copy: *"Connecting an institution isn't enabled for this account yet."* (final wording at UX's pass) — a named state, never the generic failure; manual accounts (§2.4.2) and everything else stay available. (5) The allowlist contains the founding tenant on first deploy of the feature; a test covers allowlisted-passes / non-allowlisted-refused / unauthenticated-401-unchanged. (6) A SECURITY note (Sec-owned, one paragraph) records the control and its scope: it bounds **who can mint an Item**, not how many; the app still never counts to 10 (§E-10). (7) **"Production signup on" is a separate, later operator action** that this item blocks: it is not flipped in this PR.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: SELF-386 (deployed target; the production signup switch named and off). Downstream: the operator action "production signup on" (ADR-036 open signup resumes in production); PRD P-3's second clause becomes true. No relation to I-1 / I-2 / I-3 (the sole-tenant soak does not need it).

**2. Silent half-applied-state control for `pfin_provider_sync`.** [Backend (worker start-up assertion) + DevOps (deploy check); blocks the Phase-7 deploy pass's provider-sync cutover step; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** Sec joint-review of PR #671 / migration `116` (`temp/`-scratchpad review, md5 `fd82cae2b6562d05048f6941d327ead3`), condition C4, plus `116`'s own header analysis ("the two half-applied states are NOT symmetric, and the asymmetry is the reason this block exists" — `supabase/migrations/116_pfin_provider_sync_role.sql:96`). Nothing in the tree observes which login identity the provider-sync container actually uses at runtime (`git grep -n "current_user\|session_user" -- workers/provider-sync/src` returns zero hits, per Sec's measurement). Env-switched-but-role-absent fails loudly (an outage). Role-present-but-env-unswitched is silent, keeps the ADR-023 C1-labeled rotation coupling live, and looks finished from every catalog read (`rolcanlogin = t` proves only that the deploy step ran).
- **AC.** Sec's recommended shape, **option B**, optionally composed with **option C** (Sec did not pick unilaterally — this AC records the recommendation, not a mandate; Backend/DevOps confirm at build time): (1) the provider-sync worker, in production mode, asserts `select current_user` at (or before) its first `TenantBoundClient` connection and **refuses to boot** (non-zero exit) if it returns `authenticator` or anything other than `pfin_provider_sync` — fail-closed, no new credential, no new secret; must not fire in local/dev where `authenticator` is legitimate, so it needs an explicit production discriminator (a config surface in itself — name it in the PR). (2) Optionally, a deploy-time check (DevOps, `docs/deployment-runbook.md` §6.2) reads `select distinct usename from pg_stat_activity where application_name = 'provider-sync'` to observe the container's effective identity from the server side — this requires the worker to set `application_name` on its connections, which it does **not** currently do (precondition, not yet true; booked here rather than assumed). (3) Catch criterion for (1), testable: the provider-sync container exits non-zero at boot when its effective login role is `authenticator` in production mode. (4) B and C compose; neither substitutes for the other — B is continuous and per-boot, C is point-in-time but reads reality rather than configuration.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: migration `116` merged (PR #671). Downstream: blocks the Phase-7 deploy pass's provider-sync cutover step (`docs/deployment-runbook.md` §6.2's post-cutover check references this item for the `pg_stat_activity` limb's `application_name` precondition).

**3. Migration `117` — comment-only correction of `055`'s `comment on role` C1 label.** [Architect; RULING LANDED 2026-09-09 — UNBLOCKED; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** Sec joint-review of PR #671 / migration `116`, condition C1 (md5 `fd82cae2b6562d05048f6941d327ead3`): `supabase/migrations/055_pfin_etl_role.sql:475`'s `comment on role` labels the PostgREST/provider-sync rotation coupling "ADR-023 condition C1". Migration `116`'s header (the "MIS-ATTRIBUTED LABEL" block) carries the measured, corrected state, superseding the retracted claim this entry previously stated — *"no text in `DECISIONS.md` attaches the rotation coupling to a condition labeled C1 under ADR-023"* — which ADR-041 refutes by attaching it in several paragraphs, including the sub-decision promoting ADR-019 C2 to a Phase-7 deploy gate (the paragraph migration `116` realizes). No count and no site list is recorded here: an earlier fixed-count enumeration measured at four paragraphs / seven occurrences, and a corrected count would re-arm the same trap. Locate them with the grep below, bracketed to ADR-041's body by its `## ADR-041` heading. The label is **mis-attributed, not unsupported**: right content, wrong pointer. ADR-023's own enumerated C1 is a different condition — an exposure-readiness artifact (per-table RLS + policy proof) reviewed before exposure. **F/CTO ruled 2026-09-09** (option 1 of three): reconstruct C1 — and C3/C4, text-unrecoverable, number-retained — as a dated amendment, marked reconstructed. Because `comment on role` is a database object, it is correctable only by a further comment-only migration (the `052` shape — regenerate-and-diff, containment proof, render-verify per the `apply-migration` procedure), not an edit-in-place.

This item is **UNBLOCKED**. The comment-only correction migration is authored: `supabase/migrations/117_pfin_etl_role_comment_c1_reattribution.sql` (PR #675 `feature/117-c1-relabel-055-warning`, **merged 2026-09-09 at `c3314890`**, after PR #674 `f4568f36` — a re-pointed label needs its target committed first, and it was). Folds the same pass as the `DECISIONS.md` ADR-041 amendment's C1 re-attribution (Sec correction-vehicle table: ADR-041 — located by bracketing its `## ADR-041` heading to the next `## ADR-` heading, never by a stored line list or count — `055` header `--` block, `055` `comment on role`, all Architect-owned).
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: none — ruling landed, migration `117` authored (PR #675, merged `c3314890`). Downstream: item 4 below (the `secrets-manifest.yml` + runbook label re-attribution) reads this migration's landed label as its source of truth once PR #675 merges.

- **AC.** **RULING LANDED 2026-09-09** — [`DECISIONS.md` ADR-019 *Amendment (2026-09-09)*](DECISIONS.md#adr-019): the label's home is ADR-019 condition C1 (reconstructed); C3/C4 are number-retained, text unrecoverable, not citable as rules. Once `117` merges and re-attributes `055`'s `comment on role`: (1) every DevOps-owned carrier surfaced by migration `116`'s header grep — `git grep -n -E '(^|[^A-Za-z0-9])C1([^0-9A-Za-z]|$)' -- ':!node_modules' ':!.claude/agent-memory' ':!docs/archive'`, re-run at the sha being corrected and filtered to rotation-coupling uses; this is the durable instruction, not the fixed list below — is re-attributed to **"ADR-019 condition C1 (reconstructed 2026-09-09)"**: `secrets-manifest.yml`'s `PFIN_DB_PASSWORD` entry (its "ATTRIBUTION UNDER CORRECTION" inline note is removed once re-attributed), the archived §6.1's `[ADR-023](../DECISIONS.md#adr-023) C1 rotation coupling` link (`docs/archive/deployment-runbook-rationale-2026-09-20.md`, formerly `docs/deployment-runbook.md:316` — that line-number citation is stale post-W-4 re-shape), `workers/etl/.env.example`'s C1 sites (re-locate by the grep, not by line number), and **`workers/CLAUDE.md`** — a live C1-label carrier (`workers/CLAUDE.md:37–38`, Sec round-review F7) matching no row in `116`'s per-artifact vehicle-class table and named in no prior §7.36 booking; a DevOps/Backend-owned per-directory file, edit-in-place. That same line carries a **second, independent defect**: it says the label's correct home *"is under F/CTO ruling (BACKLOG §7.36 item 4)"* — false as of the 2026-09-09 ruling, same falsified-open-question class as Sec F8 on the BACKLOG bookings themselves. **6. `055`'s operator warning (re-apply guard, LOGIN-with-no-password branch) prescribes the B10-prohibited single-statement credential form.** [Architect (vehicle ruling + any migration-file edit) + DevOps (runbook interception); returns to Sec; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** Sec round-3 joint-review of PR #671 (frozen `1975bb99`), finding F12. `supabase/migrations/055_pfin_etl_role.sql`'s re-apply guard — a `raise warning` inside its `DO` block, fired exactly when `pfin_etl` is found LOGIN with no password, i.e. when an operator is at the keyboard about to remediate — instructs: *"Either complete the deploy step (ALTER ROLE pfin_etl WITH LOGIN PASSWORD ...) or disable it (ALTER ROLE pfin_etl NOLOGIN)."* That is the single-statement form ADR-041's Consequences, `055`'s own header, `055`'s own `comment on role`, `116`, `workers/CLAUDE.md` and `secrets-manifest.yml` all state is **PROHIBITED per Sec B10**: statement logging writes the credential to the server log in cleartext. `116`'s structurally identical warning gets it right (`\password`), which is what made this visible. Pre-existing on `main` (measured at `2bb6b0e6`), so it does not block PR #671's delta. Content-anchored (never by line number — `055`'s header grows over time): the `raise warning` reads *"pfin_etl exists as LOGIN with NO PASSWORD ... Either complete the deploy step (ALTER ROLE pfin_etl WITH LOGIN PASSWORD ...) or disable it..."*, inside the `if v_canlogin and v_haspass = 'NO' then` branch. A lesser instance sits in the same file's header, in the "WHY THIS GUARD DOES NOT RE-APPLY ATTRIBUTES" block (the sentence beginning *"After a successful deploy, `pfin_etl` is LEGITIMATELY `LOGIN` (an operator ran `ALTER ROLE pfin_etl WITH LOGIN PASSWORD …`)"*) and describes the prohibited form as the normal deploy action.
- **Why it is not fixed in place at PR #671 — Architect's vehicle ruling (2026-09-08).** The warning text does **not** clear `apply-migration` Step 1.6's edit-in-place bar. Step 1.6 (B) is scoped to *a file-header `--` block*; the warning is executable SQL (a string literal inside an applied `DO` block), and Step 1.6's closing constraint is categorical — *"Editing an applied migration's SQL is out under every framing — including 'it was wrong and never worked.'"* Two of the three (B) conditions fail independently: **condition 1** is the **replay-equivalence** condition, made true by construction (not by inspection) when zero non-comment lines are added or removed — this edit adds and removes exactly one non-comment line, so it fails mechanically; **condition 2** is the **voiding-a-prior-approval** condition — it permits descriptions, never commitments, and explicitly excludes changing an **operator instruction** — the warning is an operator instruction, so it fails independently. (Sec round-4 N9: an earlier draft of this entry glossed conditions 1 and 2 backwards, as both being "about voiding a prior approval, not about replay" — condition 1's own text is the opposite, *"this makes replay-equivalence true by construction rather than by inspection."* The conclusion is unaffected — both conditions fail regardless — but a future reader should not inherit the wrong gloss on condition 1.) Sec's supporting measurements (no CI migration-checksum fence in `.github/workflows/` or `scripts/ci/`; the `DO` block has no DB representation; re-application is idempotent) establish **replay safety**, which is necessary but not sufficient for either condition.
- **The structural residual — no vehicle currently reaches this text.** Unlike item 3's `comment on role` (correctable by the `052` comment-only shape at `117`), a later migration cannot alter a past `DO` block's warning string and no catalog object holds it. `055` will be replayed verbatim at the one-and-only production deploy, where the warning routes an operator to log a credential in cleartext.
- **AC.** (1) **LANDED at PR #669/#671** — DevOps's interception note is live in `docs/deployment-runbook.md` §6.1 (the surface the operator is reading at deploy time): *"Do not follow `055`'s own re-apply warning text..."*, naming the prohibited form, the sanctioned two-step handoff `(1) \password pfin_etl` then `(2) ALTER ROLE pfin_etl LOGIN;`, and Sec B10. States plainly that the wrong warning text still prints (necessary-but-not-sufficient). (2) **RULED 2026-09-09 (F/CTO, option 1 of three) — exception granted.** The object Step 1.6 protects is a prior approval of *deployed state*; under ADR-021 greenfield none exists — `055` has never been applied to a production database and replays verbatim at first deploy, so the wrong instruction reaches the operator it is aimed at rather than nobody. The precedent is bounded to this one line by the ruling's own text. **Edit landed at PR #675** `feature/117-c1-relabel-055-warning` (**merged 2026-09-09 at `c3314890`**; Sec's round-3 F12 commit-ready text used verbatim, exceeding the `116`-mirror ask by adding an explicit "single-statement form is prohibited" statement); Sec joint-review is mandatory before merge (`055` is Sec-load-bearing). (3) **Re-run the sweep, never rely on a stored instance list**: `git grep -n -E "ALTER (ROLE|USER) [a-z_]+ WITH (LOGIN )?PASSWORD" -- supabase/ docs/ workers/ secrets-manifest.yml` at the sha being corrected, and correct every hit that PRESCRIBES or DESCRIBES-AS-NORMAL the single-statement form; hits that state it is PROHIBITED are correct and must be left alone (Sec round-4 N7). The lesser header instance (content-anchored above) is squarely Step 1.6 (B) — a `--` block, description not instruction — and clears all three conditions today; fix in place in whichever PR next touches `055`. Suggested wording: *"an operator ran the two-statement deploy handoff"*. A fourth carrier the sweep surfaces — `supabase/tests/rls/054_nav_daily_rls.sql`'s battery comment, pre-existing on `main`, zero operator reach — is QA-owned and out of this item's scope; booked separately as **item 7 below**.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: none — AC (1) landed, AC (2)'s ruling landed and its edit merged (PR #675 `c3314890`). Downstream: none — independent of the C1 re-attribution chain (items 3–5) and of `117`.

**7. `supabase/tests/rls/054_nav_daily_rls.sql`'s battery comment states the prohibited single-statement credential form as the deploy flip.** [QA; edit-in-place; same sweep class as item 6; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** Sec round-4 joint-review of PR #671 (frozen `90c7fc69`), finding N7. The prohibited-form sweep (`git grep -n -E "ALTER (ROLE|USER) [a-z_]+ WITH (LOGIN )?PASSWORD" -- supabase/ docs/ workers/ secrets-manifest.yml`, positive-controlled: 13 `ALTER ROLE` hits in `055`, 8 in `116`) surfaced a fourth carrier neither the round-3 review nor item 6 originally named: content-anchored at the sentence *"Architect resolved the Sec passwordless-window NOTE by creating the role NOLOGIN + NOINHERIT + NO PASSWORD ... (flipped with a single `ALTER ROLE pfin_etl WITH LOGIN PASSWORD '<secret>'` at deploy)"* in `supabase/tests/rls/054_nav_daily_rls.sql`. States the prohibited form as **the** deploy flip — same class as `055`'s lesser header instance (item 6 AC (3)). Pre-existing on `main` (empty diff `2bb6b0e6`→`90c7fc69`); zero operator reach — a test-file comment, not a merged migration or a runbook — so it does not block a merge and is freely edit-in-place. `supabase/tests/` is QA-owned; Step 1.6 does not apply to test files.
- **AC.** Replace exactly: `` (flipped with a single `ALTER ROLE pfin_etl WITH LOGIN PASSWORD '<secret>'` at deploy) `` with `` (flipped at deploy by the two-step handoff — `\password pfin_etl` then `ALTER ROLE pfin_etl LOGIN`; the single-statement `WITH LOGIN PASSWORD` form is PROHIBITED per Sec B10) `` — Sec's commit-ready text, taken verbatim. Located by the content anchor above, never by a stored line number.
- **Dependencies.** None. Independent of items 3–6 (same sweep class; no shared vehicle gate).

**8. `055`'s header carries stale ledger counts (Decision-3 family, the SECURITY DEFINER "authored so far" tally) plus two accurate figures that are Path-B non-compliant, not wrong.** [Architect; edit-in-place, `apply-migration` Step 1.6 (B); no milestone yet]
- **Source.** Booked at PR #675's Follow-ups — deliberately not taken in that PR, since #675 is a narrowly-bounded F/CTO exception on a Sec-load-bearing file and widening its diff mid-review changes what Sec is grading. Sec's round-5 joint review of PR #675 (Item 6) measured every content-anchored site (at `main` `6a19dc34`; sites will move the next time `055`'s header is touched — PR #675 alone adds 37 header lines) and classified each — this entry carries that classification, not a re-derivation:

**9. Post-merge `pfin_tmpl` template refresh did not fire for PR #671.** [DevOps; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** PR #675 body, Test plan: `pfin_tmpl` was found **stale** — stamped at migration `115` while `main` was at `116` — when PR #675's author went to clone it, and had to be rebuilt by hand with `scripts/db-template-build.sh` before use. Whatever mechanism is expected to refresh the template after a migration merges (PR #671, `115`→`116`) did not fire.
- **AC.** (1) Find why the post-merge template-refresh trigger did not fire for PR #671 — check whether a refresh step exists in CI or a hook at all, or exists but silently no-op'd, and record the finding in this entry once found. (2) Add a **watcher**: a check (CI job, or scheduled script with an alerting path) that reds when `pfin_tmpl`'s stamped migration lags `main`'s latest migration file. A watcher that only re-fixes this one instance without a standing check would leave the same silent-drift failure mode to recur at the next merge. (3) **Addendum (Sec C7, PR #675 round-5 joint review, 2026-09-09):** `pg_shdescription` is a **shared** catalog — a role comment is per-CLUSTER, not per-database. Any refresh/rebuild this item's watcher touches (`scripts/db-template-build.sh`, or any render-verify/replay of the `055`→`116`→`117` role-comment chain) MUST run against a **disposable cluster** (a fresh container), never a scratch database inside the shared local dev cluster — applying a role-comment migration against a scratch database mutates that comment for every database in the cluster. This already happened: the local dev cluster carries `117`'s comment today from Architect's scratch apply during PR #675 review. Repair path if `117`'s text changes again: re-apply `055` then `117` (overwrite is benign — new text is the intended end state; no squatting axis). Same discipline applies to whatever mechanism part (1) finds and to the watcher's own verification runs in part (2).
- **Dependencies.** None.

**10. No pgTAP leg observes `pfin_etl`'s role comment after migration `117` (Sec C6).** [QA; PR #675 (migration `117`) merged `c3314890` — UNBLOCKED; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** Sec round-5 joint review of PR #675, C6. `grep -rn "obj_description" supabase/tests` finds function-comment legs at the `052`/`071`/`074`/`098` batteries plus `116`'s (r11) **presence-only** leg (`shobj_description(oid,'pg_authid') is not null`); `grep -rn "pfin_etl" supabase/tests | grep -i "descr\|comment"` returns **zero**. `pfin_etl`'s role comment has no observer at all — `117`'s entire payload is catalog text and ships unwatched: an out-of-order re-apply of `055`, or a revert of `117`, would silently restore the wrong C1 pointer with nothing to catch it.
- **AC.** Add a boundary-pair pgTAP leg — `shobj_description(...,'pg_authid') ~ 'ADR-019 condition C1'` **and** `!~ 'ADR-023''s C1'` — inversion-verified by corrupting the control (per Sec's ask; a leg that cannot fail is not a leg). Targets the post-`117` comment text, so it lands after migration `117` (PR #675) merges to `main`. Because `pg_shdescription` is cluster-wide, the inversion must corrupt the role comment itself (set it back to the `ADR-023` text) and confirm RED in the same cluster and the same run the pass is claimed in — a local dev cluster can already carry `117`'s comment from a prior scratch apply, and a pass that never re-derives RED from a live corruption is evidence of residue, not of the leg observing the run's own apply.
- **Dependencies.** None — PR #675 (migration `117`) merged 2026-09-09 at `c3314890`; unblocked.

**15. 🟡 FIX LANDED, MATERIALIZED ON THE BOX, LIVE FIRE NOT YET PROVEN. `scripts/migrator-orchestrate.sh` addresses the Scheduled Task under `/services/{uuid}/scheduled-tasks/…`, but the task was created under `/applications/{uuid}/scheduled-tasks` — trigger 404s until reconciled.** [DevOps; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** Sec joint-review of PR #752 at `d13bf3f6` (F-3). `docs/records/v1final/standup-log.md:292` records the migrator Scheduled Task created via `POST /applications/{uuid}/scheduled-tasks` against the Supabase-stack **application** resource (`provision-supabase-stack.sh` creates that resource via `POST /applications/public`, confirming it is an Application, not a Service). `scripts/migrator-orchestrate.sh:109` and `:115` address `/services/$MIGRATOR_SERVICE_UUID/scheduled-tasks/…`. If the stack is an Application resource, the trigger 404s. `api()` in that script uses `curl -fsS` and the call is `|| fail`, so this fails **closed** — no security defect, but the CI trigger will not pass until reconciled.
- **AC.** Change `scripts/migrator-orchestrate.sh:109,:115` to address `/applications/$MIGRATOR_SERVICE_UUID/scheduled-tasks/…`, matching the resource type actually created. Verify against a live `GET` before relying on it. **Vehicle: script edit-in-place.**
- **Dependencies.** ✅ Discharged 2026-09-16 — `provision-vps.sh --apply` (F/CTO-executed, Phase C complete) materialized `migrator-orchestrate.sh`, carrying this fix, onto the box (root:root, `0755`). Full closure (confirming the corrected route does not 404 live) still waits on Phase D's first actual fire — not yet exercised.

**16. Migrator Scheduled Task inertness rests on a hand-set `enabled: false` with no watcher, and its cron (`0 0 1 1 *`) is a real date that will fire if the flag is ever lost.** [DevOps; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** Sec joint-review of PR #752 at `d13bf3f6` (F-2). Verified in Coolify v4.3.18's `ScheduledTasksController`: `$task->enabled = $request->has('enabled') ? $request->enabled : true` — a re-create or dashboard edit that omits `enabled` defaults to `true`. No script in this repo creates or patches the task (`grep -rn scheduled-tasks scripts/ .github/` finds only `migrator-orchestrate.sh`'s execute/executions calls). A box rebuild, task re-create, or dashboard edit that omits `enabled` yields an unsupervised annual Jan-1 apply — operational, not adversarial, but contradicts ADR-072 Decision 2's "never a timer."
- **AC.** The fix is a watcher (a periodic check that asserts `enabled: false` on the migrator Scheduled Task) or a creation script that sets `enabled: false` by construction on every create/re-create. Add Sec's suggested warning to `scripts/migrator-scheduled-task.md`'s Frequency row too — *"`enabled` defaults to `true` when the create payload omits it, and `0 0 1 1 *` is a real date that WILL fire. Any re-create must pass `enabled: false` explicitly, and the operator must read it back (`GET /applications/{uuid}/scheduled-tasks`) and confirm `enabled: false` before walking away."* — but the warning alone is the floor, not the fix, and does not discharge this finding on its own. **Owner: DevOps.**
- **Dependencies.** None.
