**19. `scripts/provision-supabase-stack.sh`'s secrets-mint step performs local command substitution on secret-bearing heredoc content — not cosmetic.** [DevOps; flagged, not exploitable today; no milestone yet] — **PR open at #754**
- **Source.** Observed live during PR #752's Phase A.3 execution (standup step 6, 2026-09-13): `BOX_IP=188.245.166.206 scripts/provision-supabase-stack.sh --apply`'s "Secrets: mint-if-absent" step printed `scripts/provision-supabase-stack.sh: line 480: migrator: command not found` three times before reporting `MINTED: ['MIGRATOR_DB_PASSWORD', 'MIGRATOR_DB_USER']` and all keys `OK`. **Sec joint-review of PR #752 at `ca154f2a` read the mint step live and identified the mechanism:** the `sshx_in <<REMOTE` heredoc that handles `MIGRATOR_DB_PASSWORD` has an **unquoted delimiter**, so the local shell performs command substitution on the heredoc body *before* sending it over SSH — the `migrator` bareword is being locally executed as a command in the path that handles that secret's mint. **Not exploitable today** (the value is hex from `openssl rand -hex 32`, so nothing shell-meaningful is embedded), but the mechanism is real and generalizes badly: an unquoted heredoc delimiter around secret-bearing content is a latent local command-injection surface, not a display bug.
- **AC.** Find and fix the **mechanism** — quote the heredoc delimiter (`<<'REMOTE'`) or otherwise stop local command/variable substitution on the body — not just suppress the `migrator: command not found` symptom. Confirm no other secret-bearing heredoc in this script shares the same unquoted-delimiter shape. **Vehicle: script edit-in-place. Owner: DevOps.**
- **Dependencies.** None.

**20. `infra/supabase/migrator/Dockerfile` installs the pinned Supabase CLI tarball with no checksum or signature verification — no published integrity reference exists for the exact artifact installed.** [DevOps; predates PR #753 (chunk 1, #741); no milestone yet]
- **Source.** Sec joint-review of PR #753 at `a2ce5484` (B-1). `infra/supabase/migrator/Dockerfile`'s `curl -fsSL` of `supabase_linux_${TARGETARCH}.tar.gz` has no checksum/signature check. This binary runs as `migrator` — `CREATEROLE` and owner of the production database. Measured against the v2.107.0 release: it publishes a `checksums.txt` asset, but that manifest covers **only the versioned filenames** (`supabase_2.107.0_linux_arm64.tar.gz` → `d54648dd…`; `…amd64…` → `ea233b33…`) — the **unversioned alias this Dockerfile actually downloads is not in the publisher's own manifest**. Sec's own note: this gap shipped in chunk 1 (#741, which Sec reviewed) with no record of being raised then — possibly the second review it has survived.
- **AC.** Switch to the versioned tarball filename and verify a pinned digest (from `checksums.txt`) before extracting. **Do not make this change unverified** — first confirm the versioned tarball's contents match the unversioned one currently installed, then land as its own PR with its own build check. **Vehicle: Dockerfile edit-in-place. Owner: DevOps.**
- **Dependencies.** None. Explicitly NOT bundled into PR #753 (Sec: ordering an unverified integrity-check change into a path that has now broken twice is the wrong trade against a hotfix unblocking a hard-blocked 🔒 bootstrap).

**21. The migrator Dockerfile's build-time `supabase --version` self-check cannot observe whether the `supabase-go` companion binary is present — the class of defect that let PR #753's bug ship undetected is still open.** [DevOps; no milestone yet] — **PR open at #767**
- **Source.** Sec joint-review of PR #753 at `a2ce5484` (B-2). `supabase --version` passed while `supabase db push` was broken (missing `supabase-go`) — the instrument could not see the defect. The **instance** is now fail-closed by construction (the two-member `tar` extraction + two-target `chmod` in one `&&` chain), but the **class** is not: a future CLI release shipping a third co-located binary, or dropping `supabase-go` from a different archive layout, repeats this exactly.
- **AC.** Add a *behavioural* build-time check that actually crosses the shim→Go forward (e.g. `supabase db push --help`, or equivalent — chosen because `--version` provably does not). **Condition, stated by Sec: prove the check non-vacuous by strike before landing it** — rebuild with `supabase-go` deliberately removed and confirm the new check goes RED. A second vacuous self-check is worse than none. **Vehicle: Dockerfile edit-in-place. Owner: DevOps.**
  > ⚠ **The `supabase db push --help` suggestion in this AC is SUPERSEDED by the escalation below** — Sec declined to name a flag at PR #755 because the CLI's flag surface could not be verified, and an unverified check that does not cross the shim→Go forward would be vacuous in exactly the way this item exists to prevent. Build the escalation's predicate, not this example.
- **ESCALATED — Sec joint-review of PR #755 at `9a08a7ff`: this is now a CONDITION on the next PR touching `infra/supabase/migrator/**`, not a standalone booking.** The migrator image is built nowhere in CI today (`grep -rn 'docker build' .github/workflows/` → only `web-tests.yml:68`, `docker build api/`); three defects (build context #752, companion binary #753, template paths #755) have already shipped undetected through that gap. Required check: a CI job that builds the migrator image and runs `supabase db push` against a deliberately unreachable `--db-url`, passing ONLY if the failure is a connection failure — `Invalid config` or `Could not find the` = RED. Must be **strike-proven as a boundary pair on the named token**: a bare `rc != 0` is vacuous because the connection failure itself is non-zero (would pass either way).
- **Dependencies.** None for the original booking; the escalation blocks the next `infra/supabase/migrator/**`-touching PR from merging without this CI job in place.

Authenticated-JWT smoke variant still open (needs a user token). Was: Production `PGRST_DB_SCHEMAS` gains `pfin` as the exact literal `public,graphql_public,pfin` (`public` FIRST), restoring [ADR-023](DECISIONS.md#adr-023)'s ratified Data-API exposure posture.** [DevOps + Sec; blocks step 7 (production Data API surface); no milestone yet] — **PR open at `feat/item22-pgrst-db-schemas-pfin`**
- **Source.** Sec joint-review of PR #753 at `a2ce5484` (C-1 fork, resolved by direct measurement 2026-09-14). Both the live Coolify env-store value and the running `rest` container's actual env read `public,graphql_public` — `pfin` absent. This contradicts `docs/deployment-runbook.md`'s stated assumption (`PGRST_DB_SCHEMAS=pfin` is correct) and `docs/records/v1final/standup-log.md` §5f, both of which assume `pfin` is exposed through the Data API. `scripts/provision-supabase-stack.sh`'s `NONSECRET_DEFAULTS` (check-if-absent) sets `public,graphql_public`, matching the measured value — so the live value was never hand-overridden to `pfin` as the runbook assumed.
- **Ruling paper.** `docs/records/v1final/standup-preconditions.md` §I.1 (PR #821, merged `3eec79d0`). Sec's measurement sharpened the framing from "an open architectural choice" to "a live functional defect": `api/` reaches `pfin` exclusively via PostgREST (127 call sites, 45 files, zero direct-Postgres fallback), so every one of those paths 3F000/`PGRST106`s against production today. **Option A (never expose `pfin`) is a Sec VETO** — it reverses ADR-023, a ratified, Sec-conditioned, one-way-door decision. **Option B (this item) is Sec's recommendation and F/CTO's ruling** — RLS is, and per ADR-023 was always designed to be, the Data API's **only control** on that path (not defense-in-depth behind something else, because nothing else exists in `api/`). ⚠ **The correction target is `public,graphql_public,pfin`, NOT bare `pfin`** — PostgREST's first listed schema becomes its default `Accept-Profile`, so `pfin` alone (the runbook's original literal) would un-expose `public`/`graphql_public`; Sec vetoes that literal by name.
- **AC (as ruled).** Flip production's `PGRST_DB_SCHEMAS` to `public,graphql_public,pfin`, correct the three named carriers (`deployment-runbook.md`, `standup-log.md` §5f + its Departures row, `provision-supabase-stack.sh`'s `NONSECRET_DEFAULTS` + "expected unhealthy" branch) to the literal, **and satisfy Sec's five flip conditions before/around the flip**: **B-1** (VETO trigger) measure ADR-023 C2 (`anon` zero-grant) against the **production** database, not CI, before flipping; **B-2** applied migration count = 120; **B-3** `025_aal2_step_up_backstop.sql` applied; **B-4** re-affirm (not silently inherit) item 26's `sslmode=disable` ruling now that more tenant data crosses that hop; **B-5** correct the carriers, framed as a restoration. Ship **two fence halves**: a CI literal-match check (`scripts/ci/fence-pgrst-schemas-pfin.sh`, own sentinel, order-sensitive golden fixtures) and a **production-observable** post-deploy assertion (`scripts/ci/fence-pgrst-schemas-live.sh`, wired into `provision-supabase-stack.sh`'s Verification battery) — the CI half alone is insufficient because `NONSECRET_DEFAULTS` is mint-if-absent and cannot correct an already-set Coolify store value. Numbered operator procedure at `docs/deployment-runbook.md` §6.9. **Owner: DevOps, Sec joint-review mandatory before merge** (fenced surface + ADR-023's exposure).
- **Dependencies.** Must resolve before step 7's smoke checklist / reachability checks (`production-standup.md` step 10) — those checks assume a specific `PGRST_DB_SCHEMAS` shape; see item 64 below (that checklist has no explicit criterion for this specific defect class).

**23. ✅ DONE — PR #756 MERGED 2026-09-14. `scripts/mint-supabase-jwt-keys.sh` carried the same unquoted-heredoc mechanism as #754's item 19, on the heredoc that mints `ANON_KEY`/`SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` — plus `push-production-secrets.sh:556` (latent, same shape). Sec's execution gate is DISCHARGED — both scripts are cleared to run against production.** [DevOps; was execution-gated (Sec, PR #754 GREEN with the gate, not a merge gate); no milestone yet] — **PR #756, merged 2026-09-14**
- **Source.** Sec joint-review of PR #754 at `6c5128b0` / `bc8fc661`. `mint-supabase-jwt-keys.sh` has five unquoted `sshx_in <<REMOTE` heredocs (~:272, :307, :344, :395, plus `<<REMOTE2` ~:598) — the same mechanism #754's item 19 fixed in `provision-supabase-stack.sh`: the LOCAL shell performs command/variable substitution on the whole body before it reaches SSH. The heredoc at ~:395 mints `ANON_KEY`/`SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` and carries ~60 backtick characters in its own documentation comments (the "BUG 3 FIX" cluster: `` `docker restart` ``, `` `finished` ``, `` `--apply` ``). Unlike item 19's `migrator` bareword (a clean `command not found`), the mechanism here **deletes the backtick span** from the body that actually runs on the box — a future edit that drops a `umask`, a `chmod 600`, or an overwrite line inside a backtick-quoted comment would vanish from the rendered remote script with no trace in the script's own success output, since the local shell's command-substitution failure is silent-to-empty when the barewords don't happen to collide with a real local binary (and NOT silent, but actively dangerous, when they do — `docker restart` with no arguments IS a real local command). `push-production-secrets.sh:556` shares the identical unquoted-delimiter shape (latent — no backticks in its body today).
- **AC.** Fix both with the same pattern as #754: quote the delimiter (`<<'REMOTE'` / `<<'REMOTE2'`), move intentional host-side interpolations to `env VAR=value` on the ssh argv, confirm every moved value is non-secret. Produce the full before/after rendered-body diff for every heredoc, secrets elided. Retrospective: determine what, if anything, was dropped from the ~:395 heredoc in the run(s) that produced the live production `ANON_KEY`/`SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` — identify that run's exact `mint-supabase-jwt-keys.sh` committed sha via `docs/records/v1final/standup-log.md` and the PR #731/#734/#736 history, and record the finding (what was dropped, or "nothing dropped, measured") as a dated note under standup-log.md Step 5. **Execution gate stands regardless of this PR's merge status** — Sec's ruling is that the script must not run against production until it carries the fix, not until the fix merges to `main`. **Owner: DevOps.**
- **Dependencies.** Blocks any future `mint-supabase-jwt-keys.sh --apply` / `--verify-live` run against production. Independent of item 22.
- **Resolution (corrected 2026-09-19).** This item's header stood as "PR open at #756" past that PR's actual merge (2026-09-14) — stale, corrected here. `docs/records/v1final/standup-log.md` §5h carries the retrospective: no recorded live `--apply` execution of the pre-fix script exists in this repo, so there is nothing repo-evidence can show was dropped; the one live run this repo does record (PR #736's `--verify-live` probes) hit a heredoc with zero backtick characters at that sha, so it had nothing to drop either way. Both AC halves (the quote fix and the retrospective) are discharged.

**24. ✅ RULED 2026-09-19 (F/CTO) — Option 1: ship V1 with stock GoTrue emails; NOT a step-7 blocker.** [DevOps + Sec; F/CTO ruling; no milestone yet] — **PR at `chore/item24-config-toml-templates-comment`, merged**

Production `auth` service sets `GOTRUE_MAILER_AUTOCONFIRM` and four `GOTRUE_MAILER_URLPATHS_*` vars and no `GOTRUE_MAILER_TEMPLATES_*` vars, and no mount of `supabase/templates/` into the container. GoTrue falls back to its own stock templates when `GOTRUE_MAILER_TEMPLATES_*` is unset. **Not an exposure** — same class as item 22 (dev/prod config divergence), not a security defect.
- **Ruling paper.** `docs/records/v1final/standup-preconditions.md` §I.2 (PR #821, merged `3eec79d0`). Sec graded this a **product gap, not an exposure**. Sec corrected the framing twice: (i) **recovery and invite emails stay live** under this record's Q5 signup-off ruling, not just confirmation as originally framed; (ii) `supabase/config.toml` customises confirmation/recovery/magic_link only — **no `invite` template exists anywhere on the tree** — so any future branded-template build must scope from GoTrue's five `GOTRUE_MAILER_TEMPLATES_*` env vars, never from `config.toml`.

- **Dependencies.** Sec's explicit instruction: **rule together, ship separately** — item 22 (`feat/item22-pgrst-db-schemas-pfin`, PR #822) is a separate PR, same class of defect, different severity.

**25. `mint-supabase-jwt-keys.sh` (and `record-coolify-uuids.sh:71`) put `SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`/`ANON_KEY`/`JWT_SECRET`/the Coolify API `TOKEN` into on-box process argv — same class as #752 C-1, pre-existing, not introduced by PR #756 or PR #765.** [DevOps; returns to Sec; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** Sec joint-review of PR #756. `probe()`'s `-H "apikey: $key" -H "Authorization: Bearer $key"` puts `SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`/`ANON_KEY` into the on-box `docker` client argv; `python3 - "$TOKEN" "$JWT_SECRET" …` puts `JWT_SECRET` and the Coolify API token into the on-box `python3` argv. `/proc/*/cmdline` is world-readable by default, and runbook §1 step 3 mandates a separate non-root operator account on that box. Identical class to the shape Sec ruled BLOCKING at PR #752 C-1 — a credential in a `ps`-visible argv on the production box — and `SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` is the higher-value instance since it carries `rolbypassrls`. Verified unchanged by #756 (byte-identical behaviour before/after that PR's heredoc-quoting fix). **Widened by Sec joint-review of PR #765:** `scripts/record-coolify-uuids.sh:71`'s `api()` helper expands `curl … -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN"` inside the remote ssh command string, landing the Coolify API token in `curl`'s on-box argv — the identical pattern, mirroring the pre-existing `api()` helper in `provision-supabase-stack.sh` it was copied from. Not a regression, but a new file carrying the class; scoping this item's remediation to the file list that existed when it was written would miss it. Flagged, not fixed, in PR #765 — item 25's AC below now covers it explicitly so the eventual fix can't be scoped against a stale file list. **Widened again by Sec joint-review of PR #846 (N-6, W-2):** `scripts/db-role-handoff.sh` carries the identical `python3 - "$TOKEN" …` argv pattern at TWO call sites — the `api()` helper's own UUID-resolution call (~:272) and the step-D `PFIN_DB_PASSWORD` PATCH-push call (~:485), both copied from the same pre-existing shape this item already tracks. Flagged, not fixed, in PR #846 — same reasoning as the #765 widening above: a new file carrying the class, not a regression, and the eventual fix must not be scoped against a stale file list. **Widened again by Sec joint-review of PR #849 r2:** `scripts/assign-app-domain.sh`'s own `api()`-style UUID-resolution call (the `pfin-app` PATCH target lookup) carries the same `python3 - "$TOKEN" …` box-side argv pattern — flagged while confirming VETO-2's fix (which moved the PORKBUN keys off argv but did not touch this pre-existing Coolify-token site, a different credential, same class). `scripts/db-bootstrap.sh`'s own top-of-file `python3 - "\$TOKEN" "\$stack_name" "\$migrator_name" …` UUID-resolution call (used to resolve the `pfin-supabase-stack`/`pfin-migrator` uuids) is the identical pattern too — present in that file's new-this-round Phase 1 credential-handoff redesign (PATH A, VETO-1 r2), though that redesign also REMOVED a second instance of this exact class (leg D's own `python3 - "$TOKEN" "$MIGRATOR_UUID" "$SEED_FILE"` push call, which no longer exists after PATH A eliminated leg D entirely). Both new sites are the same pre-existing shape this item already tracks, not new defects; flagged, not fixed, here — same reasoning as every prior widening.
- **AC.** Pass secrets to `python3` on **stdin** (it already reads a `PYEOF` heredoc — add a first line) instead of argv; give `curl` the header via `--config -` / `-H @-` rather than argv. Apply the same `--config -`/stdin fix shape to `record-coolify-uuids.sh:71`'s `api()` helper, `db-role-handoff.sh`'s two call sites (~:272, ~:485), `assign-app-domain.sh`'s own UUID-resolution call, and `db-bootstrap.sh`'s own UUID-resolution call. **Owner: DevOps, returns to Sec** for review once fixed.
- **Dependencies.** None. Independent of items 21–24.

**26. ✅ RESOLVED — Migrator `supabase db push` TLS negotiation failure. `sslmode=disable` + `PGSSLMODE=disable` (belt-and-braces), Sec-ruled, F/CTO-ratified, confirmed working — bootstrap apply completed clean, all verifications passed.** [DevOps + Sec; PR #759; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** Standup step 6 Phase B.4, third bootstrap-apply attempt, 2026-09-14 — the first two Dockerfile packaging defects (`supabase-go` binary, `supabase/templates/**`) are both fixed and confirmed live on `main` `0fe09afa`. This is a fourth, categorically different failure: `supabase db push --db-url "postgres://postgres:<pw>@db:5432/postgres"` fails **before touching any migration or config file**, at the connection itself: `failed to connect to postgres: failed to connect to \`host=db user=postgres database=postgres\`: tls error (server refused TLS connection)`. Confirmed no state changed (`pfin`/`supabase_migrations` schemas still absent). Not diagnosed further per this task's own instruction — stop and report a CLI failure before connecting, for a new reason, rather than fix a fourth packaging defect unseen by Sec.
- **AC.** Diagnose why the Go-driver-backed CLI (post-#753) prefers/requires TLS against `db`, which offers none on its internal-network socket (every other worker — `pfin_etl`, `pfin_provider_sync` — connects to it in plaintext without issue). Candidate directions, **none evaluated or chosen yet**: an explicit `sslmode=disable` on the `--db-url` construction (runbook §6's stdin-piped form and/or the container's own `PROD_DB_URL`), a CLI flag, or accepting TLS with a self-signed/no-verify posture if `db` can be made to offer it cheaply. **Whichever direction is chosen returns to Sec joint-review before landing** — this is the explicit condition BACKLOG item 21 (Sec, PR #753) already set for the next migrator-image change, and this is that next change. **Owner: DevOps, Sec-consult mandatory.**
- **Dependencies.** Blocks Phase B step 4 entirely — no migration can be applied to production until this resolves. Highest-priority item in this set.
- **STATUS: RESOLVED — sslmode=disable ACCEPTED (Sec + F/CTO-ratified, 2026-09-14), landed and CONFIRMED WORKING, PR #759.** `sslmode=prefer` REJECTED; TLS-on-`db` REJECTED for V1 with a named re-open trigger (void if `db`/`supavisor` ever becomes reachable off-host). **First re-test with only the URL's `?sslmode=disable` query param did NOT take effect.** **Corrected 2026-09-14 (Sec C-1, PR #759):** the cause is **not** shell quoting and the query param did **not** "never survive" — the bootstrap's password-elided DSN echo shows `?sslmode=disable` present on the exact string the CLI received; it **reached** the CLI and was **not honoured**. Sec's original citation of `apps/cli/src/legacy/shared/legacy-db-config.parse.ts` is withdrawn (the legacy TS path; `db push` forwards to `supabase-go` instead, which resolves TLS from libpq env vars). **Fix: `PGSSLMODE=disable` is the mechanism of record**, set as an environment variable belt-and-braces alongside the URL param — both on the migrator service's compose `environment:` block and on the bootstrap `exec` call; never rely on the URL alone. Re-ran the bootstrap apply with the DSN echoed (password-elided) immediately before the call: connected, applied all 118 migrations clean, and every verification passed — `schema_migrations` head `118` / row count `118`; `pfin` schema present; `pfin_etl`/`pfin_provider_sync`/`migrator` all `rolcanlogin=f`; database owner still `postgres`; `rest` healthy. **Item 26 CLOSED.**

**27. Migrator's declared `environment:` block (1 var, `PROD_DB_URL`) does NOT confine the live container to that one credential — Sec's "C7 confinement-by-non-reference" property, cited in the credential-disclosure disposition, does not hold at runtime.** [DevOps + Sec; feeds ADR-072's C7 record; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** Found while verifying the `MIGRATOR_DB_PASSWORD` rotation (standup step 6 Phase B.4, 2026-09-14). Initial names/booleans-only measurement used the `docker compose exec -T migrator env` path — Sec correctly held this as contested (Coolify's own parser source can build a service's env from its declared block alone, so the exec path could in principle carry the project env into the exec'd shell without the container itself holding it). **Discriminator run to settle it:** `docker inspect --format '{{range .Config.Env}}...'` — the container's own `Config.Env` at `docker create` time, not the exec path — shows the **same set** (`migrator`: 67 names; `meta`: 75; sorted-diff against the exec path differs only by `HOME`/`HOSTNAME` shell artifacts). **Mechanism, now measured directly, not inferred:** Coolify deletes the build-time compose file post-deploy, but a **persistent, Coolify-materialized copy** exists at `/data/coolify/applications/<uuid>/docker-compose.yaml` (+ sibling `.env`, 61 keys, names only). Reading it directly: the rendered `migrator` block carries **`env_file: - .env`** (present on every service in the file) **and** an `environment:` block Coolify expanded from our source's single `PROD_DB_URL` to 12 names (`PROD_DB_URL` + 3 Coolify bookkeeping vars + 8 `SERVICE_NAME_*` discovery vars) — neither exists in our committed compose source. Same pattern confirmed on `meta` (7 declared in our source, 75 actual) — not migrator-specific, and not present in the upstream-mirroring source declarations, so this is Coolify's own rendering, not an upstream property.
- **Two halves Sec's c7-final for Architect carries that this item's original text omitted (added per Sec review of PR #759):** **(i) the both-ways half** — `MIGRATOR_DB_PASSWORD` is correspondingly present in **all eleven services** in this project, not merely the case of `migrator` holding too much. That is the direct negation of C7's text, and changes the shape of the residual: this is not "one service over-holds one credential," it is "every credential in the resource's env store reaches every service." **(ii) Amendment 1's PRESERVED claim is withdrawn** — ADR-072 Amendment 3 landed (PR #760, `f0a35484`) correcting ADR-072's C7 disposition; the prior "C7 preserved" framing (including in this PR's own §6 comment, corrected separately) no longer holds pending that amendment landing.
- **AC.** Determine whether this is configurable (a Coolify setting to scope env injection per-service) or a structural property of the `dockercompose` build pack that must be designed around. Correct ADR-072's C7 disposition (PR #741, corrected at [ADR-072](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) **Amendment 3**, PR #760 — Architect) to reflect the measured property (all eleven services, not just migrator) and the withdrawn PRESERVED claim, not the inferred one. Re-assess whether `MIGRATOR_DB_PASSWORD`'s confinement claim (and any other credential confinement claim resting on a service's declared `environment:` block alone) needs re-stating stack-wide. **Owner: DevOps + Sec** (ADR-072 is Sec-adjacent per D4/C7; Architect owns Amendment 3).
- **Dependencies.** None for the finding itself. The credential-disclosure incident this surfaced during is independently closed (rotation complete, confirmed) — this item is about the standing property, not that incident.

**28. Every service in the Supabase-stack Coolify application receives the project's entire env store via `env_file:` — one compromised container yields the whole stack.** [DevOps + Sec, F/CTO ruling; gates the §2/§9 DNS cutover, **NOT** Phase B; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** Sec's C7 live-env ruling, section (5), over DevOps's measurement at **PR #759 head `1726fae6`** (standup-log Step 6) — carried in Sec's words: *"**Every service in the Supabase-stack Coolify project receives the project's entire 61-key env store via `env_file:`, so each of the eleven containers holds `POSTGRES_PASSWORD`, `JWT_SECRET`, `SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`, `VAULT_ENC_KEY`, `ANON_KEY` and `SECRET_KEY_BASE`.** One compromised container yields the whole stack. **Not internet-reachable today** (`api-gw` `expose:`-only, no Domain in the compose; only Studio publishes, on `127.0.0.1:3000`) — **it becomes so at the §2/§9 DNS cutover, which is the deadline.** Upstream Supabase's own self-hosted compose passes secrets per-service precisely to avoid this; Coolify's dockercompose build pack overrides that."* **The mechanism, measured not inferred:** Coolify renders a persistent compose at `/data/coolify/applications/<uuid>/docker-compose.yaml` giving every service `env_file: - .env` against a sibling `.env` holding **61** keys, and expands the `migrator` service's declared `environment:` from **1** name to **12**; `docker inspect .Config.Env` names **67** on `migrator` and **75** on `meta`, with the `docker compose exec` delta `HOME`/`HOSTNAME` only. A service's declared `environment:` block does **not** bound its container environment.
- **AC.** Sec's, verbatim: *"determine whether Coolify 4.3.18 offers any per-service scoping at all (the request is out to DevOps and I expect the answer is no, since the injection happens outside `parseDockerComposeFile`); if not, the options are per-service Coolify resources, a secrets-manager sidecar, or documented acceptance with the DNS-cutover deadline attached."* **Owner: DevOps + Sec, F/CTO ruling.** Whether the measured exposure earns an RT catalog entry of its own is an F/CTO Decision-4 ratify act on a Sec proposal, taken on this item — not a deploy-time or Sec-authored act. The two `docs/deployment-runbook.md` confinement carriers are graded here as well: **§6 *Apply migrations*'s migrator Credential bullet** (confinement *"by non-reference"* — falsified outright by [ADR-072](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) Amendment 3) and **§5 *Secrets provisioning*'s per-surface `.env.example` bullet** (*"the enumeration **is** the confinement property"* — false for any service inside a multi-service Coolify application, unmeasured for the separately-deployed applications). Both are DevOps-owned in-place corrections and neither rides the Amendment 3 PR.
- **Dependencies.** **Blocks the §2/§9 DNS cutover — the deadline is the moment a Domain is attached to `api-gw`** — and blocks **nothing** in Phase B: the exposure is a function of the containers existing, which they already do. ⚠ **This is bigger than [ADR-072](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) and must NOT be settled inside its amendment.** Amendment 3 records the C7 falsification and **points here**; item 29's migrator-only remedy closes the ADR condition and closes **none** of this — `rest`, `auth`, `studio`, `meta`, `supavisor` and `db` each still hold every secret after it lands.
- ⚠ **`app`’s network attachment does NOT narrow this item** ([ADR-073](DECISIONS.md#adr-073), 2026-09-19). Re-creating `pfin-app` as a `dockercompose` resource attached to the stack’s Docker network by an `external:` network changes the stack’s *network* membership, not its *env injection*: `app` is a separate Coolify application with its own env store, so `rest`, `auth`, `studio`, `meta`, `supavisor` and `db` each still hold every secret in the stack’s store. Owner, F/CTO ruling and the §2/§9 DNS-cutover gate are unchanged.

**29. ✅ DONE 2026-09-19 (all 13 steps; step 13 = runs 35467316084 RED-on-stale-image then 35467590683 GREEN after a redeploy — the sha gate proven both ways against the new resource; item 59 is what removes the hand redeploy) — record in `standup-log.md` § "§6.8 … CUTOVER EXECUTED, 2026-09-19"; both Amendment-4 UNMEASURED questions now MEASURED (mechanism (b) works; DELETE removes the key from the rendered env). Was: Remedy for the falsified C7 confinement — `migrator` moves to its own Coolify resource, with the `MIGRATOR_DB_*` names REMOVED from the stack's env store.** [DevOps + Sec (Sec joint-review mandatory); **design RATIFIED 2026-09-16, sentinel shape (1); implementation PR pending** — [ADR-072](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) **Amendment 4** (design PR #769) rules the topology (standalone `dockercompose` resource), the credential channel (on-box mint into its own store; `SECRET_RESOURCE_MAP → migrator` forbidden), rotate-at-cutover, and a SIBLING fence under the migrator compose's OWN sentinel (unlabeled until an F/CTO Decision-4 RT ratify). The build is AUTHORIZED under that amendment's named merge conditions — `MINT_SECRETS`/`NONSECRET_DEFAULTS`/assert-array removal plus a strike-proven post-move assertion, strike-proven fixtures, and an old-credential-fails proof; does **NOT** block the bootstrap; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** Sec's C7 live-env ruling, section (3), preference **(b)**, following the falsification recorded at [ADR-072](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) Amendment 3 (2026-09-14). This is **C7's original topology, which Amendment 1 abandoned** — reverting to it is not a retreat, it is the amendment being falsified by measurement. Alternatives Sec examined and rejected: **(a)** per-service env scoping in Coolify — the injection happens outside `parseDockerComposeFile`, so there is no scoping knob to build on (its existence is item 28's AC, not an assumption here); **(c)** accept-and-record — rejected for the migrator, because accepting means recording that a DDL-applying service permanently holds the cluster superuser password when a bounded remedy exists.
- **AC.** The remedy PR must: move `migrator` out of the Supabase-stack compose into **its own Coolify resource** so it gets its own env store; attach it to the stack's project network by the pattern `docs/deployment-runbook.md` §7 already documents for the workers; restore the `push-production-secrets.sh` `SECRET_RESOURCE_MAP → migrator` entry C7 originally specified, reversing Amendment 1's `MINT_SECRETS` route; **remove `MIGRATOR_DB_PASSWORD` and `MIGRATOR_DB_USER` from the stack's env store** so they leave the shared `.env` — ⚠ **this half is what actually restores C7; the move alone does not, if the names stay behind**; and **prove it by measurement, not by declaration: `docker inspect .Config.Env | cut -d= -f1` on the new container, showing the credential present and the stack's other secrets absent.** ⚠ **A declared `environment:` block is exactly the evidence that failed here and must not be offered again.** `secrets-manifest.yml` is a standing joint-review-mandatory surface, so this returns to Sec before merge; [ADR-072](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) Amendment 1's three Sec-gated additions (i)–(iii) are re-stated to the restored topology in the same PR.
- **Dependencies.** **Does NOT block the bootstrap or the TLS fallback (item 26)** — Sec's ordering argument is that the exposure is a function of the migrator container **existing**, not of the bootstrap **running**; it is already up and already holds the full set, so delaying Phase B buys zero risk reduction and costs schedule. Proceed in the current topology; the TLS fallback comes first because it is what unblocks anything at all. ⚠ **What this remedy does NOT fix:** it restores C7 as an ADR condition and does nothing for the stack-wide exposure — that is item 28, and this item must not be read as closing it. The F/CTO re-ratify of the re-opened standing-DDL-credential residual ([ADR-072](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) Amendment 3) is the decision point that may direct this item before the DNS cutover.

**30. `supabase db push`'s silent-drop of `sslmode` on `--db-url` is direction-blind — a future `sslmode=verify-full` would be ignored exactly as `sslmode=disable` was, a silent TLS downgrade with no error.** [DevOps + Sec; no milestone yet — numbering: items **28–29 now EXIST** directly above (Architect's ADR-072 Amendment 3 bookings, PR #760, raised by item 27's AC); the earlier "28–29 reserved" note is discharged]
- **Source.** Sec review of PR #759 (§7.36 item 26 follow-up), 2026-09-14. The migrator's bootstrap apply measured that `supabase db push` v2.107.0 accepts an `sslmode` query parameter on `--db-url` syntactically but does not honour it — confirmed by the password-elided DSN echo showing `?sslmode=disable` present on the string the CLI received, while the connection still refused TLS until `PGSSLMODE=disable` was set as an environment variable. **The direction of this drop was benign this time** (asked for less TLS, got more, refused rather than silently downgraded to plaintext). **The same mechanism in the opposite direction is not benign:** an operator who sets `sslmode=verify-full` on a future `--db-url` would get whatever the Go driver's own default resolves to, with no error — a silent authentication/encryption downgrade on a path an operator believed was verified.
- **AC.** Establish which component drops the `--db-url` `sslmode` parameter (the `supabase` shim, or `supabase-go` itself), and whether `PGSSLROOTCERT` (the CA-file counterpart) is honoured or suffers the same silent drop. **Binding constraint on any future TLS-on-`db` work** (Sec's re-open trigger from the original §7.36 item 26 ruling, if `db`/`supavisor` ever becomes reachable off-host): `PGSSLMODE`/`PGSSLROOTCERT` environment variables are the mechanism of record for this CLI's TLS mode — the `--db-url` query parameter must never be trusted alone to carry it. **Owner: DevOps + Sec.**
- **Dependencies.** None today (TLS-on-`db` is rejected for V1). Becomes binding the moment item 26's re-open trigger fires.

**31. `postgres` is falsely claimed to be this image's superuser at multiple sites across the tree, not just migration `118`'s header — widened per Sec review of PR #763, 2026-09-14.** [Architect (migration + ADR text) + Sec (SECURITY §4.2 entry); comment-only corrections, no DDL; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** Standup step 6 Phase B step 5, F/CTO-executed 2026-09-14. `docs/deployment-runbook.md` §6.3's own "as `postgres`" claim was corrected in place the same day after `ALTER DATABASE … OWNER TO migrator` failed as `postgres` (`ERROR: must be able to SET ROLE "migrator"`) and succeeded as `supabase_admin` (measured: `select rolsuper from pg_roles where rolname in ('postgres','supabase_admin')` → `f` / `t`). **Sec widened this item's scope on review of PR #763** — the falsified premise reaches further than `118`'s header, including into Sec's own artifact. **Full tree-wide sweep** (`grep -rniE 'postgres.{0,40}(superuser|super-user)' docs/ supabase/migrations/ scripts/ DECISIONS.md`), every hit:
  - `supabase/migrations/118_migrator_role.sql:50` — "The rejected simpler alternative is the `postgres` superuser" — **stale claim, in scope for this item's AC.**
  - `DECISIONS.md:93` (ADR-072 Decision text, pre-Amendment-3) — "The `postgres` superuser (`POSTGRES_PASSWORD`, ...) is the rejected simpler alternative" — **stale claim, in scope.**
  - `DECISIONS.md:50` (ADR-072 Amendment 3, added 2026-09-14) — describes `migrator` holding `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` (cluster superuser) as a credential-class shorthand, not asserting the `postgres` *role* is superuser — already accurate, measured, **not in scope.**
  - `docs/deployment-runbook.md` (§6.0, §6.3 ×3, and the D4-tripwire note) — all **already corrected** by PR #763 itself (this PR); the D4-tripwire note's own "postgres-run apply" phrase is describing the historical/nominal ADR-072 model name, not asserting current superuser status — left as-is, matches ADR-072's own Decision 4 wording.
  - `docs/records/v1final/standup-log.md` (×5) — the as-executed record of the correction itself; accurately describes past-tense events, not a live claim.
  - `docs/local-dev.md:63`, `scripts/db-snapshot.sh:169`, `scripts/db-template-build.sh:232` — **already correct**, describe the *local* dev stack and already say `postgres` is NOT the real superuser there.
  - `docs/records/v15-execution/self362-close-gate-verdict.md:19`, `docs/records/v15-execution/log.md:230` — historical execution logs describing a *local* Postgres role correctly as non-superuser; not live claims, **not in scope.**
  - ⚠ **`docs/SECURITY/index.html:81` (§4.2, `studio-admin-console` entry) — Sec's own text, states `postgres` is "the `pfin` object owner and superuser-equivalent on this image" and "can ... set `session_replication_role`."** **Setting that GUC requires actual superuser, and `postgres` measures `rolsuper=f`** — false, in the safe direction (the console is *less* dangerous than the entry claims; the ownership-based claims and the `vault.decrypted_secrets` read are unaffected). **Routes to Sec — not edited by this item, not edited in PR #763.** Not required before any merge; flagged so it is not left unowned because this item's title once scoped to one file.
- **AC.** Per this repo's own migration-editing convention, a stale claim in a migration file's `--` header is a comment-only correction (no DDL change) — `118`'s header: update references from `postgres` to `supabase_admin` (or generically "a true superuser role on the deployed image," if a future image's superuser name might differ). `DECISIONS.md:93`: correct the pre-Amendment-3 ADR-072 Decision text the same way, at the next ADR-072 touch (Sec: no amendment required for a wording-only correction). `docs/SECURITY/index.html:81`: **Sec's to correct**, not DevOps's or Architect's — the `session_replication_role` claim needs retraction/correction; the ownership-based claims stay. **Owner: Architect** (migration + `DECISIONS.md`) **+ Sec** (SECURITY §4.2).
- **Dependencies.** None. Independent of items 28–30.

**32. ✅ RESOLVED PENDING PHASE D PROOF — `migrator` lacked `USAGE` on the `supabase_migrations` schema it did not own. Fixed by owner-transfer (F/CTO-executed 2026-09-14/15); `supabase db push` reports up to date with no permission error.** [Architect + DevOps; F/CTO ran both ALTERs; full confidence waits on Phase D's first real migration; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** Standup step 6 Phase B step 5 verification, 2026-09-14 (post `PGSSLMODE` fix, PR #759, confirmed live on `main`). Ran `docker compose exec -T migrator sh -c 'supabase migration list --db-url "$PROD_DB_URL"'` (the migrator's OWN credential, no override) to prove end-to-end auth without printing the password. **Connected** (TLS/auth succeeded — a SQL-level `42501` error is distinct from and postdates a connection/auth failure) but failed: `ERROR: permission denied for schema supabase_migrations (SQLSTATE 42501)`. Measured: `select nspname, pg_get_userbyid(nspowner) from pg_namespace where nspname='supabase_migrations'` → owner is `postgres`, not `migrator`; `select has_schema_privilege('migrator','supabase_migrations','USAGE')` → `f`. Owning the *database* (confirmed: `migrator` per §6.3's own verify block) does not grant schema-level privileges on a schema owned by a different role. **The bootstrap apply that landed migrations 1–118 ran as the `postgres` superuser override (runbook §6's bootstrap form), never as `migrator` itself** — so `migrator`'s own credential has never actually been exercised for a real apply, and this is the first time it was tried for anything (a read-only `migration list`).
- **RULED (Sec review of PR #763, 2026-09-14): owner-transfer, NOT a `GRANT`.** `ALTER SCHEMA supabase_migrations OWNER TO migrator;` plus one `ALTER TABLE … OWNER TO migrator;` per table in it (today: `schema_migrations` only — measured 2026-09-14: `\dn+ supabase_migrations` → owner `postgres`; the schema's only object is `schema_migrations` (relkind `r`) + its PK index, both owned by `postgres`; no sequences, no functions). **Note first what actually happened: ADR-072 Decision 4's by-design tripwire fired, for real, exactly as designed** — an operation failed against the bounded role, forcing a supervised pass rather than silently widening it; the response must not be to make the role broader. **Why ownership, not a GRANT set:** a GRANT list is a partial enumeration that rots the first time `supabase-go` touches an object nobody granted — failing the *unsupervised* Phase-D apply at the worst possible moment, with no operator watching (exactly what ADR-072 D6 exists to avoid before steady-state is trusted). **Why it costs nothing in blast radius:** `supabase_migrations.schema_migrations` is an idempotency ledger, not a security control (ADR-072 D3 puts fail-closed in the Scheduled Task's exit status, never in the ledger) — a role that already applies arbitrary DDL to this database gains no new capability by owning it; the split ownership is a bootstrap-order accident (`postgres` created the schema before `migrator` existed), not a designed boundary. **Must run before Phase C/D**, as a fourth supervised §6.3 statement (not a later patch) — written into `docs/deployment-runbook.md` §6.3 step 4 in this PR, for F/CTO to run. **Proof required is the WRITE verb, not the read verb:** `migration list` (used to surface this finding) is a READ and is NOT sufficient proof — after step 4 runs, `supabase db push --db-url "$PROD_DB_URL"` from the migrator container must report up to date with no permission error. **Even that is not the true write proof** — Phase D's first real migration (119), applied unsupervised as `migrator` and watched, is. **Owner: F/CTO executes (supervised §6.3); DevOps does not run the ALTERs.**
- **Dependencies.** Blocks Phase C/D — must be run in the same supervised pass as the rest of §6.3, before any CI-triggered unsupervised apply.
- ⚠ **REOPENED ON THE PROOF LEG, 2026-09-16 ([ADR-072](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) Amendment 6).** The heading above is left as written; what is corrected is the belief that the proof had been obtained. **The Phase D proof this item is "pending" NEVER HAPPENED.** Three trigger fires executed `db push` in a container whose baked-in `supabase/migrations/` predates the merged set, so they applied nothing and reported SUCCEEDED honestly — **a `db push` that applies nothing performs no `supabase_migrations` write and therefore exercises the schema-ownership fix not at all.** The fix may well be correct; **it is untested on the box**, and so are the swept `pfin_owner` ownership and every `055`/`116`/`117`/`118`/`119` guard's on-box branch. **Closes only on a fire that demonstrably delivers a migration** — Amendment 6 (C)'s delivery assertion.
- ✅ **CLOSED 2026-09-17 — and the closing criterion above was WRONG, which is recorded rather than quietly replaced.** The line above said this closes only on a fire that delivers a migration. **It closed on the supervised re-bootstrap instead**, which applied `001`–`119` by hand: `supabase_migrations.schema_migrations` now carries **119 rows, top row `119`, owned by `migrator`** — a real ledger write by the migrator identity, which is exactly the proof this item wanted. ⚠ **The criterion conflated the proof with one route to it.** A Phase D fire would have proved it; so did an operator. **The ownership fix is measured and this item is done** — Phase D delivery remains open at item **45**, and that is a different claim.

**33. The runbook uses `<box-ip>` (18×) and `<app-uuid>`/`<supabase-stack-app-uuid>` (11×) with no sentence saying where a stranger gets them, nothing records them, and no script prints a filled-in command — "the runbook must serve a stranger" standing directive, violated.** [DevOps; PR open at #765; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** F/CTO, 2026-09-15. Every §6 interactive vehicle asks the reader to substitute a placeholder they were never told how to find, from a record that never wrote the value down. A stand-up run by an agent that already knows the box IP and the Coolify resource UUIDs is not evidence a stranger could reproduce it.
- **AC.** (1) `provision-vps.sh` writes `BOX_IP=` into the repo-root gitignored `.env` (idempotent, update-in-place, names-only prints); a new `scripts/record-coolify-uuids.sh` queries the Coolify API over the tunnel by resource **name** and writes `MIGRATOR_SERVICE_UUID=` / `APP_UUID=` / `MIGRATOR_TASK_UUID=` the same way — §6.4 step 3 already expects these three in `.env`; this makes it one convention instead of a silent assumption. (2) `scripts/db-shell.sh` — reads `.env`; `--as supabase_admin|postgres` opens the filled-in `ssh -t ... exec -it db psql` vehicle; `--print` emits the command without running it; `--migrator-url` runs the §6.0 Step 0.2 echo, gated by Sec's OPERATOR-ONLY control (refuses unless both stdin/stdout are a TTY and `--i-am-a-human` is passed, so an agent cannot run it into a transcript). (3) Runbook §6.0/§6.1–§6.3/§6.5 show the helper invocation first, then the raw form, with one sentence per placeholder saying where the value comes from. **Owner: DevOps.**
- **Dependencies.** None. Independent of items 28–32.

**34. `classify_sudo_check_output()` (the ADR-072 C1 verification parser, `provision-vps.sh`) has no committed strike test — the three-outcome proof it correctly classifies HAS_SUDO / NO_SUDO / UNKNOWN_USER lives only in a PR body, once, on one machine.** [DevOps; Sec-flagged, non-blocking; PRs #771 + #772; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** Sec joint-review of PR #771, FLAG 2. The diff was `provision-vps.sh` + `standup-log.md` only — no test file. Sec accepted this for merge because the parser fails closed by construction (an unrecognized output classifies `INDETERMINATE` → `die`, never a silent pass), so a future `sudo` wording change turns the check RED, not vacuously green — the opposite of the defect this PR fixed. Not required before merge; booked as a follow-up.
- **AC.** A committed test (shell or pgTAP-adjacent harness, whichever this repo's script-testing convention lands on) that feeds `classify_sudo_check_output()` four fixture strings and asserts the return: the two real captured/synthesized outputs from PR #771 (`"User ci-migrate is not allowed to run sudo on pfin-prod-1."` → `NO_SUDO`; `"User ci-migrate may run the following commands on pfin-prod-1:\n    (ALL : ALL) ALL"` → `HAS_SUDO`), plus `"sudo: unknown user: ci-migrate"` → `UNKNOWN_USER`, plus one garbage string → `INDETERMINATE`. **Must also cover the `id -nG` disallowed-group sibling check (the NO_SUDO branch's `sudo`/`wheel`/`adm`/`docker`/`staff`/`disk` loop) — a `staff` fixture specifically** (Sec follow-up fold: `staff` is Debian's write path into `/usr/local`, where the C5 `migrator-orchestrate.sh` artifact lives, so membership defeats C5 with no sudo and no `docker`) plus a clean-groups fixture. **Folded from PR #772's Sec joint-review (FLAG, non-blocking): also assert `migrator_token_leak_exit_trap`'s presence — a committed watcher that `trap migrator_token_leak_exit_trap EXIT` is installed before the migrator-trigger token mint (grep the source, or a harness that deletes the `trap … EXIT` line and confirms the suite goes RED)** — PR #772's `kill -TERM` proof that the leak-check trap fires is narrative only (no test file in that diff), a weaker fail-safe than #771's parser (which `die`s on any unrecognized input by construction). **Owner: DevOps.**
- **Dependencies.** None. Independent of items 28–33.

**35. Production box has `/var/run/reboot-required` set (4 packages upgradable) — no scripted OS-patch/reboot procedure exists yet.** [DevOps; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** F/CTO's `provision-vps.sh --apply` run, 2026-09-16 (Phase C). Security updates went from 12 → 4 packages upgradable across two runs; the remaining 4 require a reboot. Coolify 4.3.21 is pinned upstream vs. 4.3.18 installed — deliberately not auto-upgraded by this script, an operator decision, not a defect.
- **AC.** Confirm read-only before any reboot: all Supabase-stack service containers (`db`/`auth`/`rest`/`migrator`/`meta`/`studio`) and `coolify-proxy` carry Docker restart policy `unless-stopped` (verified 2026-09-16 — they do); Coolify's own `coolify`/`coolify-db`/`coolify-redis`/`coolify-realtime` carry `always` (verified). None manually stopped, so a reboot should self-heal under existing policy with no script changes needed — this item is about giving F/CTO a documented, scripted reboot step (runbook + `provision-vps.sh` awareness of `/var/run/reboot-required`), not about the containers' own resilience, which is already sound. **Check whether Linear SELF-397 ("V1 ops-lifecycle: OS patching + Coolify/Postgres upgrades") already scopes this before creating new Linear work** — liaison check dispatched 2026-09-16, result pending at time of writing; if SELF-397 covers it, fold there instead of duplicating. **Owner: DevOps.**
- **Dependencies.** None.

**36. Enumerate, against the LIVE CATALOG, everything `001`–`118` created outside `pfin` — precondition on any wipe, and a file survey does NOT discharge it.** [DevOps + Sec; blocks the re-bootstrap; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** Sec's blocking precondition on the wipe ([ADR-072](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) Amendment 5 Decision B), in Sec's own reasoning: *"you cannot approve a wipe whose residue nobody has listed"* — the same argument that rejected `REASSIGN OWNED BY`. `drop schema pfin cascade` leaves non-`pfin` objects behind, still owned by the bootstrap role, and the re-apply then either conflicts or silently leaves an orphan **that by-construction does not fix**.
- **AC.** A catalog census (not a grep over migration files) of every object outside `pfin` owned by the bootstrap role in the app database, plus every `alter default privileges` and every `grant … to postgres` recorded in the live ACLs. **If the list is empty, say so AS A MEASUREMENT.** Each non-empty item gets a disposition before any wipe. ⚠ **Architect's file survey found none of these in the migration sources — that is evidence about the FILES and is not the census this item asks for.** Pair with the zero-rows gate: `pfin` tables **and `auth.users`** must both be measured empty; **if either is non-empty, STOP and route to Sec.** **Owner: DevOps + Sec.**
- **Dependencies.** Blocks the re-bootstrap. Independent of item 39.
- ✅ **DISCHARGED FOR THIS BOX, 2026-09-17.** The wipe and re-bootstrap it gated have **run**, and the census it required was performed against the live catalog as part of that pass: Step 4 reports **every** `pfin` object `pfin_owner`-owned — **34 sequences / 97 indexes / 39 relations / 7 views / 116 functions** — with the `supabase_migrations` ledger at **119 rows, top row owned by `migrator`**, and vault `SELECT` **`f`** for both `pfin_owner` and `migrator`. ⚠ **Discharged FOR THIS BOX, not retired as an item.** Its own AC is a precondition on *a wipe*; a future wipe of a different box re-arms it, and the numbers above are this box's census, not a standing property of the design.

**37. Every box-side file one script writes and another script reads needs a contract test that exercises the read — the migrator-token writer/reader agreement was never exercised until the first live fire, and it was wrong.** [DevOps; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** 2026-09-16 incident: `/etc/pfin/migrator-coolify-token.env` held the bare token value with no `NAME=` prefix (a stale write predating the current mint format); `migrator-orchestrate.sh` `source`d it under `set -a`, executing the value as a shell command and printing it to F/CTO's terminal — the third credential-into-transcript instance in this workstream. `provision-vps.sh` (the writer) and `migrator-orchestrate.sh` (the reader) had agreed in source for some time, but nothing ever exercised that agreement against the box's actual file until a real trigger fire — a class of defect a name-only round-trip check would have caught at mint time, not at first use.
- **AC.** For every box-side writer/reader pair in this family (at minimum: `provision-vps.sh` writing `/etc/pfin/migrator-coolify-token.env` + `migrator-trigger.conf`, `migrator-orchestrate.sh` reading both), a committed contract test that: (1) exercises the writer's actual write path (or a synthetic file in its exact shape) and (2) exercises the reader's actual parse path against it, asserting the expected variable(s) come back non-empty — not merely that the two files' literal strings match by inspection. `--rotate-migrator-token`'s read-back-by-name assertion (landed in the 2026-09-16 incident fix, PR `fix/migrator-token-file-contract`) is the runtime half of this; this item is the committed, CI-run half. **Owner: DevOps.**
- **Dependencies.** None.

**38. `118_migrator_role.sql`'s header asserts "`rolcanlogin` is FALSE at migration time and TRUE only in a provisioned environment" — no longer accurate as an assertion under the from-scratch re-apply order.** [Architect; comment-only correction, no DDL; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** DevOps, drafting the §6.3 by-construction rewrite (2026-09-16, F/CTO-ruled ownership-by-construction, Sec-accepted). Under the new order, `supabase_admin`'s pre-step runs `118` once (creating the role, `rolcanlogin=f`), then `migrator` applies the FULL 001–118 set from its own container, which re-executes `118`'s same file a second time — by then the role is already `LOGIN` (`rolcanlogin=t`), always, on every re-apply. `118`'s header states the FALSE-then-TRUE progression as a fact about "migration time" vs. "a provisioned environment," which was accurate under the old single-apply order but is no longer a meaningful distinction when `118` itself runs twice in the same bootstrap.
- **AC.** Reword the header line from an assertion to an observation — e.g., note that `rolcanlogin` reflects whichever pass of `118` last ran (first pass: `f`; the tolerant re-apply pass under `migrator`: `t`), rather than asserting a fixed FALSE-at-migration-time invariant. Comment-only, file-header edit in place (per this runbook's own migration-editing convention — no DDL, no new migration). Also confirm/word the guard's report-don't-repair posture explicitly tolerates "role exists, created by the pre-step, already LOGIN" while still hard-failing a genuine C8 attribute violation (Sec condition, migrator-ownership consult, 2026-09-16) — verify this is already true of `118`'s guard as authored, or add the tolerance if it is not. **Owner: Architect.**
- **Dependencies.** None. Independent of items 28–37.

**39. `pfin_owner` — the group role that owns every `pfin` object, entered by the PAIRED `set role pfin_owner;` / `reset role;` in every `pfin`-lane migration, with a strike-proven CI fence and an engine backstop.** [Architect (migration + battery) + DevOps (fence) + QA (fixtures); Sec joint-review mandatory; gated on [ADR-072](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) Amendment 5 Decision A being ruled; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** [ADR-072](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) Amendment 5 Decisions A + D + **F1** + **G2**. Sec ruled the mechanism class (enter the owner explicitly, **never** `ALTER ROLE … SET role`, because `116` leg (r12) already calls a per-role default a *"POSTURE BYPASS"*); Architect's measurements moved it from a dated gate to a **precondition**, because entering as `migrator` hits the `auth` and `vault` walls on the first `create table`. ⚠ **The mechanism was then CORRECTED at Decision F1: `set local role` is a measured SILENT NO-OP through `supabase db push` (`WARNING 25P01` — the CLI does not wrap a migration file in a transaction), and a bare `set role` LEAKS into the CLI's ledger INSERT and fails the push. Only the PAIRED form works.** This item's earlier text prescribed `set local role` and is corrected here.
- **AC.** A `pfin_owner` NOLOGIN, NOINHERIT role with **no password, no `CREATEROLE`** (Sec §2 — a creator receives ADMIN OPTION and `055`/`116` then grant an app role to what they create, a transitive path into `service_role`) and **no app-role membership**, owning every `pfin` object and holding **exactly** the out-of-`pfin` reach the ratified vault disposition requires — **under option (iii) (Architect's lean, both gates measured TRUE at Decision G1) that reach is `auth` only: `grant usage on schema auth` plus COLUMN-LEVEL `references (id), select (id) on auth.users` (Sec §G2 — table-level would hand every user row including `encrypted_password` to a role `PROD_DB_URL` reaches by `SET ROLE`), and NO vault grant of any kind, because invoker-semantics decrypt views leave the owner with no vault dependency at all.** ⚠ **RULED: F/CTO ratified disposition (iii) on 2026-09-16, so `pfin_owner` holds NO vault grant and the fallback below NEVER APPLIES.** Kept rather than deleted so the reader sees what lost: had (i) or (ii) been chosen, `pfin_owner` would additionally have needed `vault` USAGE + SELECT on `decrypted_secrets` + DELETE on `secrets` — **dead text, retained as the record of the road not taken.** ⚠ **BASIS CORRECTED 2026-09-16 — mirroring [ADR-072](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) Amendment 5 Decision H1, because carrying the falsified text here while DECISIONS carries the correction is exactly the half-a-mirror defect Sec has now flagged three times in this workstream.** (iii) was ratified on the ground that it *removes* the owner's vault dependency — *"wall 2 deleted"*. **Measured end-to-end through `supabase db push`: a view body is permission-checked at CREATE time regardless of `security_invoker`, so the creating role still needs vault privilege — wall 2 is NARROWED to CREATE time, not deleted.** The change is nonetheless **load-bearing**: ownership transfers to a vault-less `pfin_owner` without re-validating the body, but under the default the view would then execute as that vault-less owner and be **broken** — so `security_invoker = true` is a **COMPONENT** of the ratified **(iv‴)** shape, never an alternative to it. **Sec's §1 veto stands and is not withdrawn.** What remains true from the original basis: **nothing new is granted to anyone** — `service_role` already holds SELECT and DELETE on both `vault` relations in the image's own ACL (measured) — and Sec's tenant-gating reason is **withdrawn**, since `service_role` carries `rolbypassrls`. Membership is `grant pfin_owner to migrator with inherit false, set true;` and **no admin option** (the option is per-membership in PG 16+ and survives a later `ALTER ROLE migrator INHERIT`). Every `pfin`-lane migration opens with `set role pfin_owner;` as its first non-comment statement and closes with `reset role;`. **CI fence on the PAIR with FOUR strike legs** (Sec, Amendment 5 F1/G3/H5): missing opener → RED; opener without `reset role;` → RED; any statement after `reset role;` → RED; and the transaction-scoped variant anywhere → RED, the shape that looks right and does nothing. ⚠ **STRIP COMMENTS ON EVERY LEG, not only the last** — `057`/`107` carry pre-existing `--` prose about the app-runtime mechanism, and a raw predicate REDs them for being correct. ⚠ **Assert EXACT COUNTS, never presence:** naive `--.*$` stripping truncates a line whose string literal contains `--`, hiding a later hit; an exact-count predicate goes RED on that truncation, a presence predicate goes green. **ENGINE BACKSTOP, now the PRIMARY control** since `set role` is session-scoped with nothing to roll it back: pre-step `revoke create on schema pfin from migrator;`. pgTAP battery includes an (r12)-equivalent `rolconfig IS NULL` leg and the ownership watcher (every `pfin` object owned by `pfin_owner`), which **must run against the BOX before Phase C, not only in CI**. **Owner: Architect + DevOps + QA; Sec joint-review before merge.**
- **Dependencies.** **Gated on Amendment 5 Decision A** (ruled A2). ✅ **The vault-disposition ratify LANDED 2026-09-16 — (iii)** — so the AC's vault-grant fallback does not apply and this item's grant set is `auth`-only. ⚠ **Still gated on the dated Step 1.6 exception, which is NOT yet ratified.** Blocks the re-bootstrap.

**40. The `comment on role` statements in `055`/`116`/`117`/`118`/`119` and the four `grant <app_role> to <worker_role>` statements in `055`/`116` are permanently outside any bounded role's lane — they need a named SUPERVISED lane.** [Architect + Sec; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** [ADR-072](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) Amendment 5 Decision A, walls 3 and 4, measured by execution 2026-09-16. PG 16+ requires ADMIN OPTION to grant a role or to comment on it; the image creates `service_role`/`authenticated`, and **Postgres refuses to grant any role ADMIN OPTION on itself**, so `comment on role migrator` is unreachable for `migrator` under every grant.
- **AC.** Decide and document the supervised lane: which files it contains, how a reader tells the two lanes apart, and the fence that stops a role-graph statement landing in the bounded lane. Includes the `118`/`119` guard relocation under the dated one-time exception to `apply-migration` Step 1.6. ⚠ **Do NOT close this by granting ADMIN OPTION on the app roles** — that ends the role's boundedness and is refused in advance by Sec and by Amendment 5.
- **Dependencies.** Gated on Amendment 5 Decision A. Independent of item 36.

**41. `apply-migration` skill delta — the owner-privilege predicate, the `pfin`-lane rules, and the two-lane boundary.** [Architect; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** [ADR-072](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) Amendment 5 Decisions C + E, and Sec's §5.3 condition that the D9 table state a **predicate** rather than a list.
- **AC.** Add to `.claude/skills/apply-migration/SKILL.md`: (a) the owner-privilege predicate — **every object that executes with its OWNER's privileges**, `SECURITY DEFINER` functions **and** `security_invoker = false` views — replacing any `prosecdef`-only framing; (b) a `pfin`-lane migration opens with `set role pfin_owner;` as its FIRST non-comment statement and closes with `reset role;` as its LAST — the PAIRED form ruled at [ADR-072](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) Amendment 5 Decision F1, **never `set local role`, which is a measured no-op through `supabase db push`** — and may **not** reference a schema outside `pfin`, nor issue any role-graph statement (`create role`, `grant <role> to <role>`, `comment on role`) — those belong to the supervised lane; (c) the rule that a DEFINER function and every table it writes must be **co-owned**, with the `zero force row level security` basis named as a watched assumption; (d) a pointer to the dated one-time Step 1.6 exception so it is not read as general. **Owner: Architect.**
- **Dependencies.** Follows the Amendment 5 ratify; no build depends on it.

**42. REMOVE the dated Step 1.6 exception block from `apply-migration` once it expires — it is written to expire and nothing watches it.** [Architect; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** Architect's own bubble-up on PR #781, 2026-09-16. That PR added a dated one-time exception block at `.claude/skills/apply-migration/SKILL.md` Step 1.6, permitting edits to applied migrations `001`–`118` for the window F/CTO's disposability ruling opened. **The block states its own expiry and no mechanism enforces it.** An exception left standing past its window becomes exactly what it was written not to be — standing licence to edit applied migrations, which is the failure Step 1.6 exists to prevent, arriving with the rule's own authority behind it.
- **AC.** **Trigger: the re-bootstrap has landed AND one deployment has survived** (both, not either — a landed re-bootstrap that is then wiped again leaves the window open). On that trigger, a PR **deletes the dated block** from `.claude/skills/apply-migration/SKILL.md`, leaving Step 1.6 (A)/(B) governing unamended, and **cites this item** so the deletion is legible as the discharge of a booking rather than an unexplained removal. ⚠ **Do NOT soften the block into general guidance instead of deleting it** — a weakened exception that survives is worse than one that expires, because it reads as precedent. **Owner: Architect.**
- **Dependencies.** Gated on the re-bootstrap ([ADR-072](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) Amendment 5) completing and surviving. Nothing depends on it.

**43. Production-lane watcher for the (iv‴) supervised post-step — a box where `supabase/post-step-vault-view.sql` NEVER RAN is observed by nothing.** [DevOps (smoke gate) + Architect; Sec joint-review mandatory; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** Sec, #786 review 2026-09-17: the post-step's self-verifying block fails the step when it runs and says nothing about a box where it never ran; the CI pgTAP leg sees a CI database. Third instance of the CI-lane/production-lane asymmetry in the ADR-072 Amendment 5 workstream.
- **AC.** The §10 smoke gate (runbook §6.6 pass criteria / the post-deploy smoke) asserts on the production database: exactly one decrypt view `pfin.decrypted_source_credential`, owner `pfin_owner`, `security_invoker = true`, `service_role` SELECT true, anon/authenticated/PUBLIC SELECT false — the same predicates as the post-step's DO block, run from outside it. RED names the offender.
- **Dependencies.** The re-bootstrap (runbook §6.6) having run once; #786 merged.

**44. Least-privilege `meta` (Studio) role — the studio `postgres` identity CAN set `session_replication_role` (catalog-invisible privileged-settings extension), so the audit-class write prohibition is operator discipline, not construction.** [Architect + Sec; Sec joint-review mandatory; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** SECURITY §4.2 second correction, PR #785 `b15cab82` (Sec, 2026-09-17): the earlier "cannot" was inferred from `rolsuper = f` and `has_parameter_privilege = f`; measured, `postgres` sets the GUC via `supabase_privileged_role` membership. The remediation was booked in one prose sentence in `docs/SECURITY/index.html` and nowhere else.
- **AC.** A NOLOGIN→LOGIN `meta` role for Studio with no `supabase_privileged_role` membership, no BYPASSRLS, and grants scoped to what Studio needs; **behavioural probe, not a catalog read**: as the new role, `set session_replication_role = 'replica'` MUST fail; RT-31 leg (i) allowlist unchanged (Sec joint-review if any addition is proposed); runbook step for the Studio connection-string swap.
- **Dependencies.** ADR-072 Amendment 5 landed (done); Studio (§4) stood up on the box.

**45. Orchestrator: DELIVER the merged sha to the migrator before executing, and assert it — the trigger currently executes against a build-time snapshot.** [DevOps; Sec joint-review; PRECONDITION for Phase D]
- **Source.** [ADR-072](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) Amendment 6 (2026-09-16), (B) option (i) and its consequences, after three Phase D fires applied nothing and reported success. `infra/supabase/migrator/Dockerfile:99` bakes `supabase/migrations/` into the image at build; the stack resource was never redeployed after the build. ⚠ **[Decision 5](DECISIONS.md#adr-072)(1) already ratifies the required sequencing** — *rebuild the image → run the Scheduled Task → then deploy the app* — **so this is an unimplemented ratified requirement, not a new one.**
- **AC.** (1) The migrator image records the **triggering git sha** — a build-arg surfaced as a `LABEL` **and** a `/workspace/.migration-set-sha` file (measured: the Dockerfile has **no `LABEL`** today, and its two build-args name the CLI version and the platform, neither the migration set). (2) The orchestrator triggers a deploy of the **migrator resource** (`GET /deploy?uuid=<migrator-resource>`), waits for the rebuild, then **reads the RUNNING container's sha and REFUSES to execute the task on mismatch** — a hard fail. ⚠ (3) **A deployment-status poll, if kept, is a PRECONDITION only and its success is never evidence the migration applied** — Coolify marks a deployment FINISHED before its post-deploy command and swallows that command's failure, which is why [Decision 3](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) refuses that status as a gate; the sha assertion is the gate, and the Scheduled Task's exit status remains the migration-outcome gate. (4) `DEPLOY_ON_SUCCESS` unchanged. (5) A **delivery assertion** after a successful run: the `supabase_migrations` ledger's top row equals the newest migration file in the merged sha. (6) The `fail-probe` positive control stays as a **permanent, named, dated** task with `enabled` **false**, and the runbook says to verify `enabled` **by API read-back** — an API read showed it enabled despite the toggle. (7) Hops (d)/(e) exercised by a **swapped-conf** fire whose assertion is **the workflow step going RED**, then restore and verify by name; **config edit only — the token file is not touched.**
- **Dependencies.** ⚠ **Upstream: [ADR-072](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) Amendment 4 EXECUTED** (the migrator on its own Coolify resource) — **not optional here**: until then the only redeployable resource is the Supabase stack, and redeploying it **restarts the production database on every migration merge**, which is a path an operator will bypass, and the bypass reproduces exactly this defect. Downstream: Phase D proper; item 32's proof leg; the re-bootstrap **if** DevOps rules that it depends on Phase D (open — see Amendment 6 (D)).

**46. Clause-by-clause implementation walk of [ADR-072](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) and ALL its amendments — nothing in the record distinguishes a ratified clause from an implemented one.** [Architect + Sec; Sec's booked condition on the Amendment 6 GREEN]
- **Source.** Sec's condition on grading [ADR-072](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) Amendment 6 GREEN, F/CTO ratify 2026-09-17. ⚠ **The condition is a consequence of what Amendment 6 found, not a caveat on it.** [Decision 5](DECISIONS.md#adr-072)(1) ratified the sequencing *rebuild the image → run the Scheduled Task → then deploy the app*, and warned in its own words that applying on a stale image pushes a set without the new file. **The orchestrator never implemented it, nothing noticed, and it surfaced only as a symptom three trigger fires later** — three fires that applied nothing and reported success honestly. **The same class can be sitting in any other clause of this ADR right now.**
- **AC.** Walk **every ratified clause** of ADR-072 and of **each** of its amendments — Decisions 1–7 and Amendments 1–6, their sub-decisions included — and mark each one of exactly three ways: **IMPLEMENTED**, with the artifact named (file and, where it is a line-level claim, the line); **UNIMPLEMENTED**, with a `BACKLOG.md` booking created and cited; or **N-A**, with the reason stated. ⚠ **A clause may not be left unmarked, and "presumably implemented" is not one of the three** — the whole point is that presumption is what failed here. ⚠ **The walk asserts against the TREE and, where the clause is about the box, says so and marks it unmeasured rather than inferring** — several Amendment 6 findings are precisely clauses whose on-box behaviour has never been observed. Output lands as a record under `docs/records/`, not as prose inside ADR-072, **so an audit finding is not buried inside the document being audited.**
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: none — it reads the record and the tree, and blocks on nothing. Downstream: it will likely **create** bookings; item 45 (the orchestrator change) is the first one already known, and item 32's reopened proof leg is a second. **Not a gate on Phase D**, but it is the control that stops the next unimplemented clause from being found by a production symptom.

**47. The 40-hex sha regex `^[0-9a-f]{40}$` lives in two files (`infra/supabase/migrator/Dockerfile`, `scripts/migrator-orchestrate.sh`) with a comment saying "kept in sync deliberately" — a convention with no mechanism.** [DevOps; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** Sec, #790 pin at `a47e8c3f` (2026-09-17): the shape Sec flags in others — two literals, one comment, nothing watching.
- **AC.** One CI fence leg (in `security-scan.yml`'s existing fence family) asserting the two literals are byte-identical; golden with one drifted expecting RED. Or: one source (a shared file both read at build/run time) — state which and why.
- **Dependencies.** None.

**48. `/workspace/.build-sha` is written without a trailing newline (byte-compared) and `.build-sha-source` with one (human-read) — the asymmetry is correct, load-bearing, and undocumented at the second site.** [DevOps; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** Sec, #790 pin at `a47e8c3f` (2026-09-17). Anyone who later compares `.build-sha-source` programmatically will be bitten.
- **AC.** A comment at each write site in the Dockerfile naming the other file and why the newline differs; the orchestrator's read of `.build-sha` documented as byte-exact.
- **Dependencies.** None.

**49. `--yes` does NOT suppress the `supabase db push` confirmation prompt — the danger is the branch where the CLI DECLINES and exits 0, not the branch where it blocks.** [DevOps measuring; Architect + Sec on the disposition]
- **Source.** The supervised re-bootstrap, 2026-09-17: `db push` presented its confirmation prompt despite `--yes`. **The pass completed because an operator was present to answer it.**
- **Why it is not cosmetic, and ⚠ NOT for the reason first written here.** **Phase D has no operator**, so the prompt has two possible unattended branches and **they are not equally dangerous.**
  - **The BLOCKING branch is HANDLED, measured on `main` — this item's first draft said it was not, and that was wrong.** `scripts/migrator-orchestrate.sh` sets `POLL_INTERVAL_S=5` and `POLL_MAX_ATTEMPTS=120` — a **10-minute ceiling** — and on exhaustion fails with *"gave up after …s waiting for a terminal execution status … app deploy NOT triggered"*, **exit 1**. ⚠ **So a block IS converted to a RED at hop (d)**, at a **10-minute cost** per occurrence, with the residual that **the exec stays blocked inside the container** until the CLI gives up or the container restarts. Real, bounded, and visible.
  - ⚠⚠ **THE DANGEROUS BRANCH IS THE OTHER ONE: the CLI DECLINES on a non-interactive stdin and exits 0.** That applies nothing and reports success — **the exact vacuous-green shape of the three prior fires** ([ADR-072](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) Amendment 6 (A)), and the shape no status-watching gate can detect, because *applied everything* and *declined and applied nothing* are the same exit code. **A hang is loud and bounded; a silent decline is neither.** This item exists for the second branch.
- **AC.** Measure the **non-TTY** behaviour (the Scheduled Task execs without a terminal, which may itself suppress the prompt — that is the open question, not an assumption). Then either (a) demonstrate that the prompt does not appear without a TTY and record the measurement, or (b) name the flag/env that suppresses it and bake it into the task's command, or (c) if neither, book a timeout on the task so a hang becomes a `failed` rather than an indefinite `running`. ⚠ **Do not close this on the supervised pass's success** — the operator is what made that pass work, and the operator is exactly what Phase D removes.
- **Dependencies.** Blocks the Phase D delivery exercise from being trusted unattended. ⚠ **Independent of the sha assertion, which is BUILT (PR #790) — and distinct from the post-run DELIVERY assertion (ledger top row == newest migration file in the image), which is NOT built on `main`; a DevOps PR is in flight for it.** Item **45** stays open for the **exercise**, and this item is what would make that exercise's green trustworthy.

**50. Assert the `production-migrator` GitHub Environment still carries a required reviewer — deleting the environment or its reviewer silently un-gates the box-touching job.** [DevOps; Sec joint-review on the fence shape; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** Sec's third booking from the #791 review (ADR-072 Amendment 6 / #790 merge conditions): the `environment: production-migrator` key on `.github/workflows/migrator-trigger.yml`'s box-touching job only gates a `workflow_dispatch` run *if* the Environment currently carries a `required_reviewers` protection rule with at least one reviewer. Nothing in the repo asserts that state persists — a co-owner deleting the Environment, or removing its last required reviewer, in GitHub's Settings UI silently un-gates the box-touching job going forward. The workflow then runs exactly as if the `environment:` key were never added: no error, no warning, the YAML is unchanged, and the next `workflow_dispatch` (or push) fires the real SSH → forced-command → migrator-apply path against production with no approval step in front of it. This is a **drift** class, not a one-time configuration mistake — the same shape as the TimeZone-override drift item 45 (`§4.1`) documents for a different surface, and the same reasoning applies: a deploy-time check samples once; the vector here is "someone changes a setting in a UI, at any later time," not "someone misconfigures at setup."
- **AC.** A CI leg or a scheduled check that:
  1. Reads `GET repos/richmosko/mosko-fintech/environments/production-migrator` via the GitHub API (a `GITHUB_TOKEN`/PAT scoped to read repo environments — this is a read-only check, no write scope needed).
  2. Asserts the response's `protection_rules` array contains an entry of type `required_reviewers` with a non-empty `reviewers` list.
  3. **RED, by name, on either gap** — the Environment does not exist at all, or it exists but the `required_reviewers` rule is missing/empty. The failure message must name which of the two happened (a co-owner deleted the Environment vs. removed its reviewer are different repair paths) rather than a single generic "gate not verified."
  4. Runs on a cadence independent of any PR touching `migrator-trigger.yml` — this is drift detection, not a merge-time check; a PR that never touches this workflow is exactly the case this needs to catch (nobody re-reviews the Environment config when editing an unrelated file). A daily/weekly scheduled workflow (`on: schedule`) is the natural shape, matching item 45's own cadence reasoning for the TimeZone sweep — same tradeoff: tightening the cron tightens the detection window, it never closes it, and this must not be described as a gate (it is detection with bounded latency).
  5. On RED, routes to the same Coolify→Discord-adjacent (or equivalent CI-failure) notification path other scheduled fences in this repo already use — this is a production-security-relevant drift, not a routine test failure, and should not require someone to notice a red badge on a dashboard nobody watches.
