- **Owner: DevOps + Sec** (Sec joint-review mandatory on the check's own logic before it lands, matching every other change touching this Environment or the migrator-trigger surface — same standing rule as the `secrets-manifest.yml` / RT-26 allowlist class).
- **Not in scope for this item:** re-creating the Environment or its reviewer if this check finds them missing — that is a manual repo-settings repair, same as today; this item is the detector, not the auto-repair. Auto-repair of a GitHub Environment's protection rules from CI would itself be a privileged-write surface requiring its own Sec review, and is explicitly NOT proposed here.

**51. ⛔ THE TRIGGER IS WEDGED: both Amendment 6 assertions read the Docker socket and `ci-migrate` cannot — the next fire exits 3 before the task runs, with a message naming the wrong cause.** [DevOps measures (C); Sec grades (B); F/CTO rules the split]
- **Source.** [ADR-072](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) Amendment 7 (2026-09-17), on F/CTO's on-box C-1 measurement: `ci-migrate` is in groups `ci-migrate` and `users` only, `/var/run/docker.sock` is `root:docker` 660 with no ACL, `sudo -l -U ci-migrate` is not allowed, and a `docker compose … exec` as that user returns *"permission denied while trying to connect to the docker API"*. ⚠ **[C1](DECISIONS.md#adr-072)/[C5](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) HOLD — this is the confinement working, not failing.** Every earlier fire "worked" because the pre-#790 orchestrator only called the Coolify API over `localhost:8000` and never touched the socket.
- **AC.** ⚠ **(0) FIRST AND INDEPENDENT OF THE RULING: fix the exit-3 message.** Measured, it says *"either the image predates this marker (rebuild needed) or the container is not running"* — **neither is true in this failure**, and it will send an operator to rebuild a perfectly current image. It must name the permission case. **Do not wait for (1)–(3) to land this.** ✅ **(1) DISCHARGED — DevOps measured against Coolify v4.3.18's source: the execution's `message` field carries the combined stdout+stderr of the `docker exec`, 5 MB cap, returned verbatim.** ⚠ **(2) The sudoers option is NO LONGER PROPOSED and [C1](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) is NOT amended** — Amendment 7 (E) now recommends option (C) for **both** assertions, because `ci-migrate` can already cause a root `docker exec` via `POST …/execute` and reading that exec's output is strictly less than causing it. (3) **Build it:** the tagged run is a script **baked into the image** at `infra/supabase/migrator/pfin-task.sh`, and Coolify stores only **`sh /workspace/pfin-task.sh`** — [ADR-072](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) **Amendment 8**, F/CTO-ratified 2026-09-18, because the `command` column is `varchar(255)` and the tagged literal is 357 bytes. The script prints `.build-sha` and, after the push, `select max(version)` **as `migrator` under its own credential** (it owns the ledger schema), each on a **tagged line**; the orchestrator parses those lines from `message`. ⚠ **AC (4d)'s read-back is unchanged in kind and now compares the SHORT literal.** ⚠⚠ **The `rc=$?; … ; exit $rc` capture is LOAD-BEARING and must never be removed to save bytes: without it a failed push reports exit 0 (measured), and [Decision 3](DECISIONS.md#adr-072)'s gate reads that status. The prohibition belongs in the script and the task-doc Command row, not only in the ADR.** ⚠ **Two conditions ride the build: a BUILD-TIME existence-and-executable assertion on the copied script (or it inherits item 21's defect class), and on-box order IMAGE FIRST, COMMAND SECOND — switching the command first execs a missing file.** ⚠ **(4) Every parse failure — truncated, malformed, ABSENT or DUPLICATED tagged line — FAILS, never skips**, with the assertion's own exit code; the 5 MB cap makes truncation a real state. ⚠ **EXACTLY ONE anchored match per tagged line is required — zero OR two-or-more both fail closed** (Sec, #801): `db push` echoes migration **file names** into the same stdout, so a filename shaped like the tag injects a second candidate. Tag anchored at **line start**, sentinel that cannot occur in a migration filename, value taken only from a **fully-anchored** pattern — never a substring search over the blob. **Defence-in-depth, not a live path** (Architect authors those files), and cheap now / expensive to retrofit. ⚠ **(4b) The orchestrator logs ONLY the extracted tagged lines, never the raw `message` blob** (Sec standing condition) — that blob is combined output of a command whose argv carries the production database URL. ⚠ **(4c) BOTH post-hoc assertions must complete, fail-closed, BEFORE the `DEPLOY_ON_SUCCESS` branch is evaluated** — the delivery one already does (`:301` precedes `:321`); **the now-post-hoc SHA one must too, and a post-hoc sha gate reporting AFTER the deploy fired protects nothing at all.** ⚠⚠ **(4d) C5 ANCHOR UNDER THE EVIDENCE — MANDATORY, no longer conditional. MEASURED 2026-09-17 (DevOps, Coolify v4.3.18 source): the scope DOES reach.** `routes/api.php:413` gates `PATCH /applications/{uuid}/scheduled-tasks/{task_uuid}` on **`api.ability:write` and nothing else** — no narrower tier — and the shared `updateTask()` helper allowlists `command`, validated only as `string` with no content constraint, while its `authorize('update', …)` is resource-ownership rather than field-level. **The trigger token's `[read, write, deploy]` scope therefore CAN rewrite the task's command.** So, before the fire, the orchestrator **must** read the task's stored `command` back **via the list route `GET /applications/{uuid}/scheduled-tasks`, selecting by `uuid` with EXACTLY-ONE-MATCH — zero or two-or-more both fail closed** — and compare it **byte-exact** to an expected literal in `$CONF_FILE` (root:`ci-migrate` `0640`, `ci-migrate`-unwritable, already fully-trusted box-resident input), **failing closed on mismatch — built in #802 as exit 10.** ⚠ **CORRECTED 2026-09-18: this AC originally named a single-task `GET …/scheduled-tasks/{uuid}`, which does not exist at v4.3.18 — inferred from the PATCH shape, never measured, and it exited 10 on the first real execution as `ci-migrate`.** ⚠ **This is the [C5](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) anchor put back under the evidence:** (C) moved the evidence-PRODUCING instruction somewhere the invoking credential can rewrite, and the read-back re-anchors it to a file that credential cannot touch. **Without it the credential that reads the evidence could rewrite what produces it — self-fulfilling, no root and no database access needed.** ⚠ **(5) Compare versions on a fixed width or numerically** — `max(version)` is a string max, every version is 3 digits wide today, and string and numeric order stop agreeing the moment a different width lands.
- ⚠ **Semantics that must survive, and one downgrade that is accepted:** the sha gate becomes **POST-HOC** rather than a precondition — acceptable **only because migrations are forward-only**, so a stale container can no-op or apply a prefix but never write something wrong, and the delivery assertion still runs **before** the `DEPLOY_ON_SUCCESS` branch so the app never goes live against an under-migrated database. **Re-grade this if a down-migration mechanism is ever introduced.**
- ⚠ **Exit-code semantics that must survive whichever option wins:** sha and delivery keep **distinct** exit codes (measured today: **3** for the sha branch, **6** for the delivery branch; 5 is a malformed expected-sha, 4 an absent one, 7 the concurrency lock), and **every unreadable case fails closed** — all four `could not read …` branches already do, and none may become a skip.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: none — the wedge exists now. Downstream: **blocks every Phase D fire**, and therefore item **45**'s delivery exercise and item **49**'s unattended-prompt measurement, both of which need a fire that reaches the task.

**52. The trigger token can rewrite the command of ANY scheduled task on EVERY application its TEAM owns — on this single-team install, the whole box. Amendment 7's read-back covers the migrator task only.** [DevOps + Sec joint-review; F/CTO ruled "book", 2026-09-18; NOT blocking Phase D]
- **Source.** [ADR-072](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) Amendment 7's (4d) measurement (DevOps, Coolify v4.3.18 source): `routes/api.php:413` gates `PATCH /applications/{uuid}/scheduled-tasks/{task_uuid}` on **`api.ability:write` and nothing narrower**, and the shared `updateTask()` helper allowlists `command` as a writable `string` with no content constraint; `authorize('update', …)` is **resource-ownership**, not field-level. The trigger token is scoped `[read, write, deploy]` ([Amendment 2](DECISIONS.md#adr-072)'s corrected [C4](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) premise). ⚠⚠ **CORRECTED 2026-09-18 — the reach is WIDER than "per-application", measured by DevOps from Coolify v4.3.18 source. There is no per-application scope anywhere in this chain; the only filter is TEAM.** (1) **Token → team, at the token itself and not at the route:** `bootstrap/helpers/api.php:21-32`'s `getTeamIdFromToken()` takes the team from the **Sanctum token's own `team_id`**, baked in at mint time, and every controller method calls it first (`ScheduledTasksController.php:462`). (2) **Team → application, by membership only:** `resolveApplication()` (`ScheduledTasksController.php:28-31`) calls `Application::ownedByCurrentTeamAPI($teamId)` (`Application.php:495-498`), whose **only** filter is `environment.project.team.id = $teamId` plus the caller-supplied `uuid`. **Any UUID belonging to any application in the token's team resolves.** `resolveService()` (`:33-36`) has the identical shape for services. ⚠ **So the reach is every Scheduled Task on every application AND service the token's team owns.** **On this single-operator install — one Coolify team — that is the whole box, not the Supabase-stack resource.** **Gap (a) below is therefore LIVE TODAY, not an empty set waiting to be filled.**
- **Why it is a residual and not a hole.** Amendment 7's mandatory pre-fire read-back compares the **migrator task's** `command` byte-exact to a `$CONF_FILE` literal and fails closed (#802, exit 10). ⚠ **Two gaps it does not close, and naming them is the point of this item:** **(a)** it reads **one task**, so a rewrite of any **other** scheduled task — on this or on any other application or service the token's team owns — is unobserved; **(b)** it reads **at fire time**, so a rewrite that is **reverted before the next read** leaves no trace at all. **A control that samples cannot detect a change that is undone between samples** — that is a property of sampling, not a defect in the read-back.
- **AC — either arm, not both.** **(1) A narrower token**, if one exists. ⚠ **Measured today and recorded so it is not re-derived: Coolify 4.3.18 offers NO execute-without-update ability tier** — the same `write` that reaches `POST …/execute` reaches `PATCH …/scheduled-tasks/{uuid}`, and there is no deploy-only, admin-only or structural-vs-operational split on that route. **If a later Coolify version introduces one, re-scoping the token is the better arm and this item should take it.** **(2) Otherwise a periodic audit** that reads **every** scheduled task's `command` across **every application and service the token can resolve** — not just the Supabase stack — and compares each to a **committed manifest**, going **RED by name**: naming the task that drifted, not merely that something did. ⚠ **The audit closes (a) and narrows (b) to its own interval, which must be stated as the detection bound rather than left implicit.** ⚠ **It must also carry Sec's ARMING CONDITION — and the reason has changed since Sec wrote it, which is worth recording.** Sec's ground was that *"an empty set with no watcher is where a future task addition silently enters"*; the measurement above shows **the set is not empty today**, so the watcher is needed **now** rather than only against future growth. **Its arming half still stands and is the more durable half: the audit must assert the task COUNT against the manifest as well as each command, so the FIRST ADDITIONAL TASK is noticed rather than silently unaudited.** A manifest-compare that only walks tasks it already knows about cannot see one that appears. ⚠⚠ **The manifest MUST list `fail-probe` (`hffv8um6zruwslmndqc5su2l`, command `sh -c 'echo probe; exit 3'`) as an EXPECTED task with its expected command, not omit it as throwaway debris (Sec, `sec-record-step5-fail-probe.md`, 2026-09-18).** The runbook names `fail-probe` as a **permanent** positive control (§6.5), so it is a real, intentional, ongoing entry on the box, not scratch state the manifest can legitimately not know about. If the manifest is ever written from the ADR/runbook's expected-set rather than from a live snapshot of the box and omits it, the audit's **very first run** RED-flags the positive control itself as drift — a false positive on a control whose entire value depends on a RED meaning something. This is also the **narrowest live instance of gap (a)** above: gap (a) was already recorded as LIVE by the team-scope correction, but until `fail-probe` existed there was no second task on the migrator's own application to illustrate it concretely. ⚠ **(3) One live-box read-back this source measurement cannot settle:** whether a **second** Coolify team exists on this box — which would bound the blast radius back down — is not answerable from source. `GET /api/v1/teams`, **scoped to what the trigger token itself can see**, before treating the single-team reading as final. ⚠⚠ **AND IT CARRIES A CREDENTIAL-HANDLING CONSTRAINT, written into the AC now rather than discovered by whoever runs it — this surface has already had one incident** (the token file being `source`d and the value echoed, which is why `migrator-orchestrate.sh` now reads it line-by-line with `read_kv` and never `source`s it). **The obvious way to run this check is the unsafe one:** a bearer header passed as an argv-visible `-H` puts the production token in **`ps` output for any local user for the life of the call**, and in shell history. **Three constraints, all matching patterns this repo already uses:** **(i)** read the token by name from `/etc/pfin/migrator-coolify-token.env` using the orchestrator's own `read_kv` pattern — **never `source`, never an env dump**; **(ii)** hand it to `curl` via a **header file, `--config` file (mode `0600`), or stdin — never an argv-visible `-H`**; **(iii)** record **only the result — team count and ids — never the token and never the raw response.**
- ⚠ **Fold from Sec's #802 pin, carried here because it frames every assertion in this family:** item **51**'s AC is the live implementation contract and #802 builds it, **but the assertions have still never executed on the box as `ci-migrate` — the first real fire is the proof, and Sec wants that outcome MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED.** ⚠ **This item's own control inherits that condition**: an audit that has never run against the real token proves nothing about what that token can see.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: none. Downstream: none — ⚠ **explicitly NOT blocking Phase D** (F/CTO, 2026-09-18). The migrator path's own integrity is anchored by Amendment 7's read-back; this item is about the **rest of the tasks the token's TEAM owns** — on this install, every application and service on the box — and about **between-sample** rewrites.

**53. Which `api.ability:write` routes resolve through the team-filtered helper (`resolveApplication`/`resolveService`) and which take a uuid unfiltered — the trigger token's true write reach is unmeasured beyond scheduled-task and service resolution.** [DevOps + Sec; source read, no box access; NOT blocking Phase D; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** Sec, #803 re-pin ruling 2026-09-18 (`sec-803-b57a57db.md`): ADR-072 Amendment 2's C4 sentence now marks this as measured for scheduled tasks and services and NOT measured for the rest of the write-class route set; a "not measured, when next load-bearing" sentence is a conditional with no watcher. Booked as its own item rather than under item 52 (whose two arms are mutually exclusive and independent of this answer) or deferred to a C4 amendment (an amendment records a decision; this is an open measurement).
- **AC.** A source read over Coolify v4.3.18's `api.ability:write` route set (`routes/api.php`), one row per route: resolves through the team-filtered helper / takes a uuid unfiltered / other, with the line cited. Outcome either narrows item 52's stated reach or widens it beyond scheduled tasks (a route that resolves more narrowly, or one that does not filter by team at all, changes what the token can do — the unfavourable branch is a route reachable across teams). Result recorded as an annotation to C4 in ADR-072 and, if wider, a new item.
- **Trigger conditions that make it load-bearing (named so the item is not deferred silently):** any new caller of the trigger token; a Coolify upgrade; a second team created on the install; any new scheduled task or application on the box.
- **Dependencies.** None.

**54. `scripts/db-template-build.sh` applies `001`–`119` as `postgres`, but `119`'s guard needs a TRUE superuser or the comment already present — a fresh template build fails at `119` on any cluster where `postgres` is not superuser.** [DevOps; NOT blocking Phase D]
- **Source.** QA's `806` battery report, 2026-09-18 (⚠ **report md5 unverified** — the declared digest reproduces under no header-skip convention I tried: whole file, skip-1, skip-2. The report is left as-is; this line records that its integrity header did not verify, not that its content is wrong).
- **The mechanism, verified on the tree rather than relayed.** `scripts/db-template-build.sh:96` sets `SUPERUSER="${DB_TEMPLATE_SUPERUSER:-postgres}"`, and `psql_as()` (`:159`) runs the migration loop as that role. `119`'s `G4` guard branches on `rolsuper` for `current_user` (`119:194`) with a fallback that accepts the comment being **already present** (`shobj_description`, `119:212`). ⚠ **On the Supabase image `postgres` is NOT a superuser** — so on any such cluster a **fresh** `pfin_tmpl` build reaches `119` with neither condition satisfied and fails, unless a prior supervised pre-step already landed the role comment.
- ⚠ **Why CI is green and this is still real: CI's stack has a superuser `postgres`, so it takes the branch the other clusters cannot.** The script's own pre-step DOES run `roles.sql` and `auth-grants.sql` as `supabase_admin` — but **it runs none of the `055`/`116`/`117`/`118`/`119` supervised file-set** (measured: **zero** references to those files anywhere in the script). **The green is a property of CI's cluster, not of the script**, which is the shape that makes it survive review.
- **AC.** The template build runs **the same supervised file set as runbook §6.3 Phase 1, as `supabase_admin`, before the migration loop** — the same files, not a re-implementation, so the script and the runbook cannot drift. ⚠ **Strike-proof required, not a green run:** a **fresh** build on a cluster whose `postgres` is **not** superuser passes `119` **without any prior manual run**. A build that passes on CI's cluster proves nothing here — it is the case that already passes.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: none. Downstream: none. ⚠ **No cross-reference to item 45** — that item is the orchestrator delivery change and **does not name this script** (checked); linking them would imply a dependency neither has.

**55. ~15 pgTAP files report "No plan found in TAP output" (`pg_prove` exit 3) against a template clone — provenance unknown, and NOT caused by `120`.** [QA; NOT blocking]
- **Source.** QA's `806` battery report, 2026-09-18 (⚠ **report md5 unverified**, same note as item 54).
- **What is known.** Running the full `supabase/tests` tree against a template clone yields roughly fifteen files failing this way — `107`–`115` plus `self200`/`208`/`209`/`227`/`228`/`244`/`257`/`269`/`362` as QA reports them. ⚠ **They are IDENTICAL with and without migration `120`**, which is the measurement that matters for #806: **the comment-only migration did not cause them**, and #806 is not gated on this.
- ⚠ **What is NOT known, and the item exists because of it.** Whether these are a **local-harness artifact** — pgtap extension schema placement, the clone's empty `datacl`, per QA's earlier note — or **real failures the harness is merely the first to surface.** *"Identical before and after"* rules out `120` as the cause; **it does not rule out that they are real.** A count of failures whose provenance is unknown is not a finding yet, and treating it as either "known-noise" or "known-broken" would be a guess.
- **AC.** QA triages against **CI's own `supabase test db` run at the same sha** — the discriminator is a different harness on the same code, which separates the harness from the code. Each file is classified **harness-artifact** (with the mechanism named) or **real**; **every real one gets its own item** rather than being carried as part of a bulk figure. ⚠ **Do not close this item by re-measuring the same clone**: reproducing a number on the instrument under suspicion is not a triage.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: none. Downstream: none — and explicitly **not** a gate on #806, whose own battery is strike-proven in both directions independently of this.

**56. Premise inventory — every VENDOR SCHEMA constraint the trigger chain writes to or compares against has been reasoned about, not read. Three have already been falsified.** [Architect + Sec; DevOps measures; NOT blocking]
- **Source.** Sec's pattern finding across this chain, widened at [ADR-072](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) Amendment 8 (2026-09-18). ⚠ **Three vendor-shape premises have now failed at first contact**, each inferred from an adjacent shape rather than read: `ci-migrate`'s route to the Docker socket; a single-task `GET …/scheduled-tasks/{uuid}` that does not exist; and `scheduled_tasks.command` being `varchar(255)`. **Three is not luck — it is a method failing**, and the third is the one no amount of route-reading would have caught, because a column width is not visible in a route table.
- **AC.** Enumerate every field of Coolify's schema this chain **writes to or compares against** — `command` and the rest of `scheduled_tasks`, the execution record's `message` and `status`, and anything the orchestrator or the runbook sets — and record for each its **type, length and nullability**, read from the pinned vendor schema rather than inferred from a route, a UI form or an adjacent column. ⚠ **Measure them AS A SET, in one pass** — discovering them one sitting step at a time is the failure being closed. Each entry records **measured** with its source line, or **not measured** with the reason; **no third category**, since presumption is what failed here.
- ⚠ **WIDENED 2026-09-18 — a SECOND ARM, stated separately so the first arm's precision is not diluted: the same sweep covers every VENDOR ROUTE this project's OPERATOR-FACING DOCUMENTATION names.** Schema constraints and documented routes are different surfaces with the same failure class — a vendor shape written down from an adjacent shape instead of read — and the same instrument settles both: read the pinned vendor source. ⚠ **Two phantom routes have now been found by use rather than by reading**: the single-task `GET …/scheduled-tasks/{uuid}` in the read-back requirement, and `GET /api/v1/scheduled-tasks/{task-uuid}/executions` in the operator instruction (item **58**). **Same marking discipline as the first arm: measured with a source line, or not measured with a reason; no third category.**
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: none. Downstream: it will likely produce items. ⚠ **Not a gate on Phase D** — it is the control that stops the fourth premise being found by a production symptom. ⚠ **The counting is now: FOUR falsified vendor-shape premises** — the Docker socket route, the phantom single-task `GET`, the `varchar(255)` column width, and this documented executions route. **The fourth arrived while the item booked to prevent it was still unstarted.**

**57. Does Coolify's API PATCH on `scheduled-tasks` VALIDATE `max:255`, or rely on the database — i.e. does an over-length `command` REJECT or TRUNCATE?** [DevOps measures + Sec grades; belt-and-braces; NOT blocking]
- **Source.** [ADR-072](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) Amendment 8. The UI save rejected **at the database** with `SQLSTATE 22001`. **Nothing establishes what the API path does**, and the two possible answers have opposite safety.
- **Why it is not merely tidy.** ⚠ **If the API rejects**, an over-length write fails loudly and never reaches the store. ⚠⚠ **If any path TRUNCATES, a command is silently shortened — and what falls off the end of THIS command is its TAIL, which is exactly where `exit $rc` lives.** That is Amendment 8 (3)'s named prohibition arriving **by accident rather than by edit**: a task that saves "successfully", runs, and reports exit 0 on every failure. **AC (4d)'s read-back would catch it** — a truncated command cannot match the literal byte-exact — **which is the belt; this item is the braces, and it earns its place because the failure it guards against is silent at the moment it is introduced.**
- **AC.** Read the validation rule for `command` on the update path from the pinned vendor source (the `updateTask()` helper already cited in item 51) and record **reject** or **truncate** with its source line. If truncation is reachable by any path, book the consequence as its own item rather than folding it here.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: none. Downstream: none. Belongs to item **56**'s sweep and may be discharged by it.

**58. PR open at #814. The OPERATOR-facing instruction still says to read "the latest entry" of the executions list — the same selector that just cost a sitting step, on the human path rather than the machine one.** [DevOps; Sec-visible; diagnostic, NOT blocking]
- **Source.** Sec's pre-grade of the first real fire's exit 8, 2026-09-18, and [ADR-072](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) Amendment 7 §(E)'s binding requirement. ⚠ **The machine path is being fixed in DevOps's in-flight PR; this item exists because the HUMAN path has the identical defect and is not in that PR's scope.** `scripts/migrator-scheduled-task.md:70` tells an operator to poll the executions endpoint *"until the **latest entry**'s `status != running`"* — measured on the tree, not inferred.
- **Why it is worth an item rather than a footnote.** ⚠ **"The latest execution" is exactly the confusion that produced exit 8**, and an operator judging whether **their** fire worked has no `flock`, no pre-fire snapshot and no uuid to bind against — only position in a list. ⚠⚠ **And the trap is now ARMED for humans in the same way it is for the orchestrator: from the first real fire onward the newest prior row carries valid tags**, so an operator re-firing against the same image can read a stale row that looks entirely correct. **The stakes are lower than the gate's — a human misreading does not open the deploy — but the misreading is now more likely, not less.**
- **AC.** The operator instruction prints and keys on **`uuid` and `created_at` alongside `status` and the tag count**, so the reader can **bind the row themselves** rather than trusting its position; and the phrase *"the latest entry"* is replaced by a selection the operator can verify. ⚠ **Do not close this by fixing only the orchestrator** — that is the other path, and it is already someone else's PR. ⚠⚠ **AND THE SAME LINE CITES A ROUTE THAT DOES NOT EXIST — the fix pass must correct it in the same edit.** `scripts/migrator-scheduled-task.md:70` names `GET /api/v1/scheduled-tasks/{task-uuid}/executions`; **at v4.3.18 there is no top-level `/scheduled-tasks` route at all** (verified independently against the pinned routes copy: **zero** routes whose path begins `/scheduled-tasks`; only the `/applications/{uuid}/…` and `/services/{uuid}/…` families exist). It must read **`/applications/{app-uuid}/scheduled-tasks/{task-uuid}/executions`**. ⚠ **Why this is worse than an ordinary typo, and the reason it cannot wait for a tidy-up pass: the manual verification path is reached EXACTLY WHEN THE AUTOMATED ONE IS WEDGED** — and a 404 there reads to an operator as **"the task does not exist"**, which is a false and alarming diagnosis at the worst possible moment. **A broken instrument in the emergency drawer.**
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: none — it is a documentation change and blocks on nothing. Downstream: none. ⚠ **Explicitly not a gate on the binding PR**, which should not grow to carry it.
- ✅ **DISCHARGED at #814 (2026-09-18).** The binding PR grew to carry it after all — both halves of the AC landed in the same PR, not as a separate follow-up: `scripts/migrator-scheduled-task.md:70`'s poll step is rewritten to the uuid set-difference discipline (pre-fire snapshot, poll for the one new uuid, read status/message from that uuid only — stronger than merely printing `uuid`/`created_at` for the operator to eyeball, since it hands the operator the actual binding algorithm), and the phantom `/api/v1/scheduled-tasks/{task-uuid}/...` route is corrected to `/api/v1/applications/{app-uuid}/scheduled-tasks/{task-uuid}/...`, cited to `routes/api.php:415-416` with the controller methods named (`executions_by_application_uuid` / `execute_scheduled_task_by_application_uuid`), fetched and grepped directly, not inferred.

**59. ✅ DONE 2026-09-19 — BUILT (#831 `b95054aa`, Sec GREEN) and PROVEN on the box by run 35472610634 (name guard OK → migrator redeploy `yiislncnulilfbpafboemla6` → pre-execute commit OK → apply SUCCEEDED, build-sha match, ledger 120 = newest 120; `main` had advanced past the image and no hand redeploy was needed). Was: Orchestrator: deploy-then-execute against the migrator resource — `GET /deploy?uuid=<migrator resource>`, wait for terminal state, assert the running container carries the fired sha, THEN execute the task.** [DevOps; Sec joint-review; **IN FLIGHT, PR pending** — F/CTO said go 2026-09-19; NOT in the item-29 PR]

- **✅ BUILT, PR pending Sec joint-review (2026-09-19).** `scripts/migrator-orchestrate.sh` gained the deploy-then-execute section this AC describes, inserted after the pre-fire task-command integrity check and before the execution-binding snapshot. ⚠ **Two deviations from this AC's literal text, both forced by measurement, not by choice:**
  1. **The verb is `POST`, not `GET`** — measured against Coolify v4.3.18's `routes/api.php:144-145`: `GET /deploy` is bound to `OtherController::post_required`, which returns HTTP 405 unconditionally; only `POST /deploy` reaches the real handler. (This AC's own `GET /deploy?uuid=...` phrasing carries the SAME pre-existing defect already present at this script's own APP-deploy call site, `DEPLOY_ON_SUCCESS` branch — fixed at both call sites in this PR, not introduced by it.)
  2. **The sha assertion reads the Coolify deployment record's `commit` field via the API, not `/workspace/.build-sha` via `docker exec`.** This AC's literal text assumes a direct container read is available; it is not — [ADR-072](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) Amendments 7/8 (which post-date this AC's drafting) measured that `ci-migrate` has no route to `/var/run/docker.sock` at all (no group, no ACL, no sudo — C1 holding as designed), which is exactly why the EXISTING post-execution sha/delivery assertions already read the Scheduled Task's own self-reported stdout via the executions API instead of `docker exec`. This item's pre-execute assertion follows the same already-established evidentiary channel: `GET /deployments/<uuid>`'s own `commit` field (populated by Coolify's `git ls-remote`-and-save logic during the build, per `bootstrap/helpers/applications.php`), asserted equal to `MIGRATOR_EXPECT_SHA` before ever calling `.../execute`. Same evidence-independence tradeoff Amendment 7 already accepted for the post-execution checks (trusting Coolify's own record, not an out-of-band read) — not a new one introduced here.
- **Also measured (not guessed):** deployment terminal states are `finished` / `failed` / `cancelled-by-user` (Coolify v4.3.18's own `ApplicationDeploymentStatus` enum — `queued`/`in_progress` are non-terminal); `force` (rebuild without cache) is not needed for correctness — every deploy call with no pinned `git_commit_sha` on the application (true here, by construction) rebuilds the CURRENT branch HEAD via `git ls-remote`, unconditionally, on every call; **a `main` advance between trigger and deploy therefore REDs the new pre-execute commit assertion, correctly** — recorded as a known-RED class below, not a defect.
- **Known-RED class, with recovery:** if `main` advances between a fire's trigger and this new deploy call, the freshly-built image carries a NEWER commit than `MIGRATOR_EXPECT_SHA`, and the pre-execute assertion REDs by design (exit 18) before the Scheduled Task ever runs. Recovery: the newer commit's own fire is already queued behind this one under `migrator-trigger.yml`'s `concurrency` group (or fire again deliberately) — never relax the assertion to paper over this.
- **Ledgers flat.** No DDL, no §10 catalogued-instance change, no new `RT-NN` (this is an orchestrator ordering change over the CI-fenced set, the same class Amendment 6/7/8 already established is distinct from the §10 ledger).
- **Source.** [ADR-072](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) Amendment 6 consequence (i) — recommendation (i)'s mechanism: *"the orchestrator triggers a DEPLOY of the migrator RESOURCE, waits for the rebuild, ASSERTS THE RUNNING CONTAINER CARRIES THE TRIGGERING SHA, and only THEN executes the task."* Amendment 6 named this a hard precondition on [Amendment 4](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) ("Until the migrator has its own resource, the only resource that can be redeployed is the Supabase stack resource, and redeploying that restarts the database... This is why Amendment 4 is promoted below from hygiene to PRECONDITION"). Item 29 (this PR) discharges that precondition — `migrator` now has its own resource, so a deploy-then-execute orchestrator no longer bounces production Postgres on every migration merge. **This item is the consequence itself, not the precondition — it is NOT built by item 29 and does not ride this PR.**
- **AC.** `scripts/migrator-orchestrate.sh` gains a deploy step before the existing execute step: `GET /deploy?uuid=<MIGRATOR_SERVICE_UUID>` (the token's existing `deploy` ability, [ADR-072](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) Amendment 2 — no new scope needed), poll to a terminal state (never a bare "finished" — [Decision 3](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) already ruled Coolify's deployment status unreliable as a SUCCESS signal; this poll is a precondition-wait only, mirroring Amendment 6 consequence 3's "a deployment-status poll, if kept, is a precondition, never a success criterion"), then re-assert `/workspace/.build-sha` on the freshly-deployed container equals `MIGRATOR_EXPECT_SHA` BEFORE calling `.../execute` — the existing sha assertion moves from "precondition on an already-current image" to "precondition on an image THIS FIRE JUST BUILT." Sec joint-review mandatory (this touches the production deploy mechanism and the fail-closed gate, same surface class as Amendments 6/7/8).
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: item 29 (this PR) — the migrator resource must exist and be independently redeployable before this is safe to build; building it against the OLD sibling-service topology would bounce Postgres on every merge, exactly the defect Amendment 6 named. Downstream: none. **NOT blocking Phase D** — the current (execute-only, no deploy-then-execute) orchestrator remains correct and safe to keep firing against the new resource; this item is a hardening, not a fix to a live defect.

**60. `scripts/provision-migrator-app.sh` copies two pre-existing hygiene gaps verbatim from `scripts/provision-supabase-stack.sh` — fix both scripts together so the copies don't drift.** [DevOps; Sec explicit non-objection for the item-29 PR; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** Sec's joint review of PR #819 (item 29), non-objections list, 2026-09-18: `provision-migrator-app.sh:117`/`:119` puts the Coolify token in the remote curl's argv (`ps`-visible on the box) while the inner Python `api()` at `:283-284` correctly uses `-K -` stdin config; `:192` writes `/tmp/migrator-app-diff-check.$$` rather than `mktemp`. Both are copied verbatim from `provision-supabase-stack.sh:241`/`:243` and `:308` — pre-existing conventions in the script this PR's new sibling script was modeled on, not a regression introduced by item 29. Sec's own words: *"Pre-existing convention, not a regression. I do NOT require either fixed in this PR — fixing one copy creates drift. Book one item against both scripts."*
- **AC.** In `provision-supabase-stack.sh` AND `provision-migrator-app.sh`, together, in the same PR: (1) route the outer bash-level Coolify API token read through a `curl --config`/stdin form (matching the inner Python `api()` helper's own already-correct pattern in both files) rather than an argv-visible `-H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN"`; (2) replace every `/tmp/<name>.$$` scratch-file path with `mktemp` (or `mktemp -d` for a directory), matching this repo's own `AWK_SCRIPT="$(mktemp)"` convention already used elsewhere in both scripts. Fix both scripts in the SAME pass so neither is left as the stale copy. **Owner: DevOps.**
- **Dependencies.** None. Independent of items 59 and the §6.8 CUTOVER PROCEDURE — this is a hygiene fix on the provisioning scripts themselves, not a behavior change to what they provision.
- **Widened at #827:** ~4 more box-side shell `api()` calls on the token-in-argv half; `$$` temp-file occurrences 5→13 (migrator) / 5→14 (stack); new files hold only the predicate's OK/FAIL line, no secret; class maximum unchanged.

**61. Promote `fence-datastore-bind` (RT-32), `fence-migrator-bind`, and `fence-pgrst-schemas-pfin` to required GitHub branch-protection status contexts — all three are currently advisory.** [F/CTO RULED 2026-09-18 on the first two: **leave booked, LOW priority** — not required by any ADR or ratified condition (Sec: C9 holds advisory→advisory); F/CTO reviews every PR and team-lead checks the full CI rollup before merge, so this guards only an unwatched merge of a red PR; two-minute change whenever convenient; branch-protection change; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** Sec's joint review of PR #819 (item 29), addendum, 2026-09-18 — measured live via `gh api repos/:owner/:repo/branches/main/protection`: only `fence-admission-bind` (RT-27) appears in the required-status-checks list. `fence-datastore-bind` — the fence keeping production Postgres/its pooler/its gateway off the public internet (RT-32) — is **not** required, and neither is `fence-migrator-bind`. **Widened by Sec's joint review of PR #822, 2026-09-19:** measured at `d2f64e9b`, **25 jobs exist in `security-scan.yml`, 12 are listed in `.github/required-contexts.tsv`** — `fence-pgrst-schemas-pfin` (BACKLOG §7.36 item 22's CI-lane fence) is a **third** instance of the same gap. Not a regression introduced by PR #822 — the gap is pre-existing and structural — but every new fence lands into it silently unless this item's AC is kept current against the file list that exists when a reviewer reads it, which is exactly the failure item 25's own AC was widened to prevent. A PR reverting `PGRST_DB_SCHEMAS` to `public,graphql_public` would turn this job RED and merge anyway.
- **AC.** F/CTO decides whether to add `fence-datastore-bind`, `fence-migrator-bind`, **and `fence-pgrst-schemas-pfin`** (job name + context, per `.github/required-contexts.tsv`'s own 3-field format) to `main`'s branch-protection required-status-checks list, via the GitHub UI or API. If ratified: add all three rows to `.github/required-contexts.tsv` in the same PR that flips the branch-protection setting (that file's own header already treats "listed" as "must not be skippable," so listing without the branch-protection flip would be a false claim — order matters, protection first or in the same PR). **Owner: F/CTO decides; DevOps executes the branch-protection change and the manifest edit together once ratified.**
- **Dependencies.** None. Independent of item 29's or item 22's own merge — each new fence ships unlabeled and un-required either way; this item is about whether the *class* of fence-not-required gap should be closed for all three (and whatever comes next), a standing gap successive PR reviews keep surfacing rather than introducing.

**62. A job-level watcher for `.github/required-contexts.tsv` drift — the existing `required-unfiltered` fence is workflow-level and cannot see an unrequired job inside a listed, unfiltered workflow.** [**WON'T-DO — F/CTO ruling 2026-09-18**: overkill for a one-person project where every merge is reviewed; reopen only if item 61 is executed and enforcement of the required-list is wanted. Entry kept as the record of the gap Sec measured; not to be built.]
- **Source.** Sec's joint review of PR #819 (item 29), addendum, 2026-09-18. `required-unfiltered` (security-scan.yml) asserts that every WORKFLOW listed in `required-contexts.tsv` cannot be skipped (no `paths:` filter, etc.) — a one-way check: listed → unfiltered. It says nothing about the reverse direction: a workflow can be listed and unfiltered while carrying a job that is NOT in the required-contexts list at all (exactly item 61's finding, for `security-scan.yml` itself — `fence-datastore-bind` and `fence-migrator-bind` are both jobs inside a listed, unfiltered workflow, and neither is required). `required-contexts.tsv`'s own derive-command (cited in its header) operates at workflow granularity, so this gap is invisible to every existing check in the repo.
- **AC.** A new CI check (or an extension of `required-unfiltered`) that enumerates every `jobs:` key in `.github/workflows/security-scan.yml` (and any other workflow named in `required-contexts.tsv`) and asserts each job ID either appears in `required-contexts.tsv` OR is on an explicit, committed allowlist of jobs that are deliberately advisory (with a one-line reason each — e.g. a job that is genuinely optional, not merely forgotten). Fails closed (new job, no list entry, no allowlist reason = RED) so a future fence added to `security-scan.yml` cannot silently ship un-required the way `fence-datastore-bind`/`fence-migrator-bind` did. **Owner: DevOps.**
- **Dependencies.** None. Complements item 61 (which fixes today's two known gaps) by making the NEXT one visible instead of requiring another Sec review to find it by hand.

**63. `graphql_public` — once `pfin` joins `PGRST_DB_SCHEMAS`, does `pg_graphql` reflect `pfin` relations into the GraphQL schema, and under which role?** [DevOps measures, returns to Sec; NOT a step-7 blocker; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** Sec's review of item 22's ruling paper (`docs/records/v1final/standup-preconditions.md` §I.1). `graphql_public` is already in production's *current* value (`public,graphql_public`) — item 22's flip does not add this exposure, it is pre-existing — but item 22's flip is the first time `pfin` sits alongside it in the same exposed set. The Coolify gateway already routes `/graphql/v1` to `pg_graphql`'s resolver with `Accept-Profile: graphql_public` (`infra/supabase/volumes/api/envoy/lds.template.yaml:391-405`); `grep -rn 'pg_graphql\|graphql_public' supabase/migrations/ infra/supabase/volumes/db/` returns zero — the schema arrives from the `supabase/postgres` base image, not this repo's migrations, so no committed migration answers this question.
- **AC.** On a scratch instance carrying the ruled `PGRST_DB_SCHEMAS=public,graphql_public,pfin`, issue an introspection query at `/graphql/v1` as `anon` and separately as `authenticated`, and enumerate whether any `pfin` type appears in either response. If `pfin` types are reflected, this is a **second, unreviewed route to the same rows** — return to Sec for a grading pass (does RLS/`anon` zero-grant still hold through the GraphQL resolver, or does `pg_graphql` have its own privilege-resolution path worth checking separately). If nothing is reflected, record the negative result and close. **Owner: DevOps, returns to Sec.**
- **Dependencies.** Independent of item 22's flip — does not block it, per Sec's explicit note that this is a flag, not a veto.

**64. ✅ DONE (stage A execution, 2026-09-20, PRs #833 #835 #836 #837 #838 #839 #840 #841) — `production-standup.md` step 10's smoke checklist has no explicit criterion for the PGRST_DB_SCHEMAS-pfin defect class item 22 found — "a migration-backed query" is too vague to have caught it.** [DevOps; fed step 7/step 10]

Wired as `docs/deployment-runbook.md` §7.1 step 1(vii). Folded into `production-standup.md` step 10's own checklist text (this PR) — both halves of this item's AC are now closed.

**65. Wire branded GoTrue email templates (all five `GOTRUE_MAILER_TEMPLATES_*` vars) once the §2/§9 DNS cutover gives `app` its public domain — item 24's Option B, booked not built.** [DevOps + Sec (HTTPS-only, send-test-in-smoke-checklist conditions); tied to the same BACKLOG §7.36 allowlist milestone as Q5; no milestone yet]

Item 24's own PR landed the interim `config.toml` comment flagging this gap.

**68. Migrate `etl` / `pdf-render` / `provider-sync` to the same `dockercompose` + `external:` network attachment pattern `pfin-app` adopts at PR #833 — F/CTO-ruled fleet convention, 2026-09-19.** [DevOps; no milestone yet]

The three worker containers (`etl`, `pdf-render`, `provider-sync`) are ALL still specified in `docs/deployment-runbook.md` §7.1 as Compose-pack resources already (per §3's topology table, unaffected by this PR), but their OWN cross-application network reachability to `pfin-supabase-stack` (`etl`/`provider-sync` both need direct-Postgres reach to `db`; `provider-sync` additionally needs the `app`↔`provider-sync` SELF-212 admission handshake) has never been measured against the SAME project/environment-mismatch class this PR found for `app` — the same defect could be silently present on any or all three.

- **Siblings missing the cross-resource/per-lookup uniqueness assertion PR #840 added.** `scripts/provision-supabase-stack.sh:291` (`m=[a for a in d if a['name']=='$APP_NAME']` then `m[0] if m else ''`) and `record-coolify-uuids.sh`'s equivalent lookups pick the first match silently on >1 result — never die on ambiguity the way `push-production-secrets.sh` and `provision-app.sh` now do. **AC:** extend the same die-on-ambiguous-match guard to every sibling `/applications`-by-name lookup in this repo.
- **`fence-deploy-app-strikes.sh` suffix-match item (from #836's review; not yet independently re-verified this session — recorded as team-lead named it).** A container-name match in that fence's fixture used substring/suffix matching where an exact match would be tighter; flagged during PR #836's review, not fixed there.

- **`-fsS`-swallows-body observation (from #837).** `curl -fsS`'s `-f` flag discards the response body on a non-2xx status before any of this repo's scripts can inspect it for a more specific error — noted during #837's review as a general hardening opportunity across the `api()` helpers, not scoped to one script.

- **`secrets-manifest.yml` line 150 — doubled "each."** PR #840's own correction to the "zero PLAID_* references" claim reads "now each carry one commented line each" — grammatically redundant (repeats "each"). Cosmetic; a `secrets-manifest.yml` edit is Sec-joint-review-mandatory regardless of size, so bundled here rather than opened as a standalone PR for one word.

- **Dependencies.** None between these eight — genuinely independent small items, batched into one entry only to avoid eight one-line BACKLOG rows.

**70. `fence-provision-worker-strikes.sh`'s unknown-resource-name scenario fences REMOVAL of a `case` arm, not ADDITION to the allowlist.** [DevOps; Sec F-5, PR #844 (item 68 / W-1) review, 2026-09-20; no milestone yet]

Sec added a FOURTH accepted name to the `case` allowlist (`pfin-bogus-not-a-worker`) as an inversion and the fence stayed GREEN — a widened allowlist is invisible to a probe that only checks one still-refused name. A new `case` arm is visible in code review, so the residual is graded small, not a merge condition.

**71. `coolify-env.sh`'s `authenticator` allowance for `pfin-provider-sync`'s `PFIN_DB_USER` is transitional and has no written expiry; and Sec's pre-probe prevention idea for the V-1-class trust-path hazard is unbooked.** [DevOps; Sec F-8 residual + V-1 residual, PR #846 review, 2026-09-20; no milestone yet]
- **Source (expiry half).** Sec F-8 (PR #846 second review, `security-pr846-reconfirm-f22e1e4d.md`): `coolify-env.sh`'s per-resource `PFIN_DB_USER` gate correctly accepts `authenticator` on `pfin-provider-sync` — required, since the runbook §7.2 table and `secrets-manifest.yml`'s `PFIN_DB_PASSWORD` entry both document it as provider-sync's real PRE-§6.2-cutover value — but that acceptance is only meant to hold UNTIL the §6.2 cutover runs (after which `pfin_provider_sync` is the sole correct value). Nothing on the tree records that expiry; left alone it silently becomes permanent, and the gate would keep accepting a value that should no longer be reachable.
- **Source (pre-probe half).** Sec's V-1 residual, same review: the connect-as-role missing-password-prompt guard (`db-role-handoff.sh:465`, PR #846) is **post-disclosure detection, not prevention** — in the case it catches, the cleartext credential has already been sent as a SQL statement and written to the server log via `log_min_error_statement` before the guard can observe the missing prompt. Sec's own words: "if you want prevention rather than detection, the cheap shape is a pre-probe: one throwaway connection carrying a deliberately wrong password, observe whether a prompt is issued, and only then send the real credential." Sec graded this "worth booking, not worth blocking" — no merge condition, PR #846 ships without it.
- **AC (expiry half).** Add a written expiry condition to `coolify-env.sh`'s `authenticator` allowance for `pfin-provider-sync` (same shape as the ADR-072 Step 1.6 exception-window precedent Sec cited) — e.g. a dated comment plus a BACKLOG/runbook cross-reference to remove `authenticator` from the accepted set once `docs/deployment-runbook.md` §6.2's cutover is confirmed run, and/or a CI fence that flags (not necessarily blocks) continued use of `authenticator` past a recorded cutover date.
- **AC (pre-probe half).** Design and implement a throwaway pre-probe connection in `db-role-handoff.sh`'s step C: attempt a connection with a deliberately WRONG credential first, confirm a password prompt IS observed (proving the connection path is password-authenticated, not trust), THEN and only then send the real generated credential. Replaces detection-after-disclosure with prevention-before-disclosure for the exact V-1 hazard class. Needs its own golden-test fixture (a fake psql that accepts a wrong-password probe silently, simulating an undetected trust path) proving the pre-probe itself fails closed.
- **Dependencies.** Pre-probe half depends on nothing merged elsewhere; can land independently of the expiry half. Expiry half is blocked on docs/deployment-runbook.md §6.2's cutover actually running (no earlier trigger makes sense).

**72. ✅ DONE-PENDING-MERGE 2026-09-20 (BACKLOG.md §7.36 item 68's W-5, branch `feat/provision-orchestrator`, PR #849). `scripts/assign-app-domain.sh --apply` built: reads `PORKBUN_API_KEY`/`PORKBUN_SECRET_KEY` from `.env`, snapshots the full record set read-only and refuses to touch anything but the apex A / `www` CNAME (a wrong-type-at-target refusal, strike-proven), sets both via Porkbun's API, PATCHes the Coolify `pfin-app` resource's `fqdn` field (flagged UNMEASURED in the script's own header — no prior script ever wrote this field; the preflight prints the exact PATCH body and only `--apply`'s own read-back turns it into a measured fact), polls for the LE cert bounded, and confirms `www` also serves. Offline strike-proof: `scripts/ci/fence-assign-app-domain-strikes.sh` (9 scenarios incl. MX/TXT-refusal, wrong-record-refusal, uuid-ambiguity, keys-never-in-argv, PATCH-readback-mismatch). Was: DNS/domain assignment (registrar A/AAAA records, TTL, propagation check, and the Coolify Domain assignment on `pfin-app`) is entirely BY-HAND — no `assign-app-domain.sh` exists, even though Porkbun (the confirmed current registrar) has a documented DNS API.** [DevOps; booked at the W-4 runbook re-shape (PR #847), revised 2026-09-20 per team-lead's grading pass; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** `docs/deployment-runbook.md`'s original §2 (archived verbatim) described the DNS-registrar step as a human console action — at the time this item was first booked, `scripts/provision.env.example` had zero references to a registrar credential, so "no script reads it" was true, not a scripting oversight description alone. Team-lead's revision: that absence is itself the defect — Porkbun exposes a DNS API (apikey + secretapikey auth) and nothing here has ever tried to use it.
- **AC.** A `scripts/assign-app-domain.sh --apply` (folds into `scripts/provision.sh`, item 76) that: (1) reads `PORKBUN_API_KEY`/`PORKBUN_SECRET_KEY` from `.env` (added to `scripts/provision.env.example` in PR #847, not yet consumed by any script until this one lands), (2) snapshots existing A/AAAA/MX/TXT records read-only, (3) sets A/AAAA at the apex + `www` alias via Porkbun's API and lowers TTL ahead of cutover, (4) runs the propagation check against ≥2 independent public resolvers, (5) assigns the Domain on the `pfin-app` Coolify resource, and (6) waits for the Let's Encrypt cert to issue and confirms an HTTPS `200` on the assigned domain before returning.
- **Dependencies.** None blocking; can land independently.

**73. 🟡 PARTIALLY DONE, STAYS OPEN (Sec F-7, PR #849 review — do not mark this item DONE; the fire-and-poll follow-up below is unbuilt, not a formality). BACKLOG.md §7.36 item 68's W-5, branch `feat/provision-orchestrator`, PR #849. `scripts/github-ci-setup.sh --apply` built and landed: sets `CI_MIGRATE_SSH_PRIVATE_KEY` via `gh secret set NAME < file` (value never touches argv), sets `PROD_SSH_HOST` via `gh variable set` with an immediate read-back, and creates/confirms the `production-migrator` Environment's required-reviewer rule via `gh api`, reading the reviewer's identity LIVE from `gh api user` (never hardcoded) — GET-then-merges the existing reviewers list before the PUT (Sec F-4b, PR #849 review) so a pre-existing co-reviewer survives rather than being silently dropped by a naive single-element PUT, and refuses to report success unless the post-PUT read-back actually shows both the current user AND every pre-existing reviewer still present. Offline strike-proof: `scripts/ci/fence-github-ci-setup-strikes.sh` (8 scenarios incl. not-authenticated/private-key-absent preconditions, variable/environment readback-mismatch refusals, the co-reviewer-survival assertion). DEFERRED, NOT DONE: per this item's own original scope the per-fire approval click was to stay BY-HAND — that boundary itself is now under review (team-lead, mid-session): dispatching the migrator fire from inside `provision.sh` and printing/polling the exact review URL, rather than pointing the operator at the Actions tab, was scoped for W-5 and deliberately NOT built (a live production migrator trigger is a one-way-door-shaped risk not worth building without a real box to verify against). `docs/deployment-runbook.md` Part 3 and `provision.sh`'s own `run_github_ci()` comment both state this deferral. Was: GitHub CI-side setup for the migrator trigger (Actions secret, Actions variable, the `production-migrator` Environment + required reviewer) is entirely BY-HAND — no `github-ci-setup.sh` exists.** [DevOps; booked at the W-4 runbook re-shape (PR #847), 2026-09-20; no milestone yet]

Needs a live box + a real Actions run to verify the polling shape against — cannot be built and proven correct offline. Confirm the exact `gh api` endpoint and polling cadence with Sec before building (a live production migration trigger is the one-way-door concern).
- **Source.** `docs/deployment-runbook.md`'s original §6.4 (archived verbatim) names three F/CTO `!`-steps done by hand in the GitHub UI: adding `CI_MIGRATE_SSH_PRIVATE_KEY` as an Actions secret, `PROD_SSH_HOST` as an Actions repository variable, and creating the `production-migrator` Environment with F/CTO as a required reviewer. The `gh` CLI can perform all three (`gh secret set`, `gh variable set`, `gh api .../environments/...`) but nothing in this repo wraps them.
- **AC.** A `scripts/github-ci-setup.sh --apply` that sets the Actions secret (value piped via stdin, never argv) and variable via `gh`, and creates/confirms the `production-migrator` Environment with the required-reviewer rule via the GitHub API — preflight-by-default, `--apply` to execute, matching every other script in this repo's convention. **Explicitly out of scope for this script:** the per-fire required-reviewer APPROVAL click itself (item 73 books only the one-time Environment setup, not the recurring approval gate, which stays BY-HAND by Sec's own ruling on that control).
- **Dependencies.** None. Needs `gh` authenticated with repo-admin scope to run.

**75. ✅ DONE-PENDING-MERGE 2026-09-20 (branch `feat/provision-orchestrator`, PR #849, folded in alongside item 76). 🔒 `scripts/db-bootstrap.sh` built: the full three-phase §6.3 procedure (roles.sql → auth-grants.sql → engine-backstop `REVOKE`s → the migrator credential handoff, reusing PR #846's piped-stdin `\password` mechanism → role-comment files → `supabase db push` in the migrator container → `post-step-vault-view.sql`), running AS `supabase_admin` (a new privileged-automation surface — Sec joint-review mandatory), structurally refusing to run outside an operator SSH session (establishes its own SSH connection; a Scheduled Task has no code path to invoke it). Building this fence caught and fixed a real defect in the script itself: two remote-script `OUT="$(cmd 2>&1)"` assignments were missing the `set +e`/`set -e` bracket `db-role-handoff.sh`'s own identical mechanism uses, so a genuine command failure silently killed the script at the assignment before its own FATAL diagnostic (and the captured error text) could print. Was: `docs/deployment-runbook.md`'s §6.3 database bootstrap (`roles.sql`, `auth-grants.sql`, the engine-backstop `REVOKE`s, `\password migrator` + `ALTER ROLE migrator LOGIN`, the role-comment files) is entirely BY-HAND, on a premise PR #846 already falsified.** [DevOps; booked at team-lead's grading pass on PR #847, 2026-09-20; no milestone yet]

There is no reason the same mechanism can't drive `\password migrator` (run as `supabase_admin` instead of `postgres`/superuser-equivalent) the same way. **This does not make SELF-395 itself false or unnecessary** — SELF-395 additionally covers client-side SCRAM verifier computation, a different (arguably stronger) mechanism; this item just stops waiting on it for something PR #846 already proved doesn't need it. **Scope of the falsification, stated precisely (Sec, PR #847 review F-2):** #846 retires the interactive-terminal premise only for an **OPERATOR-RUN script executed over SSH** — it says nothing about, and does **not** touch, [ADR-072](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) Decision 6's separate Scheduled-Task clause ("a Scheduled-Task-triggered apply cannot perform it"). The `migrator` Scheduled Task must never own this bootstrap; `db-bootstrap.sh` (this item's AC) is an operator-invoked script, not a Scheduled-Task body. Not editing SELF-395's own Linear record or [ADR-072](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) Decision 6 text — that's Architect/F/CTO territory (see item 78, which books that amendment); this item is the BACKLOG-side booking only.

Sec joint-review mandatory (DB-role + credential-handling surface).

**78. [ADR-072](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) Decision 6 ("Bootstrap stays supervised until SELF-395; steady-state is E's whole point") now reads as broader than it should — item 75 shows PR #846 already falsified its interactive-terminal premise for the operator-run half of the bootstrap.** [Architect; Sec F-2 on PR #847 review, 2026-09-20; no milestone yet]

PR #846's `scripts/db-role-handoff.sh` falsifies claim (1) for an operator-run script over SSH (piped-stdin `\password`, non-interactively, no cleartext logged, prompt-issuance asserted) — it does **not** touch claim (2), which stands: the `migrator` Scheduled Task must never own this bootstrap regardless. Left as written, Decision 6 reads as still requiring a human at a terminal for something item 75's `db-bootstrap.sh` will do non-interactively, which is no longer accurate.
- **AC.** Architect amends [ADR-072](DECISIONS.md#adr-072) Decision 6 to narrow it to the Scheduled-Task clause only (claim 2, unchanged and still binding), and records PR #846's falsification of the interactive-terminal premise (claim 1) as the reason claim 1 was dropped rather than silently disappearing from the text. **No ADR edit lands in PR #847** — that PR only books this item and states the same distinction inline (item 75's Source, and `docs/deployment-runbook.md`'s step 3 reason cell) without touching the ADR itself.

**79. ✅ DONE-PENDING-MERGE 2026-09-20 (branch `feat/provision-orchestrator`, PR #849, folded in alongside item 76). `scripts/smoke-ca1-env-pattern.sh` built per the corrected AC below: reads the container's own injected env names, cross-checks them live against the container's OWN compiled `PUBLIC_ROUTE_ENV_MATCHERS` (never hand-copied), fails on Subject A's inversion (an injected route-signal name matched by nothing), does not fail on Subject B's benign zero-match. The route-signal reference set is PINNED (stated in the script's own header), not independently live-measured against a real Coolify install — none exists in this repo to measure against. Strike-proof: `scripts/ci/fence-smoke-ca1-env-pattern-strikes.sh` (9 scenarios, including the inversion itself). Was: CA-1's deploy-gate half (`workers/provider-sync/src/http/admissionGuard.ts`'s `PUBLIC_ROUTE_ENV_MATCHERS`: DevOps verifies the code's public-route env-name pattern set against the running Coolify version's ACTUALLY-INJECTED var names, at deploy) is entirely BY-HAND — no `smoke-ca1-env-pattern.sh` exists.** [DevOps; Sec F-1/F-3 carry-forward on PR #848 review, booked on PR #847's #848 fold, 2026-09-20; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** `admissionGuard.ts`'s own header names two halves of CA-1: the code half (pattern-match, not exact-name, so a Coolify env-var rename can't slip past it) lives in the worker; the deploy-gate half — confirming THIS Coolify version's actually-injected env-var names still match what the code's pattern set expects — is explicitly named as DevOps's job, and nothing in this repo does it. **This became a live gap, not a theoretical one, at PR #848**: that PR is the first time `provider-sync` actually deploys to a real Coolify resource in this sequence (Part 3 step 7/10 of `docs/deployment-runbook.md`), so CA-1's deploy-gate half now executes in production for the first time with nothing checking it.
- **AC.** A `scripts/smoke-ca1-env-pattern.sh` that: `docker exec`s into the running `provider-sync` container, reads `env | cut -d= -f1` (names only, never values — same discipline as every other env-reading script in this repo), and cross-checks the resulting name set against `admissionGuard.ts`'s own `PublicRouteMatcher` pattern set (read the patterns from the file, not hand-copied, so the two can't drift). **Corrected criterion (Sec, PR #847 review F-7 — the original wording, now replaced, described an INVERTED check):** the script reports, for each injected name, whether any `PublicRouteMatcher` matches it, and **fails the check when a name that IS a route signal on the running Coolify version is matched by NO pattern** — that inversion is the whole control; a name that matches a pattern is one the code already anticipated and would have refused to boot on. The route-signal reference set is the running Coolify version's own documented injected-var list, supplied as a pinned input to the script, not inferred from the name. Does not need to fail hard on a benign zero-match result (Subject B) — CA-2's empirical reachability smoke is the actual backstop for that case; it must fail hard on Subject A's inversion (an uncovered route-signal name).
- **Dependencies.** None blocking. Independent of CA-2 (`scripts/smoke-admission-endpoint.sh`, already built at PR #848) — this item checks the PATTERN-vs-reality gap CA-2's empirical smoke doesn't directly observe.

**80. ✅ CLOSED 2026-09-21 (branch `fix/worker-resources-clear-default-fqdn`, CA-1 run-8 stop; ruling by Sec, PR #862 review). `smoke-ca1-env-pattern.sh`'s pinned `ROUTE_SIGNAL_REFERENCE` list was DERIVED FROM the same matcher families it checks against, making Subject A structurally unfirable — team-lead's own live `docker exec ... env` read against a real `pfin-provider-sync` deploy (Coolify 4.3.18, dockercompose, no domain) confirms `COOLIFY_FQDN`/`COOLIFY_URL` genuinely ARE injected, so Subject A is firable against a real name on a real deploy. Sec's ruling: keep the curated list as-is — the original AC's "source from a full captured env-name fixture" is the WRONG shape for this control, not merely unimplemented.** [DevOps; Sec F-3 on PR #849 review, 2026-09-20; Sec ruling closing it, PR #862 review, 2026-09-21]
- **Source.** Sec, PR #849 review: `admissionGuard.ts`'s `PUBLIC_ROUTE_ENV_MATCHERS` defines exactly 5 matcher families; `smoke-ca1-env-pattern.sh`'s pinned reference list was 1:1 the same 5 names, each read off the matchers' own code comments. Every pinned name therefore matched by construction — the only reachable outcomes were "all matched" (exit 0) or "none injected" (exit 0, trivially clean); the gate could not emit the Subject A finding it was built to catch. `temp/self212-devops-ca1-coolify-route-envvars.md` remains absent, never committed (the original `temp/` loss).
- **What changed 2026-09-21.** team-lead's own measurement (CA-1 run-8 stop, relayed names-only per this repo's env-dump-leak discipline): on a real `pfin-provider-sync` dockercompose deploy with no domain assigned, Coolify 4.3.18 injects `COOLIFY_FQDN`, `COOLIFY_URL`, `COOLIFY_CONTAINER_NAME`, `COOLIFY_BRANCH`, `COOLIFY_RESOURCE_UUID` — and does NOT inject `SERVICE_FQDN_*`/`SERVICE_URL_*` on this shape. `smoke-ca1-env-pattern.sh`'s header now records this measurement in full; `ROUTE_SIGNAL_REFERENCE`'s own content is UNCHANGED (still the 5 curated names — `COOLIFY_FQDN`/`COOLIFY_URL` now confirmed real, `SERVICE_FQDN_APP`/`SERVICE_URL_APP` confirmed absent on this shape but kept for other resource shapes).
- **Ruling (Sec, PR #862 review, closing this item).** DevOps flagged an open design question — curated list vs. full-fixture-sourced-with-exclusions. Sec's ruling: keep the curated list. `COOLIFY_CONTAINER_NAME`/`BRANCH`/`RESOURCE_UUID` are not route signals; adding them would make the script assert a false thing and false-positive on a correct non-match. The full-fixture-plus-exclusion-list alternative INVERTS the artifact's direction — it turns a positive reference set ("these are the route-signal names we know of") into a denylist ("everything except these is a signal"), the wrong shape for a drift detector, needing maintenance against every future Coolify var. `tests/fixtures/ci/smoke-ca1-env-pattern/coolify-4.3.18.names` (committed already) stands as the evidence that nothing route-shaped was missed at 4.3.18 — no further fixture-sourcing work needed.
- **Dependencies.** None — closed.
- **Dependencies.** None further for the design ruling (a judgment call, not a live-deploy dependency); the fixture-capture half needs another live deploy read to do properly (names only), or can reuse this round's already-measured 5 names as a starting fixture.
