- **What shipped, and where it diverges from this item's own original AC (both deliberate, both stated rather than silent).** (1) TZ-1 — the query is never retyped: it is extracted LIVE from `docs/deployment-runbook.md` §4.1 by re-using `scripts/ci/check-tz-sweep-identical.py`'s own `extract_runbook()` as an imported module (a third hand-typed copy would be exactly the drift class that fence exists to prevent), then run against the box's `db` container as `supabase_admin`. (2) CA-7 — the original AC's "resolve/respond probe run from inside the app container" undersold the real archive §10 CA-7 definition (`docs/archive/deployment-runbook-rationale-2026-09-20.md`): it is a NEGATIVE control from the *operator's own machine* (outside the Coolify network entirely — a `nc`-based TCP-connect substitute for the archive's named `nmap`, whose presence on the operator's Mac is unmeasured; BSD netcat ships with macOS by default and its absence is checked as an explicit precondition) against 5432/6543/8000, plus a Coolify-Domain-assigned check, PLUS the positive control (from inside `pfin-app`, attached to the stack's Docker network) the original AC named. Built to the real archive definition, not the AC's compressed paraphrase. (3) RLS isolation — READ-ONLY throughout, never a pgTAP INSERT-then-rollback battery against production (considered and rejected — even a rolled-back write carries a different risk posture against a live production database than an ephemeral CI/local one): every `pfin.*` table carrying `users_id` is discovered live, checked for `relrowsecurity`/an attached policy/zero `anon` grant, and — the behavioral proof — a session with `SET ROLE authenticated` and NO `request.jwt.claims` set (so `auth.uid()` is NULL) must see ZERO rows on every one of them, regardless of how many real rows exist; `service_role.rolbypassrls` is confirmed structurally. Flagged for Sec design review as an ADR-011 surface, per this item's own dispatch. (4) auth login — reports SKIPPED before `pfin-app` carries a public domain (the `dns` step); once one exists, checks GET `/login` (200) and a malformed POST `/signup` missing `password` (400, `.strict()` validation fires, no real account created) and a Resend send-acceptance probe to `delivered@resend.dev` (Resend's own documented test address; a no-op, not a failure, while `SMTP_PASS` remains unconfigured per `infra/supabase/README.md`'s own stated posture) — then caps at MANUAL regardless, because the email-confirmation round-trip cannot be scripted. `provision.sh`'s registry moved `remaining-checks` to immediately before `cutover` (after `deploy-on-success`, so the auth-login leg can reach the domain `dns` assigns) — `STEP_REQUIRES` states this as a hard `dns` dependency, not just registry order. Offline strike-proof: `scripts/ci/fence-smoke-remaining-checks-strikes.sh`, 16 scenarios, one guard struck alone → exactly one RED each, wired into `.github/workflows/security-scan.yml`.
- **Dependencies.** None further — closed. Sec joint-review requested on the RLS-isolation leg's design (ADR-011 surface) before merge, per this item's own routing.

**82. `run_deploy_app()`/`run_deploy_workers()` always redeploy on a resume, even immediately after their own prior invocation already succeeded — no done-predicate short-circuits either step the way `live_done_pgrst_flip()`/`live_done_standup()` already do for their own steps.** [DevOps, Sec-consult mandatory (live container-env read); team-lead ruling on PR #856's item-5 deferral, 2026-09-21; AC amended per Sec's PR #856 round-1 note on the filter-before-ssh-hop discipline; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** team-lead's run-4 brief (`realrun4.log` step 15) asked whether Coolify exposes a source-commit label/env usable as a done-predicate. PR #856 measured: **yes** — `SOURCE_COMMIT` is available at runtime by default on any Coolify-built container, independent of `include_source_commit_in_build` (that setting only gates BUILD-time availability, per this repo's own prior measurement, `docs/archive/deployment-runbook-rationale-2026-09-20.md:401`). Deferred building the mechanism itself in PR #856 (a new live-container-read pattern on top of an already-large PR) — team-lead's ruling: "a redundant redeploy on resume costs minutes, not correctness," accepted as booked follow-up rather than blocking #856.
- **AC.** `run_deploy_app()`/`run_deploy_workers()` gain a `live_done_deploy_app()`/`live_done_deploy_workers()`-shaped preflight check (same memoized-done-predicate pattern already used for `pgrst-flip`/`standup`): read the running container's own `SOURCE_COMMIT` value by **filtering on the box, inside the remote command string** — `docker inspect --format '{{range .Config.Env}}...'` or `docker exec <ctr> env | grep '^SOURCE_COMMIT='` with the grep **before** the ssh hop, never after it (Sec, PR #856 round 1 — the same discipline `scripts/pgrst-schemas-live-check.sh` already follows, and the hazard `scripts/coolify-env.sh`'s `--post-check` comment names: an unfiltered container `env` carries `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` on `app` / `PGRST_DB_URI` and `PGRST_JWT_SECRET` on `rest` across the wire — `SOURCE_COMMIT` itself is a commit sha, not a secret; its neighbours in that env are). Compare the filtered value against the app's configured-branch tip (the same commit the next `--apply` would actually deploy), and short-circuit to VERIFIED-without-applying when they already match, the same shape `live_done_pgrst_flip()` uses for its own store-vs-container comparison. Offline strike-proof scenarios in `fence-provision-strikes.sh` for both the match (skip) and mismatch (fall through to a real deploy) cases, plus a probe-unreachable UNKNOWN-not-done case matching the existing `live_done_*` conventions. **Route back to Sec when built** — a done-predicate over live container state.
- **Dependencies.** None blocking. Independent of item 81 (`remaining-checks`) — different steps, same orchestrator file.

**83. `serve-admission.js` ignores SIGTERM — every stop/redeploy of `pfin-provider-sync` costs Coolify's full 30s stop-grace-period then a SIGKILL, instead of exiting promptly.** [Backend; team-lead's own live measurement during CA-1 PR #862 verification, 2026-09-21; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** team-lead, live measurement while redeploying `pfin-provider-sync` for PR #862's CA-1 fix verification: dockerd logs "Container failed to exit within 30s of signal 15 - using the force" on every stop. Cause, confirmed by grep: `workers/provider-sync`'s compose `command` runs `node dist/cli/serve-admission.js` in exec form, so node is PID 1 with no signal handler installed (zero `process.on` matches anywhere in `workers/provider-sync/src`) — the default Node behaviour for an HTTP server process is to keep the event loop alive through SIGTERM unless a handler explicitly closes it.
- **AC.** Add a SIGTERM/SIGINT handler in `serve-admission.js` (or its entrypoint) that: stops accepting new connections, **drains in-flight requests** (specifically: does not abort a request mid-flight that may have already resulted in a Plaid link-token being issued — a force-kill there can leave a token issued with no local record, a reconciliation gap, not just a graceful-shutdown nicety), then exits 0. Verify live: a redeploy/stop no longer logs Coolify's 30s-timeout-then-force message.
- **Dependencies.** None. Not part of PR #862 (CA-1 fix) — booked separately per team-lead's own instruction, since it's an orthogonal defect surfaced during that PR's verification, not caused by or required for it.

**84. No CI job detects a Debian-pinned package version falling out of the live apt repos before a deploy hits it — every pinned Dockerfile version can go stale silently between the pin's authoring date and the next build.** [DevOps; team-lead's own live measurement during CA-1/run-10 verification, 2026-09-21; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** team-lead, run 10 (`--from deploy-workers`, main `156e10c8`): Coolify's build of `pfin-pdf-render` FAILED — `E: Version '152.0.7977.75-1~deb12u1' for 'chromium' was not found` (apt exit 100) at `workers/pdf-render/Dockerfile`. That pin was measured correct at authoring time (2026-09-05); a Debian bookworm security update superseded it in the live apt repos sometime between then and the run-10 build, and nothing in this repo's CI would have caught the drift before a real deploy hit it — the pin's own header comment says "reproducible builds trade against staying current... bumping this pin is a routine maintenance task," but nothing schedules or triggers that maintenance. Bumped in the same PR that surfaced this (fix/pdf-render-chromium-pin-and-resume-clear) to `153.0.8010.52-1~deb12u1`, arm64-measured only (no CI job builds this image on any arch to cross-check).
- **AC.** A scheduled CI job (same `scheduled-dep-audit.yml` family as the existing npm/pip-audit liveness lane) that, for every `RUN apt-get install ... <pkg>=<pinned-version>` line across every tracked Dockerfile in the repo (grep-derived, not a hand-maintained list — a Dockerfile added later must be picked up automatically), runs `apt-cache policy <pkg>` against the SAME base image + Debian release the Dockerfile itself declares (via a throwaway container, not the runner's own OS) and compares the pinned version against the live Candidate. A mismatch is a FINDING (flag/open an issue), not a hard CI failure on unrelated PRs — this is a liveness/staleness detector, not a merge gate, since drift can occur with zero code changes to the Dockerfile itself and would otherwise redden every unrelated PR touching that file. Route the finding to DevOps (Dockerfile owner) per the existing per-Dockerfile ownership convention.
- **Dependencies.** None blocking. Independent of the chromium pin bump itself (already fixed live in the PR that found this gap) — this item is the detector that would have caught it earlier, not a fix for the specific pin.

**85. ✅ DONE 2026-09-21 (this PR). `fence-supabase-stack-healthy-check-strikes.sh`'s scenario 8 ("jwt-secret-unset") did not test what its own label and comment claimed — it duplicated scenario 11's already-covered case, not a distinct one.** [DevOps, measured; Sec ruled the remedy same-day; held from PR #852 round 3, remedy text not recovered, re-derived instead; 2026-09-21; no milestone yet]
- **Source.** DevOps measured the underlying defect directly (Sec's #852 round-3 commit-ready text was not recovered): scenario 8 passed `FAKE_JWT_SETTING="f"` — the IDENTICAL value scenario 11 ("probe5-guc-empty-string") used — while scenario 8's own comment claimed it modeled "current_setting('app.settings.jwt_secret', true) returns NULL for a genuinely unset GUC." Sec re-derived the finding independently from the tree (declining to reconstruct the round-3 text from memory) and reached a sharper conclusion than a simple relabel: a genuinely unset GUC makes the `<>` comparison evaluate to NULL, which `psql -At` renders as EMPTY output — observationally identical to scenario 9's own "psql died / empty output" case, not a distinct wire-level case at all.
- **Resolution (Sec's ruling, applied verbatim).** Scenario 11 dropped (exact duplicate of 8). Scenario 8 relabeled `PROBE-5-NON-CANONICAL-TOKEN` — what it actually tests: probe (5/5) alone fails because the GUC answers with a non-positive token, not because it's "unset". Scenario 9's own comment extended to state explicitly that it covers BOTH psql-dying AND a genuinely-unset GUC, since both render as empty output and are indistinguishable at this fake's interface by construction — collapsing them costs no coverage. Sec also checked the real script's own FATAL message ("app.settings.jwt_secret is unset, empty, or the db did not answer") and confirmed it already hedges disjunctively across all three causes, so no message-text fix was needed. Sec's "battery cardinality" lead (10 `run_case` calls numbered to 11) resolved as a side effect: now 10 calls numbered to 10.
- **Verification.** Fence green (13 scenarios, was 14), full self-contained strike-fence sweep clean.
